# AGENTS.md

## Project Shape

- Root app: React 18 / Vite frontend in `src/`.
- Backend: Node ESM Express API in `server/`, with custom RAG logic in `server/rag/`.
- Runtime/generated paths to avoid editing unless requested: `node_modules/`, `build/`, `server/node_modules/`, `server/data/`, `server/uploads/`, `server/upload-sessions/`, `server/evaluation/generated/`, and timestamped files under `server/evaluation/results/`.

## Setup

- Install frontend deps from the repo root: `npm install`.
- Install backend deps separately: `cd server && npm install`.
- The backend needs Node >= 22.13 (`server/package.json` engines; pdfjs-dist 6 uses `Promise.withResolvers`, missing on Node 20). CI runs Node 22, the image is `node:22-bookworm-slim`; local development has used Node 24.
- Copy `.env.example` for frontend settings and `server/.env.example` to `server/.env` for backend settings.
- Start the default retrieval backend with `docker compose up -d` from the repo root (PostgreSQL 16 with pgvector, healthcheck, persistent volume); `server/.env.example` already points `POSTGRES_DATABASE_URL` at it. Migrations run at startup and on health checks.
- Required backend keys for full RAG/web behavior: `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `SERPAPI_KEY`. A failed web search is never answered: `mcp-server.js` returns `isError` with a fixed reason per kind of failure (no `SERPAPI_KEY`: `Web search is not configured: SERPAPI_KEY is not set.`; an answer other than 200: `Error performing web search: SerpAPI answered with an error.`; no answer, a network error or a timeout: `Error performing web search: the request to SerpAPI failed.`; never SerpAPI's body or the network error's text, which go to its stderr, the server's log) and `chat-mcp.js` throws it once, unretried, as 409, so the approved `capability_call` step and the run fail and the approval answers `{ error: reason }` (409 because `serializeError`, `lib/error-text.js`, shows a 4xx's message and hides a 5xx's).
- One-click deployment: `docker compose --profile app up -d --build` builds the root `Dockerfile` (the API server also serves the built frontend through `FRONTEND_BUILD_DIRECTORY`, and the page calls the API on its own origin via `VITE_DOMAIN=same-origin`) and runs it next to PostgreSQL on http://localhost:5001; `--profile layout` adds docling-serve, `--profile shared-state` Redis, and `-f compose.rerank.yml --profile rerank` the cross-encoder reranker. See `docs/deployment.md`. The image installs server dependencies with `--legacy-peer-deps` because npm 10.9 insists on a TypeScript peer the npm 11 lock file leaves out; keep that flag or regenerate the lock with the image's npm.
- Compose network exposure: every published port (postgres 5432, redis 6379, app 5001, docling 5010, and `api` in `compose.services.yml`) binds `${ARCHIVE_RAG_BIND_HOST:-127.0.0.1}`, and `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (default `postgres`, URL-safe characters only, applied only when the `pgdata` volume is first initialized) feeds the postgres service and every compose database URL. To open it on a network, set `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`, enable API auth, then start with `ARCHIVE_RAG_BIND_HOST=0.0.0.0` (or one interface address); it widens every port at once and Redis has no password. The app healthcheck probes `/ready`. `test/deployment-contract.test.mjs` pins all of this.

## Development Commands

- Run frontend and backend together from the repo root: `npm run dev`.
- Run only the frontend: `npm start`.
- Run only the backend: `npm run server` from the root, or `cd server && npm run start`.
- Monolith shutdown (`server.js`, role `all`): the first SIGTERM/SIGINT runs the split roles' `createGracefulShutdown` (`rag/agent-service/role-server.js`). It stops listening, lets in-flight requests (a `/chat`, an SSE stream, an upload) finish within `SERVICE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_MS` (default 25 s), and stops the in-process ingest worker at the same time (`INGEST_WORKER_STOP_GRACE_MS`, 5 s, then running jobs go back to the queue). It then runs `app.locals.stop`, the span flush (`createTracingShutdownFinalizer` in `otel.js`; never a flush that exits on the signal, which would cut the drain), the metrics listener close and `resetPostgresPool`, and exits 0. A second signal exits 1 at once, and so does a shutdown still running after max(drain window, 5 s when a worker runs) + 3 s. The compose `app` service has `stop_grace_period: 30s` (pinned by `test/deployment-contract.test.mjs` against the default grace + 3 s); raise it together with `SERVICE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_MS`. The drain covers HTTP requests only: background agent tasks in the process are still cut and left to startup recovery. `test/monolith-graceful-shutdown.test.mjs` runs `node server.js` as a child process.
- Default ports are frontend `3000` and backend `5001`; the backend also honors `PORT`.
- Run one tier of a split deployment: `cd server && ARCHIVE_RAG_ROLE=<api|agent|retrieval|model-gateway> node server.js`. Ports: `PORT`, else 5001 (api, agent), 5002 (retrieval), or `MODEL_GATEWAY_PORT`, then `PORT`, then 5003 (model-gateway). With the role unset the process is the monolith (`all`). `npm run start:api|start:agent|start:retrieval|start:model-gateway` wrap the same entry. `compose.services.yml` (an override of `docker-compose.yml`, not combinable with `--profile app`) runs the four tiers from one image with internal identity taken from the shell only (`INTERNAL_SERVICE_AUTH`, default `hmac` with `INTERNAL_SERVICE_KEYS`; under `ed25519` each calling tier gets its own `INTERNAL_SERVICE_SIGNING_KEY_API|_AGENT|_RETRIEVAL` plus the shared `INTERNAL_SERVICE_TRUSTED_KEYS`, printed by `eval "$(node server/service-keys.mjs compose)"`) and only api published. Missing keys are refused by topology validation when a container starts (it exits and `restart: unless-stopped` retries), not by compose interpolation. `test/deployment-contract.test.mjs` pins it as text (CI never builds it), and it was built and started once by hand (2026-11-20 on ca7184a0: hmac, a static token, host Ollama; all five services healthy, upload, /chat through the agent tier and an arXiv import worked, and `--scale agent=2` raised `several_agents_behind_one_name`; `docs/evaluation.md`). `test/service-split.e2e.test.mjs` drives /chat and /chat/stream through all four tiers and compares with the monolith. See "Service split" below and `docs/deployment.md`.
- Build the frontend: `npm run build`.
- Backup and restore (`server/backup-tools.mjs`, `server/scripts/backup-postgres.sh|restore-postgres.sh|verify-backup.sh`, `npm run backup:postgres|backup:restore|backup:verify`; docs/operations.md "备份与恢复"). The URL comes from `--database-url` or `BACKUP_DATABASE_URL` only, never `server/.env` (no dotenv; `audit-verify.mjs` is imported, not spawned, because its CLI loads dotenv); credentials go to pg_dump/pg_restore as `PG*` variables, never argv or the manifest. Every connection of a command (the tool's `pg.Client`s and pg_dump/pg_restore) is resolved once from the URL alone (`resolveBackupConnection`: host or `?host=` socket, port, user, database, password, libpq's defaults for what it omits); the children run without the caller's `PG*` variables and the clients are built with them hidden, so a shell's `PGHOST`/`PGPORT`/`PGPASSWORD` cannot send the checks to another server than the dump, and a URL without a host is refused. TLS follows `rag/postgres-tls.js`: with `POSTGRES_SSL_ENABLED` on the clients take `buildPostgresSslOption` and the children the libpq equivalent (`verify-full` -> `PGSSLMODE=verify-full` with `PGSSLROOTCERT` the CA file or `system`, libpq 16+; `verify-ca`; `no-verify` -> `require`), and a URL with TLS parameters is refused up front (`POSTGRES_SSL_CONNECTION_STRING_CONFLICT`, as verify's in-process health probe would); off, the URL's own `sslmode`/`sslrootcert`/`sslcert`/`sslkey` decide for both, and without them the clients use no TLS and the children libpq's default. Verify hands the app code a canonical URL (`toCanonicalDatabaseUrl`, every part spelled out). Every file a backup writes is created 0600 and an `--out` directory it creates 0700. Backup: the owner login, `pg_dump -Fc --snapshot` inside one exported REPEATABLE READ snapshot with `row_security = off`, and the manifest's inventory (migrations, every table's row count, per-tenant active/trashed document counts, each audit chain's head) read in that snapshot; `--with-files` tars uploads, upload sessions and the data directory without the tenant keystore and the KMS master key, which are deliberately never backed up with the data so erasing a tenant's key crypto-shreds old backups: left out by path (`--keystore-dir` / `--kms-master-key-file`, else `TENANT_KEYSTORE_DIR` / `TENANT_KMS_MASTER_KEY_FILE` in the environment, else the defaults; the tool never reads `server/.env`, where they usually live), by name (`*keystore*`, `*kms-master*`), and by content (`findKeyMaterialFiles`: any regular file of at most 1 MiB whose JSON `format` is `LOCAL_KMS_FILE_FORMATS`' key file, tombstones and temp copies included, or master key), listed in the manifest's `files.keyMaterialExcluded` by path and format only. Restore: checksum check, refuses a non-empty target (exit 3) unless `--force` (`--clean --if-exists`), creates the policies' NOLOGIN roles and the dump's extensions first and drops their TOC entries (they need a superuser), `pg_restore --no-owner --exit-on-error --single-transaction`, then grants the tenant role to the restoring login. Verify (exit 0/1/2): the dump's checksum (`--dump`, else the dump the manifest names when it lies next to it) and the files archive's when it lies next to the manifest (`filesChecksum`), migrations (all applied, none unknown, equal to the manifest; `--allow-pending-migrations`), row and tenant counts, `checkRowLevelSecurityHealth` without error, `audit:verify` plus chain heads. Verify never migrates the restored database: with migrations pending (only possible under `--allow-pending-migrations`) the rowLevelSecurity check, which migrates first, is `skipped: "pending_migrations"`, and the audit chains are read through a store whose `runMigrations` does nothing. Without `--database-url`, `verify-backup.sh` provisions a disposable cluster on a unix socket in a 0700 `/tmp` directory with trust auth (no password anywhere). `test/backup-restore-postgres.integration.test.mjs` needs `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL`. WAL archiving / PITR and per-tenant restore (no `tenant:export` importer) are not implemented.

## Tests And Evaluation

- Backend test runner: `cd server && npm test`; this also validates the GitHub Actions quality gate workflow contract.
- CI job layout (`.github/workflows/quality-gate.yml`): `frontend-checks` (npm test, build), `server-tests` and `quality-gate` run in parallel. `server-tests` (`timeout-minutes: 20`, pgvector service, `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` at job level) runs the backend suite exactly once, through `npm run coverage:gate`; it no longer runs `npm test` first. That is safe because `test/run.test.mjs` and `test/coverage-gate.mjs` discover the same files (every `server/test/*.test.mjs` except `run.test.mjs`, sorted) and the gate exits non-zero when any test fails, so the workflow contract in `ci-workflow.test.mjs` is still checked. `ci-workflow.test.mjs` pins this: exactly one `coverage:gate`, no `npm test`, the timeout, and the two discovery functions (compared as whitespace-stripped source, so a change to either one must be made to both). The gate buffers the child's stdout until the end, so test output appears in the CI log only after the suite finishes (stderr is live). `quality-gate` runs the deterministic evals and `quality:current`.
- Frontend test runner: `npm test` from the repo root. Watch mode: `npm run test:watch`.
- Default synthetic RAG eval: `cd server && npm run eval:synthetic`.
- Current tracked `latest.*` synthetic report uses the near-duplicate corpus: `cd server && npm run eval:synthetic -- evaluation/synthetic-corpus-near-duplicate.json`.
- Run a specific synthetic corpus: `cd server && npm run eval:synthetic -- evaluation/synthetic-corpus-compare-hard.json`.
- Chunking comparison corpus: `cd server && RAG_CHUNK_STRATEGY=structured RAG_CHUNK_OVERLAP=180 npm run eval:synthetic -- evaluation/synthetic-corpus-chunking.json`.
- Parameter sweep benchmark: `cd server && npm run eval:param-sweep`; use `-- --profile full` for the larger topK/overlap/rerank/hybrid matrix.
- Feedback corpus generation: `cd server && npm run feedback:corpus`.
- Feedback regression eval: `cd server && npm run eval:feedback`; this writes ignored `evaluation/generated/feedback-corpus.json` and `evaluation/results/latest-feedback.*`.
- Trajectory eval: `cd server && npm run eval:trajectory`; this writes ignored `evaluation/results/latest-trajectory.*` and checks skill selection, follow-up, clarification, access scope, budget behavior, and three pinned custom-Skill graph cases.
- Historical metrics viewer: `cd server && npm run quality:gate`; its PASS is not current-commit evidence.
- Current PR quality gate: `cd server && npm run quality:current`; it requires the `quality-current` profile plus fresh, clean, same-SHA synthetic/feedback/trajectory/planner/recovery evidence, validates versioned case/check/corpus/response contracts, binds citations to raw retrieval, independently recomputes claim support from pinned corpus pages, verifies deterministic upload identity and recovery cases, and compares only against the pinned deterministic baseline. Metrics remain `unverified` until worktree, lineage, report contracts, and baseline all pass. High-risk trajectory cases persist minimal privacy-safe `case.response.observed` facts; `check.detail` is diagnostic only. Response projections are stable report contracts, not runner-execution proof, content-digest proof, or full internal-event attestations.
- Strict release evidence gate: `cd server && npm run release:gate`; it requires the complete robust/planner/recovery/runtime/readiness report set to carry current-commit, clean-worktree, fresh, corpus/provider-consistent lineage. The guarded rollout evidence additionally requires a real-provider DAG planner case, guarded runtime smoke, and readiness checks; a local mock result is not real-model evidence. Use `-- --no-fail` only for diagnostics; it does not change a failing report to pass.
- Real-corpus eval expects a local corpus file created from `evaluation/real-corpus.example.json` or passed explicitly: `cd server && npm run eval:real -- evaluation/real-corpus.json`.
- Ragas eval runs against saved Node eval payloads: `cd server && npm run eval:ragas -- --input evaluation/results/latest.json`. It requires optional dependencies plus `OPENAI_API_KEY`; see `server/evaluation/ragas-requirements.txt`.
- Retrieval arm comparison: `cd server && npm run eval:retrieval-comparison` uses the deterministic hashed-TF embedding (plumbing only, never an effect claim). `-- --embedding-provider openai` runs real embeddings against any OpenAI-compatible endpoint (including local Ollama) and writes `latest-retrieval-comparison-openai.*` so it never overwrites the deterministic report; `--rerank-provider cross-encoder --cross-encoder-endpoint <url>` measures a neural reranker. Every split reports paired bootstrap 95% CIs; do not describe an arm difference as better unless its interval excludes 0.
- LLM call resilience: `cd server && npm run eval:llm-resilience [-- --no-fallback]` injects 429 (precise and coarse Retry-After), 503s, hanging requests, empty completions, and a down primary against a local fake OpenAI server; no model or key needed.
- Prompt-injection red team: `cd server && npm run eval:prompt-injection` (real model required; cases in `evaluation/prompt-injection-cases.js`) measures attack success on the document RAG answer that MCP `archive_ask` returns and on the `/chat` agent path, plus claim-judge false accepts under a checker-directed payload; `--only` runs selected cases. It writes ignored `evaluation/results/latest-prompt-injection*.{json,md}`.
- Tenant isolation eval: `cd server && npm run eval:tenant-isolation` provisions its own throwaway database (the `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` login needs CREATEROLE and CREATEDB), seeds 20 tenants, and compares `POSTGRES_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY=off` (the pre-RLS connection path) with `enforce` on cross-tenant reads/writes, latency, and query plans; it writes ignored `evaluation/results/latest-tenant-isolation.*`.
- QASPER: `npm run corpus:qasper -- --input <qasper-dev-v0.3.json> --papers all` converts a QASPER release (downloaded to the ignored `evaluation/generated/qasper/`) into the corpus format with paragraph-level evidence pages by default (`--granularity section` is coarser and inflates recall) and every annotator's answer in `referenceAnswers`; `test/qasper.test.mjs` covers it. Run retrieval on it with `npm run eval:retrieval-comparison -- --embedding-provider openai --corpus evaluation/generated/qasper-dev.json --splits full --no-refusal` (the tuning/held-out splits are defined for the arXiv corpus only), and answers with `RAG_ANSWER_TEMPERATURE=0 RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS=1024 npm run eval:qasper-answers [-- --cases 200 --surface rag|agent]` (the answer-side protocol since 2026-11-10, see "Answer temperature"; earlier answer reports ran at the endpoint default 1.0 without a limit, except the 24-question mechanics check `latest-qasper-answers-cap-smoke`, which ran at 0 with the limit), which scores the official QASPER answer F1 (no LLM judge) plus abstention and two evidence figures that must not be confused. Cited: `citedEvidenceRecall` (an annotated evidence page among the pages the answer's `[Source N]` labels resolve to through `citedSourcePages` in `evaluation/qasper-answer-metrics.js`; an abstention cites nothing), `citationPrecision` (macro mean of evidence pages over distinct cited pages, answered answerable rows that cite a page, `citationPrecisionCases`), and `noCitationRateWhenAnswered`. Context: `contextEvidenceRecall` (an evidence page among the pages the model was shown, cited or not; the QA path returns every context chunk as a citation, so it is not a citation metric). Reports before 2026-10-04 called the context figure `evidenceRecall` and the row field `evidenceHit`; pair an old `evidenceHit` only with a new `contextEvidenceHit`. Recall figures also come as `...WhenAnswered`. Two runs over the same sample are compared question by question: `npm run compare:qasper-answers -- <this.json> <other.json> [--iterations 10000] [--seed 12345]` (`evaluation/compare-qasper-answer-reports.mjs`) prints this run minus the other with paired bootstrap 95% CIs for the official F1 (all questions), the answerable F1 with an abstention scored 0, the answer rate (answerable only), unanswerable questions answered, and the cited and context evidence recall where both recorded them (an old `evidenceHit` pairs with `contextEvidenceHit` only), plus the answerable flips (+N / −M) and the config fields that differ; `eval:qasper-answers -- --compare <other.json>` writes the same block as `comparison`. `--exclude-report <report.json>` takes that report's questions out of the corpus before sampling (recorded in `config.excluded` with the report's name, time, commit, count and an id digest; given more than once it takes out the union, and `config.excluded` then holds one such record per report under `reports` beside the union's count and digest, while a single report keeps the old shape) and `--list-cases` prints the sample and exits before anything is ingested. Each row also carries `f1DashNormalized` (dash variants folded to a hyphen before the official F1): a diagnostic, never a summary or a rule. Since 2026-10-22 each row also carries `f1Direct` (the official F1 of the prediction's first sentence; `toQasperDirectPrediction`; until 2026-11-19 rows also carried the deleted extractive mode's `directAnswer`, which it scored instead when present), summarized as `answerF1Direct` / `answerF1DirectWhenAnswered` and paired by `compare:qasper-answers` as `answerF1Direct` / `answerableF1Direct`: the short-answer score, not the official figure. Since 2026-11-20 `--surface agent` plans as `/chat` does, with the four planners `app-services.js` builds (`createAgentSurfacePlanners`) as `AGENT_PLANNER_ROLLOUT` picks them: `llm` by default (slower; its planner calls run at the endpoint's default temperature with no output limit, as in `/chat`, so two runs can plan differently) or `deterministic`, which reproduces the earlier agent reports (they planned with the rules and carry no `config.plannerRollout`); a mistyped value or a removed agent dial (`AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT=off` and the like) stops the run before ingest. Agent reports record `config.plannerRollout`, per row `planners` (whether the intent, execution and graph planners fell back and why, the graph's with the validator's `fallbackReasonCodes`, and the graph's replans) and `summary.agentPlanners`, printed in the .md (per planner the fallback rate and the reasons, an invalid graph's counted by its codes because its message names the model's node ids; the rows where a replan was triggered and applied, `replan_not_triggered` counting nowhere); `compare:qasper-answers` reads an agent report without the field as `deterministic (not recorded)`; `test/qasper-agent-checks.test.mjs` covers it. The paired bootstrap itself lives in `evaluation/paired-bootstrap.js`, shared with `eval:query-adapter` and `eval:qasper-retrieval`. `test/qasper-answer-compare.test.mjs` covers it; the D2/D0 reports of the answer-rate work reproduce the documented deltas. Token F1 punishes long answers; compare this system's runs with each other, not with span-extraction leaderboards.
- Abstention gate analysis: `cd server && npm run eval:abstention-gate -- --corpus evaluation/generated/qasper-train.json` retrieves each QASPER question once through `retrieveQaCandidates` (exported from `rag/document-rag-execution.js`, the QA route up to its gate) and replays the real `assessQaConfidence` over a threshold grid without a chat model. Single-document QA reads `RAG_MIN_QA_QUERY_TERM_COVERAGE` and comparison reads `RAG_MIN_QUERY_TERM_COVERAGE`; both default to 0.51. Do not lower the QA default: every value at or below 0.5 flips the adjacent-topic behaviour tests in `test/rag.test.mjs` and `test/rag-boundary-coverage.test.mjs`, and 0.2 lowered QASPER dev answer F1 from 0.206 to 0.153 (paired CI excludes 0). The report's second row, the gate with the partial coverage band `RAG_QA_ANSWER_VERDICT` opened, went with that flag (deleted 2026-11-20), and so did the dumps' per-question `evidenceRequirementCount`; `eval:qasper-answers` rows record `abstainSource` (`gate`, or `output_limit` for a reply the output limit cut before it wrote anything; reports from before 2026-11-20 can also hold `answer_model`).
  - **Dumps (2026-11-12):** `--dump <file>` writes every question's candidates after retrieval and `--replay <file>` reads them instead of ingesting and retrieving (no embedding model; the report then records the dump's run config, `replayOf` and `analysisGit`). Every summary row carries whole question `counts`, which the reranker re-measure's pick reads instead of the rates rounded to 4 decimals. The script loads dotenv inside `main()` only, so tests importing its pure parts never read `server/.env`. The dense rescue section (`denseRescue`, `--dense-rescue`) went with `RAG_QA_DENSE_RESCUE` (deleted 2026-11-20, see below): the best dense cosine's AUC is the report's dense similarity AUC, and the margin AUC is gone. Tests: `test/abstention-gate-analysis.test.mjs`.
  - **Dump comparison (2026-11-12):** the candidate files also hold each question's `retrievalMs` (the whole retrieval). With reranking on, `describeRetrievalConfig` also records `crossEncoderScores` (`RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_SCORES`). `npm run compare:gate-dumps -- <base dump> <arm dump> [--iterations 10000] [--seed 12345] [--json]` (`evaluation/compare-abstention-gate-dumps.mjs`, never reads `server/.env`) pairs two files of the same questions: evidence among the candidates, at rank 1, in the top 3, admitted by the gate, and in the answer model's context (the gate passes and `selectQaContext`, at the run's own top-K, holds an evidence page), the gate's answerable pass and unanswerable catch, each with a 95% interval resampling papers, plus each file's retrieval milliseconds. It replays the gate under the current environment and refuses when a gate setting either file recorded differs (`GATE_SETTING_FIELDS`, the score scale included), so run it with an allowlisted environment. It also refuses two runs that differ in anything recorded (`describeGateDumpConfigDifferences`: the checkout's sha and changed paths, chunking, every retrieval field, the embedding model, query adapter, corpus and sample) unless `--allow-differences`, and prints each run's commit. It was built for the query expansion round, whose arms could differ only in `retrieval.qaQueryExpansion*`; since the expansion went (2026-11-20), any two runs that differ need `--allow-differences`, and the archived arms' expansion fields are ignored. Tests: `test/compare-abstention-gate-dumps.test.mjs`.
  - **Reranker section (2026-11-12):** with the reranker on, the report also pairs every probability threshold with the lexical gate on the same reranked candidates (`rerankGate.paired`, intervals by paper) and picks among the thresholds and that lexical gate (`pickRerankGate`, `rerankGate.pick`): the cost rule on whole counts (`exactGateCost`); within `RERANK_COST_TOLERANCE` (0.01) of the lowest cost, the most unanswerable questions caught, then the lower cost, then the higher threshold. It serves the re-measure at top-K 10 (docs/evaluation.md "重排在 top-K 10 下重测"); `rerankPick` (the earlier rule, thresholds only) is still reported.
- QASPER evidence recall: `cd server && npm run eval:qasper-retrieval -- --corpus evaluation/generated/qasper-train.json --cases 400 [--compare <other report.json>]` retrieves each answerable question once through `retrieveQaCandidates` and records per question whether an annotated evidence paragraph is at rank 1, in the top 3, among all candidates, and among the chunks the default gate admits; `--compare` adds paired bootstrap CIs against another run over the same sample. No chat model. Tune on train, confirm on dev. Since 2026-10-28 the report also records `config.retrieval` (`describeRetrievalConfig` in `evaluation/eval-run-config.js`: route, hybrid, fusion, RRF k, weights, top-K and sparse top-K, rerank, decomposition, BM25 k1/b, gate floors, read from the same getters the route reads), per row `hitDenseRank` / `hitSparseRank` (the rank each route gave the first evidence chunk, null when that route did not return it; `describeEvidenceHit`) and `timing` (ingest and retrieval ms); `--compare` lists the retrieval settings that differ (field by field when both reports recorded them, one `retrieval` entry when only one did, nothing when neither did, so answer-report comparisons are unchanged). A sweep of fusion or pool settings used to be invisible in the report. The defaults were re-measured on chunker v2 with pre-declared rules (2026-10-29, docs/evaluation.md "检索参数在 chunker v2 上重测"; train seed 11, 400 questions, paired bootstrap by paper): no chunk arm (600/120, 1200/240, overlap 0, overlap 360) or fusion arm (weights 0.5/0.5, RRF k 4, weighted) raised candidate coverage with a paper-clustered interval excluding 0 (weighted fusion +0.0025 [0, +0.008], one question), so nothing changed; under the default fusion the final candidates were a subset of the dense top 8 at top-K 6 (0 sparse-only candidates in 1,621 questions; at top-K 10 both pools are 10 and the final list is a subset of the dense top 10, again 0 in 821 dev questions), weights 0.5/0.5 and k 4 let sparse-only chunks through the 0.51 coverage gate mostly without the evidence being admitted (2 of W50's 33 and 0 of K4's 18 newly gate-confident questions; W50's +0.025 admitted is 10 questions, 8 of whose evidence only the sparse route returned, against a net -6 in candidate coverage: gate coupling, not better retrieval), top-K 10 was recorded at +0.0925 candidate coverage and +0.0475 evidence in context for +25% admitted characters (not adopted there: the answer model's context and cost needed their own measurement, which the next bullet's round made), and dense-only loses evidence in context (-0.0275 train, -0.0171 dev) while ranking top-3 better on dev (+0.033: under the default fusion every both-route chunk outranks every dense-only one, so B's top 3 is both-route first). Do not describe any of these arms as better.
- Retrieval top-K (`RAG_RETRIEVAL_TOP_K`, `getRetrievalTopK`) is 10 since 2026-10-29 (was 6), after a round with pre-declared rules (docs/evaluation.md "候选数 topK 6 → 8 / 10"; tooling f42ccedf, default change 72af7c0a). It is the QA route's candidate count per retrieval query, and `selectQaContext` fills the answer model's context up to it (gate-admitted chunks are never dropped, so a decomposed question can read more). QASPER dev, 821 answerable questions, paper-clustered intervals: evidence among the candidates 0.704 -> 0.784 (+0.080 [+0.063, +0.099]), evidence in context 0.361 -> 0.407 (+0.046 [+0.032, +0.061]), top 3 not significantly different (-0.004 [-0.019, +0.012], +18/-21 flips; recorded only). Gate guardrail (`eval:abstention-gate`, 367 unanswerable questions of train and dev): catch rate 0.531 -> 0.515, inside the pre-declared -0.02 by 0.004. Answers (200 dev questions sampled after excluding the citations report, rag surface, qwen2.5:7b): official F1 +0.013 [-0.008, +0.034], answerable F1 with abstention 0 +0.019 [-0.001, +0.040], F1 on the 106 questions both runs answered +0.027 [-0.006, +0.060], 3 more answered, unanswerable answered unchanged at 12/23; a repeat of the baseline moved F1 by about 0.01 with the same answered set. Cost: about 1,700 more characters of context per answered question (2,876 -> 4,557 on the questions both answered; input tokens median 1,254 -> 1,753, largest prompt 2,354 tokens); latency could not be measured in that round (stage C ran while the machine slept: the baseline repeat moved the median 16%; T10 1.18x) and was measured awake on 2026-11-10 (next bullet: +0.6 s per answered question on that machine). T10 also beat T8 on evidence in context (+0.024 [+0.014, +0.036]). Describe it as more evidence in the model's context at no detected answer-F1 loss, not as better answers. Stage D: `verify:quality` 18/18 at top-K 10; the near-duplicate and compare-hard deterministic reports identical at 6 and 10 once ids, timestamps and timings are masked, apart from `retrievalTopK` and the config hash computed from it; the pinned baseline is `quality-near-duplicate-deterministic-v2` (manifest 1.15.0), written from a pgvector run whose corpus, models, metrics and cases equal v1's (v1 is kept). Not affected by that round: the agent `/chat` document retrieval, whose planner set top-K per intent (`INTENT_OPTIONS` in `agent-query-planner.js`: fact 4, timeline 9, comparison 8, analysis 10; only gap-planner supplemental searches after a gate abstention and `research_question` sub-calls read the default, and they moved to 10 unmeasured), and the comparison route (`RAG_COMPARE_TOP_K_PER_DOC`). Since the fact alignment (docs/evaluation.md "事实题检索条数对齐与跨语言检索") the fact intent sets no top-K: its primary, follow-up and custom-Skill retrievals and its answer context read this default, as the rag surface does (it keeps `topKPerDoc` 2); timeline 9, comparison 8 and analysis 10 keep their own. The robust suite's compare-hard step declares the default stack and now says 10 (top-K does not bind there: its QA case retrieves 2 candidates; `release:gate` recomputes the expected config hash from `eval-suite.js`, no literal is pinned), while its rerank ranking stack (hybrid off, combined scoring) keeps 6; the param sweep's `broad_topk` arm is 12. The cross-encoder rerank pool is top-K x 3 fused candidates per retrieval query, merged per retrieval step and reranked once down to top-K x the step's query count: 30 -> 10 for a one-query step, 60 -> 20 for the agent's two-query fact step, plus 30 -> 10 per cross-language rewrite; the agent's follow-up step still reranks 30 -> 10 per query (see "Neural reranking"). Its weight and probability threshold were tuned on 18 -> 6, and the threshold was re-measured at 30 -> 10 per query on 2026-11-12, before the one rerank per step (the weight was not re-tuned). The round's answer sample is spent, and since 2026-11-12 so is the rest of dev: see "Spent samples" under "Answer temperature".
- Answer funnel, QA latency and answer labelling (2026-11-10): three tools over reports and events the evals already write, none of which calls a model (docs/evaluation.md "默认配置的答案漏斗、延迟和 token"; tests `test/qasper-answer-funnel.test.mjs`, `test/qa-latency-breakdown.test.mjs`, `test/answer-labeling.test.mjs`).
  - `npm run funnel:qasper-answers -- <answers.json> <retrieval.json> [--high-f1 0.5] [--json]` (`evaluation/qasper-answer-funnel.mjs`) joins a rag-surface `eval:qasper-answers` report with the `eval:qasper-retrieval` report of the same settings by question id and puts every answerable question in exactly one stage (`not_retrieved`, `refused`, `not_in_context`, `in_context`), with answered and refused counts (by `abstainSource`) and the answered questions' mean F1, F1 = 0 and F1 >= `--high-f1` counts. It refuses a report that did not record `config.retrieval` (two such reports show no difference and prove no match), reports whose recorded settings differ in anything but the checkout (`git.*`, which is listed, and an unclean or unrecorded checkout is said so in the output), an agent-surface report (the agent retrieves with its planner's own plan: template sub-queries beside the question, no query adapter, and its own top-K for timeline, comparison and analysis), and an answerable question it cannot pair; rows the two runs disagree on (gate decision, evidence in context) are counted in `inconsistent`, never corrected. Whether the evidence was among the candidates comes from the retrieval report alone, so that stage is only as good as the match between the two runs' retrieval: on the local index the sparse statistics span everything a run ingested, which can change the candidates below top-K 8 (top-K 6 picks from the dense top 8). Only an answer or a gate refusal can disagree with the retrieval run, which stops at the gate: a refusal after it (`output_limit`, or `answer_model` in a report from before 2026-11-20) is not counted in `inconsistent`. `eval:qasper-answers` records the resolved embedding model since 2026-11-10 (the environment variable's raw value, null when unset, before), as `eval:qasper-retrieval` does.
  - `npm run latency:qa -- --events <events.jsonl> [--skip N] [--chat-price in,out --embedding-price in] [--compare <other events.jsonl>] [--iterations 10000] [--seed 12345] [--json]` (`evaluation/qa-latency-breakdown.mjs`) reads the observability events (`RAG_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED=true`, `RAG_OBSERVABILITY_EVENTS_PATH`) of a run that asked one question at a time: each `qa` trace closes one question and takes the model calls logged since the previous trace, so it is wrong for a concurrent run and refuses agent traces. Answered and refused questions are reported apart (whole question, answer model, query embedding, and the route's own work, which is the rest), with token figures and, given prices, the cost through `estimateLlmOpsCost`. `--compare` pairs two runs by position (each question's text hash must agree; the text itself is never output) and gives this run minus the other on the questions both answered and on the ones both refused: a bootstrap CI of the mean difference and the median difference. Chat tokens in the events are the endpoint's (`tokenSource: actual`); embedding tokens are estimates from characters. A failed question (a trace without a route) closes itself and is counted in `failedTraces`, so its model calls never land on the next question; other model calls inside a question (a reranker) are counted and said, their time sits in the route's own work; a file holding both the model gateway's metered events and the callers' mirrors is refused; "ingest" counts document embedding calls, so a retried ingest counts twice; a question's query embeddings run concurrently, so their summed time can exceed the question's.
  - `npm run label:answers -- --report <answers.json> --corpus <qasper corpus.json> [--out dir] [--name slug] [--seed 1]` (`evaluation/build-answer-labeling.mjs`) writes a self-contained labelling page for the answered questions (question, every reference, the annotated evidence, the answer; verdicts are `JUDGE_VERDICTS`; labels live in the browser's localStorage) and `<name>-judge-input.json` for `eval:judge`. The page exports `{items, unlabeled}`: `items` is what `eval:judge --labels` reads (every entry has a verdict), `unlabeled` keeps a note or the annotation flag of an answer without one. `buildLabelExport` and `mergeLabelExport` are embedded in the page through `toString`, so they must stay ES5 and self-contained. Keep what protects an hour of labelling (each has a test that runs the page's own script on a stand-in DOM): the storage key names the page and a fingerprint of its answers (`fingerprintLabelingItems`), so a page rebuilt from another report under the same name, or another report's export, never attaches old verdicts to new answers (`--adopt-legacy-labels` lets a rebuild take over labels saved under the name alone before 2026-11-10); a held key does not repeat a verdict (`event.repeat`, arrows excepted) and a clicked button gives up the focus (Space or Enter would press it again on the next answer); every save starts from what is stored, so two tabs do not roll each other back, unless this tab's own saves fail; an import asks before it changes a verdict the page holds, and a note without a verdict never removes one. Nobody has labelled the 2026-11-10 page (121 answers) yet, and the judge needs a model other than the one that answered, so `eval:judge` is still uncalibrated.
  - `eval:qasper-answers` stores each `prediction` whole since 2026-11-10 (cut at 400 characters before; `label:answers` warns about answers of exactly that length).
  - Measured (the topK round's 200-question dev sample, rag surface, qwen2.5:7b through Ollama, local index, machine awake, one question at a time; the default three times and top-K 6 once): of 177 answerable questions the evidence is not among the 10 candidates for 34, among them but refused by the gate for 56, answered without it in the context for 10, answered with it for 77 (mean F1 0.316, 14 at F1 >= 0.5; 31 of the 77 answers contain every token of some reference and 20 less than half; token recall does not judge correctness, so how much of the low score is the metric and how much is a wrong or a reworded answer needs labels); over the 109 answered answerable questions the median answer has 35 words and the median shortest reference 6; every refusal is the gate's. QA route latency of answered questions: p50 2.4 s, p95 3.9 s, 99.5% of it the answer model (prefill and decode about half each on this machine); refused questions p50 17 ms. Tokens per answered question: input mean 1,660 (p95 2,165), output mean 66 (p95 145). Top-K 10 against 6 in one Ollama process, paired over the 118 questions both answered: +604 ms [438, 791] (p50 1.75 s -> 2.40 s), +437 input tokens [398, 474] (mean +36%), no detected change in output tokens; official F1 over all 200 questions -0.001 [-0.021, +0.017]; the same setting twice differs by +113 ms [-36, 263] and +76 ms [-113, 276] for consecutive runs and by +189 ms [52, 327] between the first and the third (the machine slowed over the 45 minutes), and the other order of the arms in separate processes gives +535 ms [376, 702], so read the cost as 0.5 to 0.6 s. From the server log (one-off scripts kept with the logs in the ignored `evaluation/generated/latency/`, not repository tools): prefill +423 ms [368, 474], decode +164 ms [-5, 361], so the certain part is reading the longer context. A later arm in the same Ollama process can restore the earlier arm's cached prompt of the same question (llama-server's prompt cache; 7 of 121 requests here, 0.14 to 1.2 s of prefill each, about 45 ms averaged over the arm): give each arm of a latency comparison a fresh Ollama process. The app sends no temperature and the endpoint samples at 1.0: a setting's answered set is the same every run while its official F1 was 0.2079, 0.2046 and 0.2073 in the three awake runs and 0.2146 in the earlier round's run of the same setting, so do not read an F1 difference of 0.01 or less without a paired interval. Cost is a formula with example prices, never a bill. Not measured: concurrency, the agent `/chat` path with a real model, pgvector retrieval on this route.
- Answer temperature (`RAG_ANSWER_TEMPERATURE`, 0-2, default unset; `getAnswerTemperature` in `rag/config.js`): sent with the two document answer calls (single-document QA and comparison; `completeAnswerText` in `rag/answer-writer.js`) only when set, so by default the requests are what they were; an invalid value refuses the start (`assertAnswerGenerationSettings` in `createAppServices`, code `RAG_ANSWER_TEMPERATURE_INVALID`) and still fails an answer call if it changes at run time. It stays unset by default because some models accept only their default temperature (OpenAI's reasoning models, for example). `eval:qasper-answers` records `config.answerTemperature` and `compare:qasper-answers` lists a difference in it (a report without the field ran without one). The pre-declared round of 2026-11-10 (docs/evaluation.md "答案温度设为 0") failed: at 0, qwen2.5:7b never stopped generating on one question of the clean dev sample (every attempt ran to the 120 s client timeout with 5,600 to 5,800 tokens written, three retries, then the eval aborted), while the same prompt at the endpoint default 1.0 took 91 tokens; in five 1.0 runs (599 answers) the longest answer was 554 tokens. Answer calls have no output cap unless `RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS` is set (next bullet), so do not set 0 without it. Tests: `test/answer-temperature.test.mjs`.
  - **Re-test with the limit (2026-11-10, rules pre-declared in 88fad23d; docs/evaluation.md "答案温度 0 重测：带输出上限") passed all four rules:** arms Z1, B, Z2 (fresh Ollama process each), all with `RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS=1024`, Z at temperature 0 and B at the endpoint default, on the next clean dev 200 (181 answerable / 19 unanswerable). (z1) Z1 and Z2 answered the same 113 questions with 113/113 identical answers; (z2) official F1 Z1 − B +0.0025 [−0.0132, +0.0162], `answeredBothF1` +0.0045 [−0.0241, +0.0299]; (z3) `verify:quality` 18/18 at 0 with the limit; (z4) no answer cut in any arm. Recorded: answered sets equal in all three arms (108/181 and 5/19); answers shorter at 0 (median 30 vs 36 words; 61 shorter, 43 longer, 9 equal; output tokens −4.4 [−11.6, +2.6]); answered questions 0.13–0.16 s faster (Z1 − B −157 ms [−293, −26], Z2 − B −126 ms [−263, +6]), but 29–52 ms of that is B's Ollama process prefilling the same prompts more slowly and the decode difference's interval includes 0, so do not credit the speed-up to temperature. The 1.0 run-to-run swing (0.2046–0.2146) was measured on another sample; this round ran 1.0 once.
  - **Protocol since then:** answer-side evaluation runs with `RAG_ANSWER_TEMPERATURE=0 RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS=1024`; compare such a report with an earlier one (1.0, no limit) only knowing that `compare:qasper-answers` lists both settings as differences. App defaults are unchanged (both unset); deployments that accept a temperature may set 0 only together with the limit. The round left the real-model CI alone; its answer sample (report only) moved to the protocol afterwards, the same day (`real-model-eval.yml`, that step only; its blocking `verify:quality` keeps the endpoint default; pinned by `test/ci-workflow.test.mjs`).
  - **Identical in two local runs, not in two CI runs:** the 113/113 identical answers (z1) came from two runs on the local machine with Ollama 0.34.4. Two CI runs of 2026-11-10 at temperature 0 (Ollama 0.40.3, CPU only; 42909240063 and 42922531176) on the same 40 questions differed. Both answered the same 15 questions, but at least 9 of those 15 answers changed: official F1 0.297 against 0.296, cited evidence 5/15 against 7/15. Nobody has compared two 0.40.3 runs on one machine, so read no small change in the CI answer sample as an effect.
    - **Likelier cause, other hardware (not confirmed):** the second runner was about 2.2x slower. Its evidence-recall step ingested the 141 papers in 1486 s against 685 s, and the answer step's 35 papers took about 334 s against about 144 s.
    - **Unlikely cause, the probe (not excluded):** the second run's probe request came at least 5 min 35 s before its first answer request (question 1 was a gate abstention with no model call). That is past Ollama's default 5-minute keep-alive, which neither the workflow nor the app changes, so the chat model, and the prompt cache inside it, was most likely reloaded, as in the first run. The Ollama log was not kept, so this is not confirmed.
    - **Since then:** the workflow records each runner's CPU model, logical CPUs, physical cores per socket and AVX-512 support (step "Record the runner's CPU"). It also unloads the chat model after the probe (step "Unload the chat model after the probe", `ollama stop`). Without that, a fast runner would reach its first answer within the keep-alive and reuse the probe's model process.
  - **Spent samples:** the round used the next clean sample (`--exclude-report latest-qasper-answers-citations.json --exclude-report latest-qasper-answers-tk-B.json --exclude-report latest-qasper-answers-temp-default.json --cases 200 --seed 1`); the next answer-side confirmation also excludes `latest-qasper-answers-tz-b.json` (the three arms share one sample), which left 116 unused dev questions. The reranker re-measure of 2026-11-12 used all of them (`latest-qasper-answers-rr-B` and `-rr-R` share that sample), so no clean dev question is left: the next answer-side confirmation needs another sample (the QASPER test set, which is a download, or a part of train held out from every selection).
- Answer output limit (`RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS`, a whole number of tokens, default unset; unset, empty or 0 sends none; `getAnswerMaxTokens` in `rag/config.js`): sent with the same two answer calls as `maxTokens` (`completeAnswerText`), only when set, so by default every request is byte-identical. An invalid value refuses the start (`assertAnswerGenerationSettings` in `createAppServices`, code `RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS_INVALID`); read per call it would fail every answer call, and the V1 `/chat` loop carries on past a failed document step to the Web stage. A value changed at run time still fails the call before it is sent.
  - **Field:** `RAG_LLM_MAX_TOKENS_FIELD` (`getLlmMaxTokensField`, code `RAG_LLM_MAX_TOKENS_FIELD_INVALID`): `max_tokens` (default; OpenAI-compatible servers) or `max_completion_tokens` (OpenAI's reasoning models refuse `max_tokens`, and count their hidden reasoning tokens in the other, so a limit sized for the visible answer can leave the reply empty; Ollama ignores it). Read for each capped call by the process that calls the endpoint (the model gateway in a split deployment), and resolved before the request enters the model-call guard (`toMaxTokensField` in `openai-client.js`), so a wrong value takes no slot and the circuit breaker never counts it as a success. `createAppServices` and `createModelGatewayApp` refuse to start with a wrong value.
  - **Paths:** a stand-in provider gets `maxTokens`; the direct client sends the field streamed or not; the gateway client always sends `max_tokens`, and the gateway (`readChatOptions` in `model-gateway/app.js`) refuses a value that is not a positive whole number with 400 instead of dropping it (it still drops an invalid temperature) and reads its own field there, before the streamed and plain answers part ways. An older gateway ignores `max_tokens`, so upgrade the gateway before setting the limit on its callers.
  - **Finish reason:** `completeTextWithMetadata` reports the finish reason of the reply it returns through the `onFinishReason` option, once, after any retry or failover; a stand-in provider reports none. Its result stays `{ modelRoute, text }`: the planner-model and adapter goldens record it, so do not add a field there. A cut reply is never retried, empty or not (an empty finished one still is, once).
  - **Marking:** a reply that stopped at an output limit (`finish_reason: "length"`, this one or the endpoint's own) marks the answer `answerTruncated: true` on the RAG response, in its trace and on the V1 agent's `document_rag` / `follow_up_retrieval` step output, and MCP `archive_ask` (`formatAskResult`) carries it, only then (`markTruncated`). The mark says an answer call behind this answer was cut, not that the final text is: a QA answer keeps the cut text as written, and a comparison goes through its gate and fallbacks as before. A QA reply cut before it wrote anything is an abstention, `abstainSource: "output_limit"`, no citations (a reasoning model can spend the whole limit out of sight); `funnel:qasper-answers` counts it as a refusal after the gate. The semantic cache never stores a marked answer (`storeSkipReason: "answer_truncated"`), and `RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS` is part of its key.
  - **Reports:** `eval:qasper-answers` reads the settings once before it ingests anything and records `config.answerMaxTokens` / `config.answerMaxTokensField` (null without a limit; `"model_gateway"` when the calls go through a gateway, whose own setting picks the field), `answerTruncated` per row and `summary.answerTruncations` (answerable / unanswerable); `compare:qasper-answers` lists a difference in either field (a report without them ran without a limit).
  - **Tests:** `test/answer-max-tokens.test.mjs` (getters and their codes, the start-up refusals, the two calls, request bodies direct and streamed, the field resolved before the guard, the finish reason, cut and empty replies, the marks, the gateway in both directions and its refusal), `test/answer-max-tokens-report.test.mjs`, the verdict-retry case in `test/rag.test.mjs`, the MCP case in `test/archive-mcp-tools.test.mjs` and the cache key case in `test/semantic-cache.test.mjs`.
  - **Measured (2026-11-10, a mechanics check, not a pre-declared round; docs/evaluation.md "答案输出上限"):** Ollama 0.34.4 honours `max_tokens` (8 -> `length` after 8 tokens) and ignores `max_completion_tokens` (8 -> 185 tokens, `stop`), so never pair `max_completion_tokens` with Ollama. With `RAG_ANSWER_TEMPERATURE=0 RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS=1024`, the first 24 questions of the clean dev sample (the same sampler prefix) finished in 68 s with ingest: question 24, which never stopped at 0 without a limit, stopped at 1,024 tokens (20.5 s of decoding at 50 tok/s, 22 s for the question) with `answerTruncated: true` and a repetitive 747-word answer; no other question reached the limit. The pre-declared re-test of temperature 0 with the limit then passed (see "Answer temperature").
  - **Probe:** `npm run probe:output-limit [-- [--max-tokens 8] [--json]]` (`evaluation/probe-output-limit.mjs`, tests in `test/output-limit-probe.test.mjs`) checks that an endpoint honours the field. Run it before trusting the limit on another endpoint, model or server version.
    - **What it sends:** one request through the app's own chat client (`createChatClient`), with the answer calls' model, key, field and timeout, to one endpoint: `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, else `OPENAI_API_BASE`, else `https://api.openai.com/v1`. The request is "Count from 1 to 200, one number per line." with a limit of 8 tokens and no temperature. For a model gateway, run it with the gateway's settings. When `MODEL_GATEWAY_CHAT_UPSTREAMS` lists replicas, run it once per replica with `OPENAI_BASE_URL` set to that replica; the probe does not read the list.
    - **Exit codes:**
      - 0: the reply stopped for length and the endpoint reported at most the limit's tokens.
      - 1: the endpoint reported more tokens than the limit, whether the reply finished on its own or was cut at a longer limit of the endpoint's own.
      - 2: no verdict. That covers a bad argument or setting (nothing is sent), a request the model-call guard refused, a failed request, a reply that finished on its own within the limit, and a reply without a token count. A stop for length without a count is included, since it may be the endpoint's own cap.
    - **Output:** the verdict, the finish reason, the token count, the limit, the field, the model and the endpoint's host. It never prints the reply or the key.
      - A failed request adds the endpoint's HTTP status and error message, with the key and the URL's credentials removed, cut at 300 characters.
      - An error raised before any answer prints only its name and code, because fetch quotes the Authorization value or the whole URL in some of its messages.
      - The command calls `process.exit` once it has printed: under `RAG_SHARED_STATE=redis`, the guard's Redis client would otherwise keep the process alive.
    - **Measured:** on Ollama 0.34.4 it passes with `max_tokens` (`length` after 8 tokens) and fails with `max_completion_tokens` (723 tokens, `stop`). That is a different request from the mechanics check above, which counted to 60 at temperature 0 and wrote 185 tokens.
    - **In CI:** the real-model CI runs it before its answer sample. The first CI run under the answer protocol (2026-11-10, run 42909240063) had no reply reach the limit, so it showed nothing about the pinned Ollama 0.40.3. The probe's first CI run (42922531176, the same day) passed: 0.40.3 stopped for length after 8 tokens, in a 12 s step that included loading the model. If a later version ignores the field, the step fails once the probe's own unlimited reply ends. That case has not been timed on the runner, and it takes one request timeout at most.
- Layout parsing: `cd server && npm run eval:layout-parsing` parses the QASPER dev papers with the most table-evidence questions (PDFs from arxiv.org, downloaded beforehand into the ignored `evaluation/generated/qasper-pdfs/`) with both pdf.js and Docling, ingests both side by side, and asks every answerable question of both versions: whether a gold extractive span occurs in the parsed text and in the retrieved chunks, and the official answer F1, with paired CIs; text-evidence questions of the same papers are the control. Needs docling-serve (`DOCLING_SERVE_URL`) and a real model. `-- --parsers pdfjs,pdfjs-paragraphs` compares legacy pdf.js with pdf.js under `PDF_PARAGRAPH_DETECTION` instead (no docling-serve; the delta is the second parser minus the first, and the report adds an all-questions section); `--concurrency N` keeps N questions in flight. `auto` is a third arm (what `PDF_PARSER=auto` ingests, needs docling-serve; use `--parsers pdfjs-paragraphs,auto` to isolate the routing, because the `pdfjs` arm forces paragraph detection off) and adds `routing.auto` to the report. Measured once (2026-10-18, docling-serve 1.37, qwen2.5:7b, `pdfjs-paragraphs,auto`, 149 questions): no CI excludes 0 (table-question F1 +0.008 [-0.031, +0.053], all questions +0.005 [-0.020, +0.032]), so do not describe `auto` as better.
- Office files against Office's own PDF export (rules pre-declared in docs/evaluation.md "Office 文件对比 Office 自己导出的 PDF"):
  - **Corpus:** `cd server && npm run corpus:office-formats [-- --seed 1 --sheets 18 --decks 16 --papers 30]` (`evaluation/build-office-format-corpus.mjs`) builds the corpus with Word, PowerPoint and Excel through AppleScript. It needs macOS, Microsoft 365 for Mac, and permission for the calling app to control the three apps. The Office sandbox writes only where it may, so the work happens in a folder inside the Office group container, which is removed at the end.
    - `word`: QASPER train papers set as Word documents by the fixture writer, then opened and saved by Word.
    - `sheet`: synthetic workbooks written by SheetJS. Excel fits the columns, repeats the header row on every page and scales to one page wide before saving and exporting.
    - `deck`: synthetic decks that PowerPoint makes from scratch, with speaker notes.
    - Each document is kept as the Office file and as the PDF its application exported, in the ignored `evaluation/generated/office-formats/`. They are never committed, because Office writes the account name into file properties. Their digests go into `manifest.json`, with the questions and gold answers.
  - **Run:** `npm run eval:office-formats` (`evaluation/run-office-format-eval.mjs`).
    - It checks the digests first.
    - It ingests both versions through `ingestDocument` (`<id>__native`, `<id>__pdf`) into a throwaway standalone archive.
    - It asks every question of both on the single-document QA route. Speaker-notes questions go to the native deck only; the PDF has no notes.
  - **Scores and report:** `evaluation/office-format-eval.js` holds the pure parts, tested in `test/office-format-eval.test.mjs`.
    - Scores: QASPER answer F1 for `word`, with an abstention scored 0 as the rules declare (the official evaluator scores it as the answer "Unanswerable", which earns 1 when one annotator marked the question unanswerable); whether the answer gives the gold value for the other two (numbers by value, dates by day, text by whole words; a "[Source N]" label, an ISO date's parts and digits glued to a letter such as "Q3" are not numbers the answer gives). Also whether the evidence was in the chunks the model was shown (none when the QA gate refuses, so it is false on every gate refusal and not independent of the answer rate; spans and chunks are folded alike, typographic quotes and dashes included), and whether the answer abstained.
    - The report gives native minus PDF with bootstrap 95% intervals by question and by document, in the ignored `evaluation/results/latest-office-formats.*`. Read differences by the by-document interval.
  - **Measured (2026-11-11, a9aefd00, qwen2.5:7b through Ollama 0.34.4 under the answer-side protocol; the detail is in docs/evaluation.md):** all 64 documents ingested in both arms, so rule 2 dropped none. By-document intervals:
    - `sheet` (18 workbooks, 108 questions): answers correct 106/108 native against 96/108 PDF, +0.093 [+0.037, +0.157], the only corpus-level difference whose interval excludes 0; all 10 flips went to native. Of the pre-declared column-type subgroups, person columns also exclude 0 (+0.240 [+0.043, +0.500], 25 questions in 11 workbooks) and numbers do not (+0.093 [0, +0.185]); the subgroups only show where the difference sits. The PDF loses which column a value belongs to: its rows are space-separated values under one header line per page.
      - Three rows sat in chunks without that line, and at 0.5 query-term coverage none reached the QA gate's 0.51: no chunk of those PDFs passes 0.5, while a native chunk reaches 1.
      - The other seven were the model's misreadings with the header line in its context (rebuilt from each row's `contextChars`; in four the header shared the row's chunk): five Manager questions answered "no manager listed", and two Spent questions given the Budget value one column to the left.
      - Evidence in context and answer rate are the same three gate refusals, +0.028 [0, +0.056] each: no detected difference.
    - `word` (30 QASPER train papers, 117 questions): F1 with abstentions at 0 is 0.130 against 0.126, +0.004 [−0.014, +0.024]; evidence in context +0.043 [−0.034, +0.132]; answer rate +0.009 [−0.027, +0.049]. No detected difference. Both arms abstained on 45–46% of the questions (the gate), and the answers given run long.
    - `deck` (16 decks): body questions 64/64 in both arms, at the ceiling, so this corpus cannot tell the formats apart. The 32 speaker-notes questions were 32/32 on the native decks. PowerPoint's default PDF export holds slides only, none of the notes; a notes-page export was not tried.
    - Rule 4: 30 seed-1 correct sheet answers per arm, read one by one by Claude (not human labels), all gave the asked column's value; the independent check then read all 376 sheet and deck answers and agreed with every verdict. Both arms miss "What is the Office for <name>?" twice in one workbook because the model reads "Office" as a noun; the pair cancels in the delta, and the cases stay as they are.
    - Scorer defects the independent check found, fixed with tests after the run, none of which changes a verdict: a word abstention could score 1 (the stored report prints F1 0.139 / 0.135; with abstentions at 0 it is 0.130 / 0.126, same delta and intervals); a gold span with curly quotes never matched (one question; corrected, PDF word evidence is 33/69 and the delta +0.029 [−0.046, +0.114] if the native context lacked it, unchanged otherwise, since the report keeps no contexts); and "[Source N]" labels, date parts and "Q3" counted as answer numbers (0 of 376 sheet and deck verdicts change).
    - No default changed (rule 3). Not measured: real-world workbooks and decks, other models and Office versions, PDFs without repeated headers or fitted columns, and questions that aggregate over a table.
- pdf.js parse bench: `cd server && npm run bench:pdf-parse -- --pdf-dir <dir> [--pdf-dir <dir>] [--workers 2,4,8] [--repeat 3]` (`evaluation/run-pdf-parse-bench.mjs`; no docling-serve, no model, never reads `server/.env`) dedupes the PDFs by content, checks that the `PDF_PARSE_WORKERS` pool (sizes 1/2/4 with 32/3/2/32 pages per task, paragraph detection on and off, signals collected) deepStrictEquals the in-process parse (exit 1 otherwise), times the whole set one after another and all at once, text only and with signals, with the longest main-thread event-loop stall, and counts pages per route and reason under the shipped `PDF_AUTO_*` thresholds. It writes ignored `evaluation/results/latest-pdf-parse-bench.*` (file names, counts and timings only); `test/pdf-parse-bench.test.mjs` covers it.
- docling-serve routing check: `cd server && DOCLING_SERVE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:5010 npm run eval:docling-routing -- [--papers 30] [--numbering-papers 3] [--arms docling:0:1,docling:4:2,auto:0:1:0,auto:0:1:2,auto:0:2:2] [--ocr-pdf <image-only PDF> --ocr-reference <the PDF it was rendered from>]` (`evaluation/run-docling-routing-check.mjs`; no model, never reads `server/.env`) needs a real docling-serve. It checks which page numbers a `page_range` request comes back with (raw DoclingDocument), warms one page per docling-serve worker, then parses the papers one after another per arm (`<docling|auto>:<DOCLING_PAGE_BATCH_SIZE>:<DOCLING_MAX_CONCURRENCY>[:<DOCLING_MERGE_GAP_PAGES>]`), times each arm and compares its pages with the first arm (identical pages and min token Dice; `auto` only on the pages it took from Docling), and with the OCR pair reports each page's route and the token recall of Docling's text against pdf.js's text of the original. It writes ignored `evaluation/results/latest-docling-routing.*` (paper ids, counts, timings, scores; never page text); `test/docling-routing-check.test.mjs` covers its pure parts.
- Real-model CI: `.github/workflows/real-model-eval.yml` (weekly and manual) installs Ollama on the runner with `nomic-embed-text` and a chat model (default `qwen2.5:7b`), no API key, then runs `verify:quality` (blocking), `eval:qasper-retrieval` on a fixed 200-question QASPER dev sample with `--min-hit-at-all 0.6` (blocking; 0.655 when set), `probe:output-limit` (blocking, right before the answer sample and under the same condition: the answer step's limit holds only if the pinned Ollama honours `max_tokens`; see "Answer output limit"), and a small `eval:qasper-answers` sample (report only; since 2026-11-10 at `RAG_ANSWER_TEMPERATURE=0 RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS=1024`, set on that step alone, so the blocking steps keep the endpoint default; two runs of 2026-11-10 gave different answers, see "Answer temperature"). Each run also records the runner's CPU before the model steps, and unloads the chat model between the probe and the answers, so the answers start on a freshly loaded model. `test/ci-workflow.test.mjs` pins that contract.
- Load test: `cd server && npm run eval:load-test:pgvector` (or `eval:load-test` for the local store only) runs `evaluation/run-api-load-bench.mjs`. It starts the real app from `createApp()` in a child process against an in-process fake OpenAI-compatible model with injected chat latency (0 and 800 ms by default), then drives closed-loop `/chat` and `GET /documents` load at concurrency 1/4/16/32. The wrapper creates a disposable Postgres.app cluster on an OS-assigned port and removes it on exit. The runner never reads `server/.env`, and it refuses a `--database-url` that already has `schema_migrations`. Keep the file name off Node's `*-test.mjs` glob, or coverage drops it. It writes ignored `evaluation/results/latest-load-test.*`. With a PostgreSQL registry, `GET /documents` and the start of every `/chat` re-read the tenant's document rows (cross-instance visibility), so the old in-memory `GET /documents` figure no longer applies. Multi-instance and ingest options: `--instances`, `--balance` (default least-outstanding), `--shared-state redis`, `--tenant` (tenant transactions under RLS; without it the report says owner path), `--no-analyze`, `--scenario ingest`, `--ingest-mode`, `--ingest-workers`, `--repeat`; keep both sides of any before/after pair under their own `--latest-name`. The agent runs on LangGraph only (the graphs since phase 6, the outer plan since phase 7), so the bench no longer measures a native scheduler: `--agent-runtime` and `--outer-plan-runtime` still exist but accept only `langgraph` and change nothing, `native` fails the bench's own argument check ("--agent-runtime native was removed (ADR 0001 phase 6) ...", "--outer-plan-runtime native was removed (ADR 0001 phase 7) ..."), and any other value is refused there too. The app children never get `AGENT_RUNTIME` or `AGENT_OUTER_PLAN_RUNTIME` (they drop inherited `AGENT_*` variables and the bench sets neither), so a shell's value never reaches them and the app's own start-up refusal never fires in a bench child. Every report still records `config.agentRuntime` and `config.outerPlanRuntime` in its `Agent runtime` and `Outer plan runtime` rows: both `langgraph` now. In reports written before phase 6 `agentRuntime: native` measured the rollback, reports before phase 4 ran `native` unless they record otherwise, and the phase-3 `latest-load-test-p3-langgraph-r3` configuration had both dials at `langgraph`; reports written between the phase-3 split and phase 7 ran the outer plan on the native loop unless they record `outerPlanRuntime: langgraph`, so their `/chat` CPU per request is about 1 ms below what the same commit measures now (phase 5: 5.68 against 6.68 ms at concurrency 1). `--topology split` (`npm run eval:load-test:split`) runs each tier as its own child process (`--api/--agent/--retrieval/--gateway N`, chat scenario and pgvector only), with the fake model behind the gateway, and reports per-tier CPU, the busiest tier and `model.directCalls` (must be 0). `--repeat N` now also applies to chat and reports min-max ranges. The model concurrency cap is per gateway process in split mode and per instance in monolith mode unless `--with-redis`, so compare 800 ms figures across topologies with that in mind. `bash scripts/run-load-test-pgvector.sh --read-replica --tenant [flags]` also provisions one streaming replica of the disposable primary (pg_basebackup -R, OS-assigned port), loads pg_stat_statements on both nodes and passes `--read-replica-url`, which sets `POSTGRES_READ_REPLICA_URLS` for the app processes (it needs `--tenant` and pgvector). The report adds `config.readReplica` and per run `readReplica` (routing counts over the measured chat levels, and per-node statement counts by kind); `test/postgres-replica-load-bench.test.mjs` covers those helpers. The replica runs on the same machine as the primary, so the report says nothing about network distance.
- Read-replica integration: `cd server && bash scripts/run-pgvector-replica-integration.sh` provisions a disposable primary (`wal_level=replica`) and a `pg_basebackup -R` hot standby on OS-assigned 127.0.0.1 ports under `$TMPDIR`, runs `test/postgres-replica.integration.test.mjs` with `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and `PGVECTOR_TEST_REPLICA_URL` (superuser, because it pauses replay with `pg_wal_replay_pause`), and removes both on exit (`KEEP_CLUSTER=1`, `PG_BIN_DIR`, `PGVTEST_DB` optional). Without both URLs the suite is skipped. It is not part of `test:pgvector` or CI; `npm run test:pgvector-replica` runs the script above.
- pgvector scale: `cd server && npm run bench:pgvector-scale` loads 10k–1M synthetic chunks into a disposable cluster through the real migrations. It times the app's dense, sparse and hybrid searches under RLS enforce, with warm series and separately timed first reads, and measures recall@10 against the exact result of the app's own SQL. Filtered series check every query, and the report states whether HNSW was used. It writes ignored `evaluation/results/latest-pgvector-scale.*`. A recall of 1 from a plan that did not use HNSW says nothing about the index. The whole-table HNSW recall on this synthetic data is a stress case, not an app path.
- Framework-only entry point (not yet run on real data): `npm run eval:judge -- --input <answers.json> [--labels <labels.json>]` is an LLM judge that reports agreement and Cohen's kappa against human labels. Do not quote judge scores that have not been calibrated.
- Measured numbers live in `docs/evaluation.md` (Chinese) with their commands and commits; update it first when a measured number changes, then the README's results and limits. `server/docs/interview/` is ignored personal notes: do not track it or cite it from tracked files.

Backend `npm test` runs `test/run.test.mjs`, which discovers every `server/test/*.test.mjs` file at runtime and runs them under `node --test` in per-file child processes; a new suite only needs to be dropped into `server/test/`, not registered anywhere. The typed-DAG suites include `agent-execution-graph.test.mjs`, `agent-execution-graph-runner.test.mjs`, `agent-execution-graph-node.test.mjs`, `agent-dag-planner-adapter.test.mjs`, `agent-replanner.test.mjs`, `agent-replan-adapter.test.mjs`, `agent-custom-skill-stage.test.mjs`, `agent-authorized-catalog.test.mjs`, `agent-dynamic-skill-selection.test.mjs`, and `agent-execution-graph-checkpoint.test.mjs`. `app-planner-adapters.test.mjs` drives a background task through `createAppServices`; `runtime-smoke-graph-signal.test.mjs` tests persisted guarded-run evidence without external I/O. Concurrency assertions in graph suites use barriers and deferred promises; do not replace them with millisecond timing assertions.

`agent-execution-graph-postgres.integration.test.mjs` uses `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and a separate Node process to verify a persisted graph checkpoint resumes from the PostgreSQL agent-run store without replaying a completed write. The disposable `cd server && bash scripts/run-pgvector-integration.sh` runner includes it; without the database URL it is skipped, not passed.

## Implementation Notes

- Keep RAG changes inside `server/rag/` where possible; route/API behavior lives in the `server/routes/` routers that `server/app.js` mounts.
- AgentRAG skills are registered in `server/rag/skills/registry.js`; built-ins live in `server/rag/skills/built-ins.js`, and whitelisted custom skills live under `server/rag/skills/custom/`. Current custom skills include `extract_timeline`, `risk_review`, `summarize_contract`, and `compare_documents`.
- New skills need stable `id/version/label/budgetKey/requiresAccessScope`, deterministic `match()`, and an `execute()` path that receives `accessScope` when reading user/workspace data. Custom skills must be exported from `server/rag/skills/custom/index.js`; do not let the model call arbitrary unregistered tools. The model-facing atomic custom-Skill catalog in `server/rag/skills/authorized-catalog.js` is narrower: it requires explicit typed input/output contracts, declared effects, a scoped document input, and successful `getDocument(docId, accessScope)` checks for every selected document; an empty or unverifiable document set grants no catalog entries. It is independent of V1 intent `match()` and composite chains.
- Agent bootstrap/session setup lives in `server/rag/agent-bootstrap.js`; keep working memory creation, planning, selected skill ordering, run context creation, and skill tracker wiring there.
- Agent preparation flow lives in `server/rag/agent-preparation-flow.js`; keep plan trace, pre-execution clarification, query planning trace, and skill chain trace there.
- Agent run context lives in `server/rag/agent-run-context.js`; keep trace appending, budget snapshot helpers, agent trace recording, observability assembly, and clarification response orchestration there rather than rebuilding those cross-cutting helpers in `server/rag/agent.js`.
- Document RAG execution loop lives in `server/rag/agent-document-loop.js`; keep primary document RAG, self-check, gap analysis, follow-up retrieval, evidence clarification, and working-memory claim/gap updates there rather than expanding `server/rag/agent.js`. Its primary and follow-up self-checks require citations from two documents (`maxRequiredDocCoverage` in `self-check/evaluate.js`) only when that phase's retrieval plan has intent `comparison`; any other question passes on one cited document however many are in scope (since 2026-11-20: before, a fact question over the UI's default scope of every uploaded document ended in a clarification). Comparison comes from the plan (compare_documents) or the question's words (`COMPARISON_SIGNAL_PATTERN` in `agent-query-planner.js`: the compar-, differ-, similar-, contrast- and conflict- words in their inflections, versus/vs/same/between/across, and 区别/差别/差异/不同/不一样/异同/对比/比较/相比/相较/比起/冲突/一致/相同/相似/类似/共同点; compartment and the technical terms differential, contrastive, similarity and 相似度 do not count), so it errs both ways. A question about one document in comparison words still needs two cited documents when more are in scope, and ends in a clarification when its answer cites one: between/same/different always did, and since the pattern was widened from whole words on 2026-11-20 (user: "把这里面的全做了") so do "How do RAG-Sequence and RAG-Token differ ...?" and "... compared with BERT re-ranking?" over every uploaded document, the only questions whose match changed among the 248 entries of the deterministic corpora and cases (4 entries: both are single-document arXiv questions, each in `arxiv-corpus-cases.json` and the rerank corpus); `eval:trajectory` (15/15) and `eval:planner -- --provider mock` (6/6) were identical after masking. A comparison in other words ("both policies", "2024 or 2025", "which is better") still passes on one cited document, so a one-sided answer to it is not caught. The compare_documents Skill's own trigger (`COMPARE_DOCUMENTS_SIGNAL_PATTERN` in `agent-intent-rules.js`) is separate and was not widened. Neither was the answer route, which is decided apart from the intent: only `routeQuery` (`query-router.js`, its own whole-word list compare/comparison/difference(s)/different/versus/vs/same/similar/conflict(s)/contradict(ion)/contrast and 区别/差异/不同/对比/比较/冲突/一致/相同, plus its document-selection, comparative and cross-document signals) sends a question to the per-document comparison route (`executeComparisonRag`: `topKPerDoc` evidence from each document and the comparison prompt). A question that is a comparison only through the wider words (differ, compared, similarities, 异同, 相比, 相似, 共同点 and the like) is answered on the QA route, from the global top-K with the comparison profile's queries, and only checked as a comparison, so an answer citing one document gets the follow-up, and a clarification if the follow-up's answer cites one too. One shared word list would give those questions evidence from each document and the comparison prompt, and would also move one-document questions in those words onto the comparison route; that choice is the owner's (review of 2026-11-20). The Final Self Check (`agent-answer-verification.js`) keeps min(docIds, 2).
- Non-document skill execution: built-in runners live in `server/rag/agent-built-in-skill-runners.js` and web search execution lives in `server/rag/agent-web-runner.js`; custom Skills are run by the graph stage `server/rag/agent-custom-skill-stage.js` (the chain runner `agent-custom-skill-runner.js` was deleted in ADR 0001 phase 8; the barrel `agent-skill-runners.js` this line used to name no longer exists). Keep those execution details out of `server/rag/agent.js`.
- The custom skill stage has one executor. `server/rag/agent-custom-skill-stage.js` plans a typed DAG over the custom Skills the runtime authorized for the request (`rag/skills/authorized-catalog.js`) and LangGraph runs it; it returns a flat skill result array, emits the `custom_skill` step type, and uses the one run store and the one replay safety matrix. The V1 sequential chain (`agent-custom-skill-runner.js`, `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT=off|shadow`, and the fallback for a graph rejected before any node ran) was deleted in ADR 0001 phase 8 (2026-11-13, the owner's decision; see the phase-8 bullet under the LangGraph migration below): do not add a second executor, a rollout value or a fallback back. What the chain did is the graph's business: (1) the deterministic graph (`createDeterministicExecutionGraph`: the selected Skills in order, each handing its findings to the next as a typed `priorFindings` input) is not rejected for the call budget at plan time, because the executor reserves the budget per node at launch and skips a node it cannot pay for, so a budget that covers one of two selected Skills still runs that one; a model's graph is still budget-checked whole; (2) a graph rejected for any other reason runs nothing: each selected Skill gets a failed result (`AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_REJECTED`) and one `skill_graph_rejected` trace step says why; (3) no Skill selected and no usable proposal returns an empty result, never the authorized catalog. There is deliberately no second attempt after partial execution. When a Skill fails, the next one still runs, on the original question, as the chain ran it: a failed dependency that feeds a node only optional inputs (the deterministic graph's `priorFindings`) and that the node's `when` does not read does not block it, and those inputs are unset (`decideExecutionGraphNode`, user's decision of 2026-11-16, ADR 0001 phase 12; from 2026-09-25 until then the second Skill was skipped and the run abstained). A dependency that failed on an outage (`didExecutionGraphNodeFailOnOutage`: the model or storage is down, so the next Skill would call it again and the run ends with the outage instead), a skipped dependency, a required input, an ordering-only edge, a node without a contract and an abort after a `fail_fast` failure still block. A request that selects one Skill and gets no Skill result answers the fixed sentence "The custom skill could not complete the request." instead of the abstention. A custom Skill must declare the typed contract (`effects`, `inputSchema`, `outputSchema`, `requiresAccessScope`, a required scoped `docIds`) or it is not in the authorized catalog and does not run. A node skipped at launch has no entry in the result array and is not listed in `agentSkills`; the skip is in the `budget_limit` trace step, the node run and the Skill observation.
