The planner dial is separate: `AGENT_PLANNER_ROLLOUT` (`llm`, the default, or `deterministic`) picks the intent, execution and DAG planners together, and a failed LLM plan falls back to the deterministic graph, so `rollout:readiness` (zero fallback, zero mock/real divergence) measures pure-LLM planning. Its other values were removed on 2026-11-19 (user: "按推荐全做"): `configured`, `guarded_llm`, `shadow` and any typo refuse in `createAppServices` (`AGENT_PLANNER_ROLLOUT_REMOVED`; a typo used to mean `llm`), and `AGENT_INTENT_PLANNER` / `AGENT_EXECUTION_PLANNER`, which only `configured` read, are no longer read; do not add a planner value, a per-planner variable or a shadow planner back. The LLM execution planner sees authorized atomic Skills and may add a `custom_skills` stage the intent did not select. Keep the composite intents and the deterministic planner. `test/agent-custom-skill-budget.test.mjs` pins what a `/chat`-shaped run answers with a budget of two, one and no calls and with documents the stage cannot verify; its comments say where that differs from what the chain answered. It also pins the first-Skill-fails case, the single-Skill request, and that the `skill_graph_rejected` trace step is stored as a `decision` step with status `skipped`.
- The V2 typed DAG is split across `server/rag/agent-execution-graph.js` (versioned graph contract plus the pure-function validator/compiler), `agent-execution-graph-runner.js` (admission and the run tail; it schedules nothing since phase 6 removed the native loop `runExecutionGraph`), `rag/langgraph/run-execution-graph.js` (`runExecutionGraphOnLangGraph`, the only scheduler: the validator's layer-order queue (`orderExecutionGraphNodeStatesByLayer`), runtime concurrency cap, side-effect serialization; every call is a fresh invocation on a tombstoned thread, and nothing parks a node or resumes a thread since phase 11), `agent-execution-graph-node.js` (what one node does once launched, with explicit state and no closures, so the LangGraph scheduler runs the nodes the native one ran: node states and checkpoint reuse, the pure skip decision, input resolution and scope checks, runtime input/output schema gates, atomic budget reservation, the fenced step lifecycle, the settle order, node run records and the run result; `control.aborted` / `control.interrupt` are raised at the moment the old closure variables were, which a scheduler woken by a sibling depends on), `agent-dag-planner-adapter.js` (redacted whitelist-only planning surface that emits atomic skill nodes rather than composite chain ids), and `agent-replanner.js` (bounded patch-only replan, `maxReplans: 1`). Every initial plan and every replan patch goes through the same validator, which accepts or rejects a graph whole so an illegal graph never partially executes. The runner also validates resolved input before budget/execution and actual output before downstream binding, including reused checkpoint output. Node inputs bind only to validated request fields or upstream node outputs; do not reintroduce concatenated free text as the primary data interface. The planner never decides scope, `docIds`, approvals, budget, concurrency, or retry limits — the runtime owns those. This DAG covers custom Skills only, not the outer document/web/built-in/capability stages.
- v3 unified graph removed (ADR 0001 phase 10, 2026-11-14; what it was and why it went is in `docs/unified-agent-dag-migration.md`). Every request is answered by the outer execution plan. There is no second way to answer a request, and none may be added back: not a whole-request graph, not a shadow observation of one, not a rollout dial or a fallback for one.
  - **Dial:** `AGENT_UNIFIED_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` selects nothing. Unset or `off` starts; `guarded`, `shadow` or any other value refuses the start (see "Start-up refusal" under the LangGraph migration). `AGENT_UNIFIED_GRAPH_CAPABILITIES` is no longer read.
  - **Stored runs:** a deployment that ran `guarded` may still hold that graph's runs. `rag/agent-removed-unified-graph.js` is all the code left for them: it recognizes one by its checkpoint version (`v2`; the custom-Skill graph writes `v1`) and never runs a node or decides a gate. Two rules, both in that module. A request that continues one of them gets a new run and leaves the stored run as it is, settled or not (since 2026-11-17, as for every run). An unfinished one is still handed to an operator: startup recovery (unless `AGENT_RUN_RECOVERY_MODE=off`) marks it manual with `unified_graph_removed` (`unknown_in_flight_node` when a node step was in flight; `server_startup_recovery` in `manual` mode; in `auto` mode one already finalized as a question is skipped). That includes a run whose graph an earlier startup recovery had claimed: it is marked under that claim, where a claimed custom-Skill graph is skipped, because no process of this version can own a v2 claim. The approval endpoint refuses a run parked at its gate with 409 `unified_graph_removed` after listing the run for an operator. The operator's only action is `cancel`, which ends the run and so settles it. Over HTTP these refusals carry `{ error: message }` and no code. A run finalized as a question stays `waiting_for_user` for good (the answer starts a new run; `auto` recovery never lists it and retention deletes only ended runs): older behaviour, left alone. Stop the old version before starting this one: startup recovery does not check whether an old-version process still runs a v3 run (read from the code; rolling deploys were not tested).
  - **Tests:** `test/agent-stored-unified-graph-runs.test.mjs` seeds an in-memory run store from `test/fixtures/stored-unified-graph-runs.json` (run-store records the v3 code wrote: eight states and one parked background task at 21eae0c6, and four more states written by the same code after the independent review found them missing; the script that wrote them existed only in the freeze commit 0ce3d23c) and pins each path with literals, the last test through the built app over HTTP. `test/agent-unified-graph-dial.test.mjs` pins what a new request does under each value of the dial. Both were written before the removal: their diff in the removal commit is the list of what changed.
  - **Phase 11 (2026-11-15) removed what only that graph used in the shared graph modules**, none of which had a production caller left: approval parking (`interrupt()`) and same-thread continuation in the LangGraph executor (`unified-graph-approval.js`, `-resume.js`, `-thread.js` are gone), the `v3` graph contract (now refused as an unknown version), the v2 checkpoint (it can no longer be created, sealed, verified or reconciled; `isExecutionGraphCheckpointIntact` makes every run-store fence refuse a stored one cleanly), the `graph_node` step identity (a node step is always `custom_skill:<nodeId>`), `awaiting_approval` / `approval_denied` / `hold`, the node preflight hook and the node's Capability registry and services, `replayExecutionGraphRun`, the run store's graph approval gate, `capabilities/graph-contract.js`, `graph-approval-preflight.js` and the Capabilities' `executionGraph` blocks, the built-in `document_evidence_check` Skill, the DAG planner adapter's `version` branch, and the task-level `deny` action (it could deny only a graph approval gate; now 400 as an unknown action). Do not add any of it back. What stays for stored runs: `rag/agent-removed-unified-graph.js`, the replay guard's `graph_node` / `agent_graph_node:` markers, and `verifyExecutionGraphCheckpoint` refusing any checkpoint that holds a `v3` graph. Phase 12 removed the built-in Skills' graph contract too (see below); `document_rag` still refuses a RAG result whose evidence fields have the wrong type (`assertDocumentRagResultTypes`, same messages), so a malformed result fails the Skill call.
  - **Phase 12 (2026-11-16) closed what phase 11 left** (user: "继续做该做的"; ADR 0001 phase 12). None of its nine items changes a request's answer; option B, done after it, does (below). `runAgentRag` takes no approvals (above). `rag/langgraph/tools.js`, the one piece of code that could call a custom Skill outside its graph node (never wired into production), is deleted. The built-in Skills `document_rag`, `web_search`, `inventory` and `document_discovery` no longer declare `plannerSummary`, `inputSchema` or `outputSchema` or return typed outputs (`evidence`, `documentCount`, `hasDocuments`, `matchedDocIds`, `hasMatches`): no graph runs a built-in Skill (the authorized catalog admits only custom Skills) and `normalizeSkillResult` already dropped them; they keep `effects`, `idempotency`, `parallelSafe`, `replaySafe` and `retryable`. Only a `running` run has a LangGraph writer (`assertLangGraphCheckpointWriter`; the phase-0 spike also admitted `waiting_for_user` so an approval could resume a thread, which nothing does any more). The replanner asks the model only while some selected Skill could still be paid for (`hasSpendableBudget`: its budget key is above 0, or it has no key or one the caller does not report; with no resolvable selected Skill, any call key above 0), otherwise it abstains with `replan_budget_exhausted` ("No skill budget remains.") without the call. The deterministic DAG planner wrapped by the rollout or shadow wrapper is recognised by its `createExecutionGraph` (`isFallbackPlanner`), so under `AGENT_PLANNER_ROLLOUT=deterministic|shadow` its record says `selected` (`shadow` was removed on 2026-11-19). The observability report counts graphs rejected whole (`recovery.skillGraphRejectedCount`; not a fallback, no gate threshold). Processes that host the agent tier import the LangGraph execution-plan graph module right after they listen (`warmAgentExecutionPlanRuntime`, never rejects). New failing-case tests pin both executors' receipt checks and every reconcile guard of the custom-Skill graph checkpoint (eight guards could be removed undetected before). Then, by the user's choice ("改成 B"), a failed Skill that feeds the next one only optional inputs no longer blocks it (see the custom skill stage bullet above).
- A background task's approval of a Capability gate (`agent-tasks.js` `approve`, the task card's Approve) decides the gate on the paused run through the run's own approval path, the one the chat page uses: `createAgentTaskIterationRunner` (`agent-tasks.js`, wired in `app-services.js`) sends an iteration that carries a gate approval to the step executor's `applyApprovalAction` (binding checked against the pending gate, gate approved, the approved Capability run as its queued `capability_call` step, run completed) and every other iteration to the agent. Nothing is planned again, so steps that completed before the gate (a document answer, a custom-Skill graph; the replay guard allows a gate created after the graph's terminal event) are not entered twice. A gate that cannot be decided any more (run ended, stale binding) is refused and fails the task with that reason. Until 2026-11-16 the task re-entered the run with the approval and planned it again, which failed on every such run. The agent awaits every completion inside its `try`, so a refused completion ends the run failed rather than leaving it `running`. A request carries no approvals: `runAgentRag` no longer takes `capabilityApprovals` (ADR 0001 phase 12), so a gated Capability runs only after its gate is decided. A gated built-in Skill (arXiv import, workspace action, Web search, discovery) ends at its Capability call (`executeGatedCapability`, `rag/skills/built-ins.js`): the call throws the approval interrupt, or the policy refuses the input before the gate, and a call that returns anyway fails the Skill ("<capabilityId> requires approval and runs only from the approved capability_call step."). Nothing in the outer plan answers from a gated Capability's result. Once the gate is approved, the `capability_call` step answers through `buildCapabilityAnswer` (`capability-steps.js`), and its output, the run result and the response carry the same text and sources: an arXiv import answers with the importer's counts and paper list in the language of the run's goal, the imported and already indexed papers as sources, and abstains when nothing was found; a discovery answers with the matches inside the approved docIds (both in `agent-run-step-handlers/capability-answers.js`); every other Capability answers with its own text and citations, or `<label> completed.` when it has no text. ADR 0001 phase 13 (2026-11-17) deleted the outer plan's branches that ran after a gated Capability returned (only tests reached them) and `test/pre-approved-capabilities-test-helpers.mjs`. `test/agent-task-approval-reentry.test.mjs` goes end to end through the iteration runner, the real agent and the real step executor.
- A request that names a run (only a background task's `continue` does: it names the run its last iteration used) always gets a new run, linked by a `run_continued` event `{ previousRunId }`; the named run is never read, re-entered or written, whatever its state (user's choice, 2026-11-17, audit item 9; before, an open run was re-entered in place and failed with 409 once one of its fixed-id stages had completed, and a settled run already got a new run). The task memory carries the context. An open run the task left behind keeps its state, including a pending gate, which can still be approved from the chat page. A task's approval does not go through here at all (see the bullet above). Pinned by `test/agent-task-continue-settled-run.test.mjs`.
- Test isolation: startup recovery scans every tenant, so each PostgreSQL integration worker must scope `listRecoverableRuns` to its own runs.
- Skill contract metadata lives in `server/rag/skills/skill-contract.js`: `inputSchema`, `outputSchema`, `effects`, `idempotency`, `parallelSafe`, and `replaySafe` on top of the existing `id/version/label/budgetKey/requiresAccessScope/match/execute`. Skills that declare none of it get safe read-only defaults for V1 compatibility, but inferred defaults do not admit a Skill into the model-facing graph catalog. The current typed output is an answer envelope (`text`, `citations`, `abstained`), not a separately verified semantic delta/risk object. `describeSkillReplayContract()` is persisted onto every `custom_skill` step (and, with its own values, onto the outer RAG steps). Nothing replays a step from that record: the replay matrix reads it only to add reason codes for the operator (a declaration only ever adds a reason).
- Conversation memory (the session turn and the long-term preferences a message states) records only the user's own message. A preference (`reply_language`, `answer_style`) is stored only from an explicit instruction (以后/默认/后续/请…用中文/英文回答, 用中文/英文回答, "reply/answer/respond in Chinese/English", "keep/make answers concise/short/brief/detailed", "be more detailed", 简短一点/详细一点…), one row per key per message; a question that only mentions a language or a length ("the reply from the Chinese supplier", "short-term borrowings") stores nothing (`rag/long-memory.js`; rows stored by the earlier bare-word patterns stay until deleted through `DELETE /memory/:memoryId`). `chat()` in `rag/index.js` takes `memoryWrites` (default `true`); `false` skips `recordSessionTurn` and `recordLongMemoryFromUserMessage` but keeps every memory read (query rewrite, preference block). Both writes go through `recordUserExchange`; a failed session write is logged and never fails an answer already generated.
  - **Writes:** each agent request, once, after the run finishes: `runAgentRag` (`rag/agent.js`) passes the user's question and the text they are given (`body.agentAnswer`: the finalized answer, a follow-up answer that replaced the primary one, or the clarification or abstention) to `ragService.recordConversationTurn` (one user turn plus one assistant turn, plus the stated preferences; `routeMode` = `body.agentMode`; for an answer the resolved retrieval question is `body.ragResolvedQuestion`, the primary `document_rag` call's rewrite of the user's question, also when a follow-up answer replaced the primary one (the follow-up asks that resolved question and is not rewritten, so its result reports the same value); a clarification keeps none), only for a 2xx response, a failure logged. Canceled, outage-ended and error responses write nothing; so do an approval answer and a startup-recovered run. Direct `chat()` callers (default `memoryWrites: true`) still record their own exchange.
  - **Never writes** (`memoryWrites: false`): every `chat()` the agent makes: the primary `document_rag` call, the V1 follow-up retrieval (it asks the primary call's resolved question), every `research_question` sub-call, the retries of these steps, and the composed prompts of the four custom Skills and `document.compare_batch`. Pass `false` from any new agent call.
  - **Background tasks:** an iteration whose question is not the task's own `payload.question` (a workflow phase question, the agent's planned `nextQuestion`) runs under `runWithoutConversationMemoryWrites` (`server/rag/conversation-memory-writes.js`, an `AsyncLocalStorage` flag `recordUserExchange` checks), so nothing inside it writes memory (the end-of-request recording included) while reads are unchanged.
  - **Tests:** `test/rag.test.mjs` (real `chat()` with in-memory session and long-term stores: direct call, a `/chat` with a follow-up, a refused claim, a follow-up answer that replaces the primary one, a research brief, a failing session store, the suppression flag), `test/agent-tasks.test.mjs` (which iterations are suppressed), `test/agent-run-step-executor.test.mjs` (retries).
- The outer `document_rag`, `follow_up_retrieval`, and built-in `research_question` steps call `ragService.chat` with live session/user identity. None of them writes conversation memory (the agent request records the exchange once, see above), but all three V1 step inputs still persist `effects: workspace_write`, `idempotency: adapter_defined`, and `replaySafe: false`: nothing proves an injected `ragService` or legacy document Skill honours `memoryWrites`, so these steps are not shown to write nothing. Startup recovery replays no step on its own (see "Phase 9" below), so an interrupted one goes to an operator (unless `AGENT_RUN_RECOVERY_MODE=off`, which skips agent-run startup recovery and leaves the run `running`), older steps without metadata included. Explicit `retry_failed_step` remains available and writes no memory (a request that failed was never recorded, and its retry does not record it). Do not describe these steps as read-only RAG or use them as auto-recovery-safe fixtures.
- Graph observability is an agent run event (`type: "skill_graph_planned"` via `recordExecutionGraph`), not a `/chat` response field. Keep `graph`, `nodeRuns`, and `replans` as versioned additive fields on run events so the `/chat`, `agentObservability`, frontend trace, feedback, approval, recovery, and evaluation report contracts stay backward compatible.
- Guarded graph checkpoints live privately in the existing agent run store (`server/rag/agent-execution-graph-checkpoint.js`), not in the public run projection or a new store. Resume must verify owner/digest, current registry contract, persisted steps, and already-charged budget before reusing completed nodes; unknown in-flight work and mismatches require manual recovery. This includes an in-flight `readOnly` Skill: storage alone cannot prove its former worker has stopped. Startup auto recovery may use the dedicated `resumeAgentExecutionGraphRun()` path only at a node boundary, for a stored outer plan containing exactly `custom_skills`, no pending approval, and a reconciled guarded checkpoint. It must claim that checkpoint once and must not regenerate Intent or the outer execution plan. An ordinary `runAgentRag` re-entry must reject any run with a graph checkpoint in the same run-revision CAS as its snapshot update, even if the request and owner match; only the dedicated recovery path may resume it. The claim, guarded node start, and run completion/failure must also fence stale workers through run-revision CAS. A second worker must skip an already-claimed graph without mutating its status; an abandoned claim is never replayed automatically and requires operator investigation.
- Generic single-step retry (`agent-run-step-executor.js`; there is no single-step resume since phase 9) must reject every `custom_skill` step, and any other graph node, before changing run or step state. The pure graph replay guard is also enforced inside the run store CAS for standalone retry, approval, and step transitions, closing preflight-to-mutation races. A post-graph outer step or approval is allowed only when its durable event proves it was created after the matching terminal graph event. Graph node start and settlement both carry the graph recovery claim; do not turn either into an unfenced generic step transition. Do not add a handler, a recovery action or a startup branch that runs a custom Skill outside its graph (phase 9 removed the last one).
- Agent planning and skill-chain selection live in `server/rag/agent-planner.js`; keep request classification, planner actions, chain ordering, and pre-execution clarification checks there rather than adding them back to `server/rag/agent.js`.
- Agent trace formatting lives in `server/rag/agent-trace.js`; keep trace step summaries and compact trace serialization there when adding new trace step types.
- Planner structured outputs: intent, execution, and DAG planner calls send a per-request strict JSON Schema `response_format` built from the runtime whitelist (`server/rag/structured-output.js` helpers, builders next to each planner prompt). Keep every free-text field bounded with `pattern` and every array with `maxItems` (an unbounded rationale ran to thousands of tokens under constrained decoding); strict mode documents `pattern`, not `maxLength`. Use `boundedString` (`^[^"\\\x00-\x1F]{0,N}$`): Ollama applies a string pattern to the raw JSON text, where `.` let a bare quote or a backslash break the reply (the claim judge's 21 unparsable replies of 137, docs/evaluation.md "评审输出格式修正"), and JSON forbids a raw control character in a string, so a bounded value holds none of them (a line break cannot be escaped without the backslash). The schema only narrows output: validators stay the final authority, parsers keep the first complete JSON value because some servers do not hard-enforce the schema, and `RAG_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_ENABLED=false` falls back to prompt-only JSON.
- Planner failure reasons: the intent, execution and DAG planners' `fallbackReason` and the replanner's `reason` go out in the `/chat` response (`agentObservability`) and the run record, so all four come from `serializePlannerError` (`agent-intent-validator.js`). The planner's own parse and validation errors keep their message (the goldens and evals pin those); a failed model call (an error with a `code` or `status`: a timeout, an HTTP or gateway error, the circuit breaker) is `planner_call_failed`, never its message, which can carry the upstream reply or name the model endpoint, and the server log gets the error's name and code once per distinct failure (since 2026-11-20, as the claim judge's `judge.error` below). Every other error text a client sees follows the rule below.
- Client-visible error text (since 2026-11-21): one rule, `serializeError` in `server/lib/error-text.js`, for a route's `{ error }` answer (`routes/helpers.js` re-exports it) and every error text in the agent's payloads: `/chat` and `/chat/stream` `errors.rag` / `errors.mcp` and `ragAnswer` / `mcpAnswer`, trace and run step summaries and error records, `agentSkills` errors, `run.error` (`failRun`), recovery events, task records (`job-orchestrator.js`), goal deliverables, research findings, experience-memory errors, arXiv per-paper and import-task errors, recommendation tasks and the archive MCP tools. An Error keeps its message only when it was raised for the client: status 4xx or `expose: true`, and never an upstream answer (`upstreamStatus` set, `isUpstreamError`). Anything else (no status: a database or file system error naming a host or a path; a 5xx; an upstream error; a value that is not an Error) is the caller's fallback text and is logged with `console.error`, once per error object. App errors written for the user whatever their status carry `expose: true`: budget refusals and skipped graph nodes (`buildSkippedSkillResult`, `skills/registry.js`), the Skills a rejected graph leaves unrun (`agent-custom-skill-stage.js`), `RequestCancelledError`, `DependencyOutageError`; an arXiv topic with no searchable term is a 400 (`arxiv-client.js`). Text and `{ message, name }` records are public already where they were built (a research finding's error, a step error record), so the run store and step builders pass them through. Not folded in, being stricter for their callers: `answerUnhandledError` (`expose` only: a non-expose 4xx that reaches Express's last middleware answers 500) and `toIngestJobErrorMessage` (`expose` or 413/415/422 only, never an upstream answer, and a string as is); the planner and the claim judge keep their fixed codes.
- Claim judge (`server/rag/self-check/claim-judge.js`, `RAG_CLAIM_JUDGE`, default `llm` since 2026-11-19, `off` keeps the lexical check only; docs/evaluation.md "语义核对": with source inheritance it took the agent from 2/176 to 39/176 answerable questions on QASPER, qwen2.5:7b): claims the lexical check rejects get one batched LLM verdict per answer, in the document loop self-check (`evaluateDocumentEvidenceWithJudge`) and the document-mode finalizer only. The judge may only upgrade unsupported to supported; never send it claims with missing/ambiguous/misattributed sources, claims whose numbers are absent from their cited evidence, or comparison answers; any failure keeps the lexical verdict. The `judge` summary goes out in the `/chat` trace (`claimSupport.judge` of the self-check and finalizer steps), so a failure is a stable `error` code next to the counts (`call_failed` when the call threw, `no_verdict` when the reply held none), never the reply (it can quote the documents) or an upstream message; the server log gets one warning per distinct failure with the error's name and code only. Its response schema needs one verdict per claim (`minItems`) and a `boundedString` reason of at most 160 characters (no quote or backslash, see the line above; docs/evaluation.md "评审输出格式修正"). `npm run eval:claim-judge` is the calibration gate (constructed contrast set plus held-out items; count an item as accepted only if every claim it splits into is supported).
- Single-document QA abstention: the lexical gate (`assessQaConfidence` in `server/rag/confidence.js`) admits a chunk at or above `RAG_MIN_QA_QUERY_TERM_COVERAGE`. Since 2026-11-17 the tokenizer (`rag/text-utils.js`) reads every script (Han one character per token, other scripts as runs of letters, marks and digits); before, a question in Cyrillic, Greek, Arabic, Hangul or kana had no terms and was admitted unread. There is no stemming or stop-word list outside English, so a chunk worded in another inflection (договор / договора, 계약 / 계약을) can fall below the threshold and abstain. The answer model's own abstention went on 2026-11-20 (user: "清理这 4 个"): `RAG_QA_ANSWER_VERDICT` asked it to open with `NOT_IN_EVIDENCE:` when the evidence does not answer (`qa_answer` v1.3 / v2.3) and opened a partial coverage band (`RAG_QA_PARTIAL_COVERAGE_FLOOR`) in the gate, `RAG_QA_VERDICT_RETRY_TOP_K` retried such a reply once with deeper retrieval, and `RAG_QA_VERDICT_OVERRIDE=supported` answered it anyway when a deterministic re-check proved its cited claims. Every round failed its pre-declared rule (QASPER dev, qwen2.5:7b: unanswerable questions caught 85% -> 70% at flat answer F1; the answer-rate rounds below), so the flags, the band, the retry and `answer-verdict-override.js` are gone and a stale setting is ignored (docs/evaluation.md "两段式拒答", "拒答标记覆盖"). Two pieces stay: `findQueryTermSubstitution` (`selectQaContext` keeps a chunk that swaps a query word for a rival out of the model's context), and answer drafts never stream a reply that opens with the marker (`startsWithNotInEvidenceVerdict`, `rag/answer-verdict.js`), as before the deletion.
- Cross-language retrieval (`server/rag/query-translation.js`, 2026-11-20, no setting; docs/evaluation.md "事实题检索条数对齐与跨语言检索"): a Chinese question over English documents found nothing, since its Han terms never occur in English chunks and nomic-embed-text does not bridge the two. `chat()` takes the question's script and each selected document's script from its profile summary (`detectScript`: the most frequent script by words among Han, kana, Hangul, Cyrillic, Latin, Greek, Arabic, Hebrew, Thai and Devanagari, a word being a Han letter or a run of letters of one other script, as the tokenizer splits terms, so "BERT模型在SQuAD上的F1是多少？" is Han (counted by letters it was Latin and never rewritten; Thai, written without spaces, counts a phrase as one word); Han counts as kana, else as Hangul, when those make up at least a fifth of the Han and kana or Hangul together, so a Chinese text quoting one Japanese or Korean word stays Han; no letter, no script; an empty summary, no script). For every document script that differs from the question's (at most 3, in selected-document order) it asks the chat model, in parallel, to translate `resolvedQuery` into that script's language, named in the prompt (`SCRIPT_LANGUAGES`: Latin reads "a language written in Latin letters (such as English)", Cyrillic "(such as Russian)", Hangul "Korean" and so on; asked only for "the language of the example text", qwen2.5:7b rewrote Chinese questions back into Chinese, KX round ML2), with the first such document's summary as the excerpt (`query_translation@v2`, `completeText` like the session rewrite, the summary screened by `screenUntrustedText`; the reply goes through `sanitizeRewriteQueryText`, which keeps its last line and drops one longer than a question may be (`CHAT_MAX_QUESTION_CHARS`; the session rewrite's 500 characters cut off a translation out of Chinese, about four times as long), and is kept only when that line is in the target script, not counting Latin letters for a target in another script since the prompt keeps names unchanged). Each rewrite is an extra non-primary retrieval query `translation-<script>` appended to the planned ones in `buildRetrievalInputs`, on the RAG-only and the agent path alike (remote retrieval carries it like any other query); the agent plan object, and so the semantic cache key, is not changed (a rewrite follows from the question and the documents, already in the key). The gate keeps reading each candidate's stored `keywordScore`, so a chunk the rewrite found is measured on the rewrite's terms, and since a translation is a paraphrase such a candidate (its winning copy's query is `translation-*`) may also clear the lexical floor on its dense score, the bypass comparisons use (`assessQaConfidence` `allowSemanticBypassFor`, built by `buildTranslationBypass`, `getMinRelevanceScore()`; anchors still apply; KX round X3), with no rival-word check against the rewrite, so on nomic (every dense score clears 0.32) such a chunk is admitted on anchors alone, even one about a neighbouring topic of the rewrite ("annual leave" for "parental leave") and, in a selection mixing scripts, a same-script chunk whose rewrite copy won the merge (a `shortcut:` in `buildTranslationBypass`; the veto was measured as X3' and not kept under its rules): a rewrite is never shown or answered, but it is the yardstick for the chunks it finds, so an injection in the example summary that got past the screen could choose which of that document's chunks pass. `resolvedQuery` (gate anchors, answer prompt, session turn) stays the user's, so a phrase quoted in the question's script (员工有多少天“带薪年假”？) stays an anchor that chunks in another script lack and the question abstains (a `shortcut:` in `chat()`). The RAG trace gets `queryTranslations: [{ script, query }]` (`query` null when the call failed or the reply was unusable; the question is then searched alone) only when a rewrite was asked for, so same-script traces are unchanged. The agent's follow-up phase gets none (a `shortcut:` in `chat()`: its gate reads coverage from the question text). Script, not language (a `shortcut:` in the module): a French question over English documents is not rewritten. Tests: `test/query-translation.test.mjs`.
- The QA gate decides whether to answer; it no longer decides everything the model reads. Once `assessQaConfidence` is confident, `executeQaRag` builds the context with `selectQaContext` (`server/rag/confidence.js`): the admitted chunks first, then further candidates in retrieval order up to `RAG_RETRIEVAL_TOP_K`, each clearing the relevance fallback floor, naming one of the question's anchors when it has any, and not replacing a query word with a rival (`findQueryTermSubstitution`); `QA_CONTEXT_MIN_COVERAGE` is 0 because a reworded evidence paragraph often shares no query term. Admitted chunks are never dropped, abstentions are unchanged, and the trace records `contextExtraCount`. Keep the substitution veto on the extras: without it the "amber ceiling" behaviour test cites the cobalt clause.
- Dense rescue on the QA gate (`RAG_QA_DENSE_RESCUE`, 2026-11-12, deleted 2026-11-20): a chunk below the QA coverage floor was admitted on its dense cosine (`absolute`) or on its margin over the mean cosine of the question's dense candidates (`margin`), under the partial band's rival-word veto. The pre-declared round on nomic-embed-text (docs/evaluation.md "门控换信号") failed at the dev confirmation: the train pick (`absolute`, 0.7737, floor 0) passed 25 more of 800 answerable questions (+0.031 [+0.020, +0.044]), but on dev the gate also let 3 more of the 95 unanswerable ones through (caught 56 -> 53, −0.032), over the declared 0.03. The getter, the gate branch, the report fields and the analysis's rescue section went with it; a stale setting is ignored.
- Query expansion on the QA route's own retrieval (`RAG_QA_QUERY_EXPANSION=multi_query|hyde`, 2026-11-12, deleted 2026-11-20): one chat call before retrieval rewrote the question as other search queries fused with its own list by reciprocal rank (`qa_query_multi_query`), or wrote a passage whose document embedding, averaged with the question's, searched the dense route (`qa_query_hyde`). The pre-declared round on QASPER train (docs/evaluation.md "检索补漏") failed rule A for both: multi_query lost evidence among the candidates (0.805 -> 0.7875, -0.0175 [-0.031, -0.0049]; reciprocal-rank fusion over four lists ranks every chunk n + 1 lists returned above any chunk n lists returned, so evidence only the question's list found was pushed out of the top 10), hyde left it unchanged and moved evidence off rank 1 (-0.0187 [-0.037, -0.0012]), and retrieval per question went from a 13 ms mean to 659 ms (multi_query) and 785 ms (hyde). The module, both prompts, the HyDE vector, the fusion, the start check, the trace field and the report fields went with it; a stale setting is ignored. Untried: a heavier weight on the question's list, rewrites filling only the slots its list leaves, several HyDE passages, other models.
- Claim-support evidence handling (`server/rag/self-check/`): a number restated in parentheses in the other form ("twelve (12)", "5 (five)", "十二（12）") counts as one number only when both forms have the same value (`collapseParenthesizedNumberRestatements` in `text.js`); keep inconsistent pairs as two. `buildCitationSupportSentences` keeps the line-level sentences and adds sentences rejoined across soft PDF wraps (`joinWrappedLines`: previous line lacks sentence-final punctuation and the next starts lowercase); do not loosen that rule to join headings, list items, or `Label:` lines, because `comparison-equivalence.js` relies on headings staying separate to drop them as structure (the compare-hard `compare_remote_paraphrase_no_diff_hard` negative control fails otherwise). The claim side reads words as the evidence side does, so a sentence quoted as it stands passes (the "claim check symmetry" tests in `test/claim-support.test.mjs`, each with a control that changes the fact and is still refused): `stripNumericValueSurfaces` removes only a number that stands on its own, so a digit run inside a word (`BIBREF20`, `seq2seq`, `GPT2`, `2nd`, `5km`, `5ème`, `15일`; a word is a run of non-Han letters, marks and digits, as in the tokenizer) stays the one token the tokenizer makes of it on both sides; the connectives the evidence clause split consumes (`MODALITY_CLAUSE_SPLIT_PATTERN`: but, however, whereas, while, whilst, yet and the Chinese ones) are taken out of claim terms, subject binding terms and additive-detail groups first (`stripClauseConnectives` in `modality.js`), because no evidence clause keeps them; additive-detail groups are read with `extractFactTerms` (aliases included) like the support terms, not with raw tokens; `haveSameNumericOccurrences` accepts two texts that are the same once their numeric syntax is normalized (signs, operators, currencies and approximation marks kept, so `+2`/`-2` and `$500`/`€500` never are) before its ambiguity rule, which used to refuse a clause against itself; an evidence sentence is read whole, not only as its clauses, when the claim is a run of its words, not only when it equals it; and `hasReversedTermOrder` never reports a claim whose terms are a run of the evidence's terms in its order. Offline replay (QASPER train, 2,521 verbatim sentences rendered as `<sentence> [Source 1]`; docs/evaluation.md "Claim 检查两侧对称"): refused 29.9% before and 2.5% after, and 44 of the remaining 64 are the unlabelled first parts of a sentence split at `;` (the `RAG_CLAIM_SOURCE_INHERITANCE` flag's domain). Paraphrases are judged as before; the model evals were not rerun.
- Claim-check memo (`server/rag/self-check/evidence-context.js`): `evaluateClaimSupport` runs under an exact memo whose scope is one call, one `/chat` when the caller passes `evidenceContext`, or one model attempt of a streamed answer's drafts.
  - **Scope and reuse:** without `evidenceContext` each call makes its own context. The document loop (`agent-document-loop.js`) makes one per `/chat`, passes it to the primary and follow-up checks and to `selectBetterRagResult`, and binds it to the returned `ragResult` (a WeakMap). `agent-finalization-flow.js` takes it back for the finalizer's check and then releases it. With the judge off it precomputes the lexical `claimSupport` with exactly `finalizeAgentAnswer`'s arguments (trimmed text, `evidenceCitations` defaulting to the citations). The judge path uses the same context for its lexical part. The answer-draft releaser (`answer-drafts.js`) makes one per model attempt (a new one in `onAttemptStart`), so each growing prefix of a streamed answer checks only the claims it has not checked yet. It keeps only the claim checks: each prefix's whole result is dropped once its drafts are decided (`clearSelfCheckEvidenceContextTable`), because no later prefix reads it and keeping them all would grow with the square of the answer's length (about 70 MB at 400 sentences, against about 1 MB now; with no default output cap a runaway model can write that much before the request times out). A context is active only during the synchronous call (a module variable restored in `finally`) and holds at most 4096 entries per table. There is no process-wide cache. Pinned counts: a `/chat` without follow-up makes 2 calls and computes 1; with follow-up, 5 calls and 2 computed.
  - **What is cached:** pure helpers by their exact input: `normalizeNumericSyntax`, numeric occurrence and constraint extraction, `buildNumericOccurrenceFacts`, citation alias entries, support-sentence splitting, `joinWrappedLines`, citation field splitting, and `extractOrderedFactTerms`. `getClaimSupportInputId` interns the exact serialization of what every claim of a check reads (every citation field, the citations' object-identity pattern (`citations.indexOf`), `comparisonAnalysisSummary`, and the one flag `evaluate.js` reads, source inheritance) to a small id per context (table `claimSupportInput`). `buildClaimSupportContentKey(inputId, value)` keys the whole `claimSupport` result by the id and the answer text, and one claim's check (table `claimCheck`) by the id and the claim as split from the answer (text, section, section id and label, source ranks, inherited ranks). The Differences-section pairing reads neighbouring claims and is never cached. `normalizeSearchText` is cached by its exact input (`normalizeSearchTextInContext`), and the claim-only terms are built once per claim, not per evidence segment. Values are copied on the way in and on the way out. Facts and `claimSupport` use `structuredClone`, because `haveSameNumericOccurrences` writes `roleTerms` onto facts.
  - **Never cache:** under a normalized key (`normalizeNumericSyntax` is not idempotent, so a caller's second pass must still run); an input that cannot be serialized exactly (a Map, a class instance, a getter, a Symbol key or a cycle computes directly); anything across requests or tenants; or anything the claim judge (LLM) decides, which keeps its own verdict cache. A new configuration read inside the claim check must join the content key. `test/self-check-memo.test.mjs` fails otherwise: it replays every `evaluateClaimSupport` call of the claim-support and answer-draft suites under fresh, repeated and shared contexts and requires `deepStrictEqual` with the memo disabled. It wraps each imported suite in its own `describe` so Node 20/22 wait for the suite's tests.
  - **Not yet on the per-`/chat` context:** `agent-answer-verification.js`, `answer-writer.js`, and `capabilities/citation.js`.
  - **Effect:** on the monolith load test, app CPU per `/chat` fell from about 12–13 ms to about 5 ms and throughput rose about 2.6x at concurrency 32. Parity was byte-identical after masking ids and timings. See docs/evaluation.md "Claim 检查 memo".
- Comparison answer gate (`assessStructuredDifferenceAnswer` in `server/rag/answer-writer.js`). The model's comparison answer is used only when all of these hold:
  - it is not empty;
  - `splitAnswerStructure` (`self-check/claims.js`) finds a heading in the `differences` section. `Differences:`, `## Differences`, `**Differences**` and `差异：` all count. `**Differences**:` (colon outside the bold) does not yet, because `normalizeStructuralClaimLabel` in `self-check/text.js` checks the bold markers before it strips the colon;
  - `evaluateClaimSupport` checked it and found no unsupported claim anywhere in the answer, not only under Differences;
  - the supported Differences claims cite every selected document.
  Otherwise the grounded difference template replaces it when that template passes the same gate, and the answer abstains when it does not.
  - **Reason:** recorded only in the RAG trace as `modelRejectReason`. It holds a `code` (`empty_answer`, `no_differences_section`, `claims_not_checked`, `unsupported_claims`, `differences_not_bound_to_every_document`), the counts `claimCount` / `differenceClaimCount` / `unsupportedClaimCount` (plus `documentCount` / `coveredDocumentCount` for the last code), and `fallback` (`grounded_template` or `abstained`). `executeComparisonRag` moves it into `traceFields`. Never add answer or evidence text to it, and keep it out of the RAG response; `/chat` `agentObservability` and MCP `formatAskResult` do not carry it. The near-duplicate short circuit never calls the model and records no reason.
  - **Fallback evidence:** the templates quote evidence through `buildDocEvidenceEntries`. `joinEvidenceSoftWraps` first sets bare heading lines apart, then rejoins soft PDF wraps with the shared `joinWrappedLines`, so a heading is never glued onto the lowercase sentence under it (the shared helper alone would do that). Bare headings are then dropped (`isBareHeadingSentence`): a Markdown heading, a structural section heading, or at most 12 Title Case words with no sentence-final punctuation and no claim predicate. A number counts as a heading word only as a leading enumerator, so a table row such as "Notice Period 30 Days" stays as evidence. Chinese lines are never dropped. Title Case fact lines without a number or predicate ("Full-Time Staff Only") are still dropped. Tests: `test/comparison-answer-gate.test.mjs`, and the trace/response split at the end of `test/rag.test.mjs`.
  - **Measured (2026-10-04, qwen2.5:7b):** every model comparison answer that reached the gate was rejected as `unsupported_claims` with every claim unsupported. That was 1 in `verify:quality` and 5 in a real-model compare-hard run. The causes were uncited Summary/Agreements prose and paraphrased difference values. Five of the six fell back to the template and one abstained. This is the lexical claim check, not a gate defect, so header spelling is not the bottleneck for this model.
  - **Structured output** (`RAG_COMPARISON_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT`, 2026-10-21, deleted 2026-11-19): the model returned aspects with verbatim quotes and the server rendered them as the template's `<document> states <sentence> [Source N]` lines. On qwen2.5:7b the gate accepted 12/16 model answers instead of 0/16, but the user-visible checks were identical both ways (`verify:quality` 18/18, compare-hard 6/8) and no stronger model was ever measured, so the mode, its prompts and its quote resolution were deleted; a stale setting is ignored and the comparison call sends the prose prompt above (docs/evaluation.md "对比答案闸门的拒绝原因").
- Answer-rate flags (all default off except `RAG_CLAIM_SOURCE_INHERITANCE`, on since 2026-11-19; `AGENT_FOLLOW_UP_ORIGINAL_QUESTION` and `AGENT_SINGLE_DOCUMENT_ROUTING` are no longer flags, their behaviour became the only one on 2026-11-19, and `RAG_QA_GATE_INFLECTION`, `RAG_CLAIM_INFLECTION` and `RAG_CLAIM_HEADING_CONTEXT` were deleted on 2026-11-20, see the bullets below; `describeAnswerRateFlags()` in `rag/config.js` reports the two that remain, `claimJudgeTemperature` and `claimSourceInheritance`, and the QASPER/answer-draft eval reports record them as `config.answerRateFlags`). Measured on QASPER train (50 questions, seed 2, `--surface agent`, qwen2.5:7b, docs/evaluation.md "Agent 答题率开关"): with the claim judge off, no flag alone or all seven together answered a single extra answerable question (0/41 in every arm), and the only arms that answered more (judge on, alone or with all flags) also answered unanswerable questions (2/9 and 4/9 vs 0/9), so no default was changed. A second pre-declared round added `RAG_QA_ANSWER_VERDICT=true` to the judge (with and without the seven flags): it kept unanswerable answers at 0/3 on train seed 3, but on dev seed 1 the selected arm (judge + verdict + all seven flags) answered 2/9 unanswerable vs 0/9, over the baseline + 1 limit, so it failed and still nothing changed; dev seed 1 (100 questions) is now used. A third pre-declared round added `RAG_QA_VERDICT_OVERRIDE=supported` (the deterministic re-check of a `NOT_IN_EVIDENCE:` reply described under "Single-document QA abstention") to that arm and failed stage 1 on train seed 4 (50 questions): answer rate 0.023 to 0.302, but 1/7 unanswerable answered vs 0/7, through the ordinary answer path and not the override, which answered none of its 42 decisions because the 7B marker replies mostly held no cited factual claim; dev and guardrails were not run and every flag, the override included, stayed off (docs/evaluation.md "第三轮：拒答标记的确定性复核"). On 2026-11-20 (user: "清理这 4 个") the verdict, its override and the three flags below that never answered an extra question were deleted. Do not flip one without a new pre-declared train run and a confirmation on a fresh dev sample.
  - Follow-up question (no flag since 2026-11-19; docs/evaluation.md "补检索问原问题还是删掉": the "Re-check the uploaded documents…" meta text resolved 0 of 187 follow-ups, asking the question resolved 8 of 50 and answered 8 more answerable questions with no extra unanswerable one): the follow-up retrieval asks the primary call's `resolvedQuery` (else the question); unsupported-claim text stays only in the follow-up retrieval plan's repair queries; the follow-up call skips session-memory rewriting (`memoryApplied: false`); its QA gate scores candidates by the question text (`confidence.coverageBasis.fromQueryText`). The follow-up answer still goes through the same claim check.
  - `RAG_QA_GATE_INFLECTION` and `RAG_CLAIM_INFLECTION` (2026-10-03, deleted 2026-11-20): inflected forms (`rag/inflection.js`: -s/-es/-ies/-ed/-ing; number words and a short list of homographs exact) counted in the QA gate's coverage and in the lexical claim check. Alone each answered 0/41 answerable questions, as the baseline did (the gate one let 38 questions reach the answer model instead of 31), and the arms that carried both failed rounds 2 and 3. `rag/inflection.js`, the getters and the report fields (`qaGateInflection`, `claimInflection`, `config.retrieval.qaGateInflection`) went with them; a stale setting is ignored, and the follow-up's `confidence.coverageBasis` keeps `inflection: false` so its trace does not change.
  - `RAG_CLAIM_HEADING_CONTEXT` (2026-10-03, deleted 2026-11-20): the claim check also read each cited sentence joined to its section heading, so a claim naming its section could pass. Alone it answered 0/41 answerable questions, and the arms that carried it failed rounds 2 and 3. The heading-scoped sentences in `self-check/attribution.js`, the getter and `claimHeadingContext` went with it (the QASPER agent rows no longer copy a source's `sectionHeading`, which the check no longer reads); a stale setting is ignored.
  - `RAG_CLAIM_SOURCE_INHERITANCE`: an unlabelled answer sentence takes the labels of the nearest labelled sentence on the same line (after, then before; never across lines), marked `sourceRanksInherited: true`; inherited labels are checked exactly like written ones and the finalizer writes out the ones that pass. Default on since 2026-11-19 (`false` turns it off). The trajectory fixtures keep an unlabelled sentence on its own line so their cases still carry two gaps, and the trajectory eval pins `RAG_CLAIM_JUDGE=off` (it has no model); the LangGraph goldens (`test/langgraph-execution-plan-graph/mask.mjs` `ANSWER_RATE_FLAGS_OFF`), frozen with the lexical check only, pin both off.
  - `RAG_CLAIM_JUDGE_TEMPERATURE` (unset = no temperature sent): sends a fixed temperature (0–2) with judge requests and adds it to the verdict cache key; `completeTextWithMetadata` passes `options.temperature` through the chat client and the model gateway (which accepts only 0–2).
  - Single-document routing (no flag since 2026-11-19; docs/evaluation.md "单文档路由成为唯一行为"): the intent rules (`narrowSingleDocumentSignals` in `agent-intent-rules.js`, the candidates both the deterministic and the LLM intent planner choose from) stop routing a one-document question to `compare_documents` / `extract_timeline` / `research_brief` on incidental wording: comparison words with no second document named, "sequence" inside a hyphenated name, "this/the/our study", and a question about the one selected document (ends in ? or ？, not "can/could/would/will you") is not a research-brief request. Real comparison and timeline requests, other-document wording (the other/another/both/these/two documents, previous version, 两份, 另一), imperatives and two or more selected documents keep their routes and clarifications. On QASPER the old wording rules stopped 166 train and 57 dev questions as comparisons and sent 81 and 30 to a research brief.
  - `RAG_QA_EXTRACTIVE_OUTPUT` (2026-10-22, deleted 2026-11-19): single-document QA as JSON, a short answer plus verbatim quotes the server rendered as `<sentence> [Source N]` lines, with a prose fallback. All three pre-declared rounds on QASPER train failed (docs/evaluation.md "抽取式作答模式"): the rag surface's `f1Direct` never rose significantly, and the agent surface answered unanswerable questions in every round (3/12, 2/14, 1/14 against 0) with cited evidence recall when answered under 0.4 after round 1. The getter, its `qaExtractiveOutput` report field, the `qa_answer_extractive` templates, the writer and the `directAnswer` / `ragDirectAnswer` fields went with it; a stale setting is ignored and QA always sends the prose template. `f1Direct` stays, scored on the first sentence.
  - Tests: `test/answer-rate-flags.test.mjs` pins each remaining flag's on/off behaviour, the single-document routing as plain behaviour, and the exact-word reading the deleted flags widened (an inflected form or a section heading supports nothing); `trajectory-eval.test.mjs` clears every flag because `RAG_CLAIM_SOURCE_INHERITANCE` changes pinned response projections. `evaluation/agent-answer-outcome.js` (`classifyAgentAnswer`) decides `answered` for eval rows: a clarification, the grounded abstention, an empty answer, or document RAG's own abstention passed on as the answer (`ragAbstained` with the answer being `ragAbstainReason`, abstain source `rag_abstention`; the QA gate's gap-plan message, in any language) is not an answer.
- Model output source labels are normalized as the text arrives (`normalizeGroupedSourceLabels` in `answer-writer.js`), so `[Source 1, Source 3]` reaches self-check, finalizer, and citation projection as one label per bracket. Variant file names such as `vendor-a.pdf` count as document labels in claim attribution (`self-check/attribution.js`); keep the topical-file-name protection and never add a shared stem as an alias of sibling documents.
- LLM call resilience lives in `server/rag/openai.js` / `openai-client.js`: jittered exponential backoff, `retry-after-ms` / `Retry-After` as a floor, `RAG_LLM_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` timeouts retried as transient, one retry for an empty completion, and failover to `OPENAI_CHAT_FALLBACK_MODEL` only through the model registry (so workspace policy applies) and only for transient errors. Each model tried is metered as its own LLMOps event and the returned `modelRoute` names the model that answered. Every HTTP request to a model (chat and embeddings, including each retry) also passes `server/rag/model-call-guard.js`: a per-endpoint-and-model concurrency cap (`RAG_LLM_MAX_CONCURRENCY`) and a consecutive-failure circuit breaker (`RAG_LLM_CIRCUIT_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` / `RAG_LLM_CIRCUIT_COOLDOWN_MS`). Only unavailable errors (5xx, 408, timeouts, connection failures) count toward the breaker, never 429 or other 4xx. An open circuit throws `CIRCUIT_OPEN` (503), which is never retried on the same model but is failover-eligible. Guard state is per process by default; the resilience eval resets it before each scenario. With `RAG_SHARED_STATE=redis` (`server/rag/shared-state.js`, `REDIS_URL`, `RAG_SHARED_STATE_PREFIX`) the breaker, the concurrency cap and the claim-judge verdict cache are shared across instances: each read-modify-write is one Lua script, the half-open probe and every concurrency slot are leases so a crashed instance cannot hold them forever, and judge cache keys include the judge prompt fingerprint and chat model. Redis is never a request dependency: every shared call falls back to per-process state on a Redis error, and the first calls wait (once, at most 2 s) for the initial connection so startup does not silently run unshared. `test/shared-state.integration.test.mjs` needs `REDIS_TEST_URL` and is skipped without it; `npm run eval:shared-state` compares memory and Redis across 4 instance processes.
- Prompt-injection defenses (`server/rag/prompt-injection-screen.js`): retrieved chunk text, upload file names, and web search results pass `screenUntrustedText` before they enter a prompt (the answer writer's context sections, the claim judge's sources, `chat-mcp.js` search results); citations shown to the user keep the original text, and RAG responses carry a rule-id-only `injectionScreen` summary. Answer, web-answer, and judge prompts carry an untrusted-evidence rule, and `guardAnswerLinks` removes markdown images and sentences whose link the model was never shown (drafts included). Planners never see document text; keep it that way. `test/prompt-injection-screen.test.mjs` requires zero flagged sentences across the benign evaluation corpora, so a new pattern must not remove real document facts; `RAG_INJECTION_SCREEN=off` is only a kill switch. The screen is pattern-based and a deliberate paraphrase (`evasion_paraphrase`) still gets through; do not describe it as complete protection.
- LangGraph runtime migration (`docs/adr/0001-langgraph-runtime.md`; phases 0–4 done; the ADR's phase 5 section records the close-out against the goal's definition of done, including what is deliberately not done and why; phase 6 deleted the native graph scheduler; phase 7 deleted the native outer loop; phase 8 deleted the V1 sequential Skill chain; phase 9 deleted the standalone replay of a custom Skill step; phase 10 deleted the v3 unified graph). The agent has one runtime and one way to answer a request: LangGraph schedules the V1 outer plan (no checkpointer; its durability is the run's steps), the `/chat/stream` relay, and the one graph that needs durable state, the custom-Skill DAG. No dial selects another scheduler, executor or way of answering a request: `AGENT_RUNTIME`, `AGENT_OUTER_PLAN_RUNTIME`, `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` and `AGENT_UNIFIED_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` are only checked for removed values. That is the owner's rule (2026-11-13: keep only the LangGraph agent workflow, no second operating mode; 2026-11-14: that includes the v3 unified graph, which ran on LangGraph too but was a second way to answer a request, removed in phase 10), so do not add a native scheduler, a sequential chain, a whole-request graph beside the outer plan, a runtime or rollout dial for them, or a second route branch back; the planner dial (`AGENT_PLANNER_ROLLOUT=llm|deterministic`) is a different matter and stays. Pins are exact and `test/langchain-dependency-pins.test.mjs` requires one `@langchain/core` and one `@langchain/langgraph-checkpoint` copy in the lock. Cancellation is a drain (`RunControl.requestDrain`), never a request signal handed to LangGraph, because a raw `signal` re-runs the in-flight node (`test/langgraph-checkpointer.test.mjs` pins that LangGraph behaviour).
  - **Native graph scheduler removed (phase 6):** the user ended ADR constraint 12's rollback window early, which waives that half of the constraint (it kept `AGENT_RUNTIME=native` for at least one release on the new default; the branch had not been pushed or released, so the window had not started). `AGENT_RUNTIME=native` no longer exists. Removed: the native scheduling loop `runExecutionGraph` (`agent-execution-graph-runner.js`); the native branches, `runtime` parameters and `CUSTOM_SKILL_GRAPH_RUNTIMES` / `UNIFIED_GRAPH_RUNTIMES` of `agent-custom-skill-stage.js` and `agent-unified-graph-stage.js`; and `getAgentRuntime()` with every branch on it. Kept: admission (`admitExecutionGraphRun`), the run tail (`finishExecutionGraphRun`), `replayExecutionGraphRun`, `agent-execution-graph-node.js`, the graph checkpoints, the replay matrix and recovery, `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT=off` (the V1 chain; phase 8 removed both), and, in phase 6, the V1 outer loop `runAgentExecutionPlan` with `AGENT_OUTER_PLAN_RUNTIME` (then the production outer-plan scheduler; phase 7 removed that loop and dial, below). The custom-Skill stage still imports the executor on demand: loading LangGraph after `agent.js` measured 174 ms in phase 6, which the api edge does not pay. Since phase 7 the V1 outer plan runs on LangGraph, so every process that answers agent requests loads it (right after listen in an agent-tier process since phase 12, otherwise on its first request; the phase-7 figures are under "Decision and cost" below); what the on-demand import still keeps out of such a process is the graph executor and compiler. (What follows describes the v3 stage, which phase 10 removed with the v3 unified graph; phase 11 deleted `replayExecutionGraphRun`.) The v3 stage rebuilt a settled graph's receipts for its finalization with `replayExecutionGraphRun` (no scheduler: the layer-order skip decisions and `recordSkippedSkill` records, and a node that would still run refused with the replay guard before any step, budget, preflight or checkpoint hook), runs a step lifecycle without a run target on `runExecutionGraphOnLangGraph` without a checkpointer (an ephemeral thread nothing reads back; a parked node returns its receipt and the stage's `pauseForApproval` persists the pause), and always checks that the lifecycle target's runId equals `agentRunId`.
    - **Start-up refusal:** `AGENT_RUNTIME`, `AGENT_OUTER_PLAN_RUNTIME`, (since phase 8) `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` and (since phase 10) `AGENT_UNIFIED_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` are read only by `assertAgentRuntimeSupported(env)` (`rag/config.js`, codes `AGENT_RUNTIME_REMOVED`, `AGENT_OUTER_PLAN_RUNTIME_REMOVED`, `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT_REMOVED` and `AGENT_UNIFIED_GRAPH_ROLLOUT_REMOVED`; `evaluation/rollout-readiness-report.js` also reads the Skill graph rollout variable, to fail its readiness gate in an environment that would not start): for each, unset, empty, whitespace or the one value left (`langgraph` for the two runtime dials, `guarded` for the Skill graph rollout, `off` for the unified graph rollout; trimmed, any case) start; the removed values (`native`; `off` and `shadow` of the Skill graph rollout; `guarded` and `shadow` of the unified graph rollout) and any other value (a typo such as `lang-graph`, `pregel` or `gaurded`, quoted in the message) refuse the start instead of silently answering another way (until phase 7 a mistyped `AGENT_OUTER_PLAN_RUNTIME` meant the native loop, until phase 8 a mistyped `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` meant `off`, the chain, and until phase 10 a mistyped `AGENT_UNIFIED_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` meant `off`). `AGENT_UNIFIED_GRAPH_CAPABILITIES` is no longer read and refuses nothing. `server.js` checks right after the LangSmith start-up guard, `startServiceRole` (`rag/agent-service/role-server.js`) checks the env it is handed before topology validation, and `createAppServices` (`app-services.js`) checks before it reads any option, so every in-process app refuses (the monolith, the agent tier, an eval runner that builds `createApp`; a load-bench child never receives either variable). Every entry that schedules on LangGraph checks again next to the LangSmith guard (the outer plan's entry `runAgentExecutionPlanOnLangGraph` before it reads the plan, the custom-Skill stage before the graph checkpoint, and `runExecutionGraphOnLangGraph` before admission), which covers eval runners that call `runAgentRag` without building an app (`eval:trajectory`, `eval:planner`, `eval:recovery-observability`, `eval:prompt-injection`, `eval:answer-drafts`, `eval:qasper-answers -- --surface agent`): since phase 7 every request that reaches the outer plan is scheduled on LangGraph, so a document question fails with the removed-value code too (a `case_error` in a trajectory report), not only a request with a custom-Skill DAG. No evaluation runner pins a dial (the last `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT=guarded` pins, in `eval:planner`'s dynamic graph case, `eval:recovery-observability`'s replan scenario, `eval:answer-drafts`, `eval:prompt-injection` and `trace:demo`, went on 2026-11-20: they let those runs through under a removed value), so an environment that asks for a removed value fails every case. Not checked: requests that never reach the plan (a pre-execution clarification), `completePlannerText` (refusing there would only make an LLM planner fall back to the deterministic one), and `chat-mcp.js` and `ingest-worker.mjs`, which run no graph. `test/agent-runtime-removed.test.mjs` runs every check for the four variables: the accepted and refused values, `startServiceRole`, a real `node server.js` (non-zero exit, the code on stderr), `createAppServices` refusing before it touches its options, the executor refusing before admission, the outer plan's entry refusing before it reads the plan, and an eval runner's in-process trajectory case (a document question and a custom-Skill DAG) refused under each removed value and not when unset.
    - **No runtime rollback:** the only way back to the native scheduler is `git revert` of the phase-6 commit, which restores the loop, the branches, `getAgentRuntime()`, the rollback suite and the native sides of the parity suites, and drops the refusal and the goldens. Runs crashed under `AGENT_RUNTIME=native` before phase 6 have a graph checkpoint and no thread; startup recovery continues them on a fresh invocation like any other interrupted custom-Skill graph (pinned by a LangGraph crash whose thread is deleted before recovery, held to the native golden). Unknown in-flight work stays manual. The outer loop likewise: the only way back is `git revert` of the phase-7 deletion commit (the freeze commit 3313b40b can stay); the outer graph writes no thread, so there is no in-flight data to handle.
    - **Goldens instead of a native side:** before the deletion the native side of the graph parity suites was frozen under `server/test/fixtures/graph-goldens/<suite>/`, compared through `test/langgraph-support/graph-goldens.mjs`; the update rules are under "Tests" in phase 2 below. The first freeze covered six suites (94 files); a review found the route-level graph scenarios of `langgraph-routes` (v3 approve and deny, the v3 stream to its pause, a background task's approval re-entry, startup recovery) and the three custom-Skill-DAG trajectory cases of `langgraph-execution-plan-graph` unfrozen, and their native sides were frozen from HEAD e1385c1c (the scheduler still present) with `AGENT_RUNTIME=native`: 102 files over eight suites. Two comparisons are not the pre-deletion ones: the `langgraph-unified-graph` replay sweep compared unmasked in one process (`assert.deepEqual`) and now compares masked digests, and the `langgraph-planner-model` goldens normalize the fake server's ephemeral port and V8's `JSON.parse` position suffix. Without a golden: the native cross-process runs of the two PostgreSQL integration suites (freezing them needs a database; they now run on LangGraph only, under their literal assertions) and `langgraph-chat-stream`'s old `AGENT_RUNTIME=native` pass (document questions only, so it ran the same code as the outer-loop pass that phase 7 later froze). Removed with the scheduler: `test/agent-graph-suites-native-rollback.test.mjs` and its preloads (`native-rollback-guard.mjs`, `langgraph-executor-forbidden.mjs`, `native-runner-counter.mjs`), `test/langgraph-native-runner-suite.test.mjs` with `langgraph-runtime-parity/runner-on-langgraph.mjs`, and `stage-native-scheduler-tripwire.mjs`. `test/langgraph-unified-graph-without-run-target.test.mjs`, in its own process, redirects the v3 stage's run-target lookup to null (`test/langgraph-support/stage-run-target-switch.mjs`), its runner import to a replay counter (`stage-replay-counter.mjs`) and its executor import to the parity suite's tap, and holds the document loop, an approval pause and approval, a denial and the finalization replay (with and without a run target) to their native goldens: as many executor invocations as the native scheduler made, none bound to a run without a run target, and a finalization replay with 0 executor calls and 1 replay. `langgraph-unified-graph` holds `replayExecutionGraphRun` to the native pass over 480 settled shapes of one five-node v3 graph (digests plus 8 full goldens; the replay side never writes a golden) and end to end (a crash before the finalization receipt, recovered on LangGraph). Until phase 6, `eval:trajectory`, `eval:planner -- --provider mock` and `eval:recovery-observability` were identical after masking under the default and `native`. Phase 10 removed the v3 unified graph and with it the suites and goldens named above for it: `langgraph-unified-graph`, `langgraph-unified-graph-without-run-target` (and its two `langgraph-support` stand-ins), five `langgraph-routes` fixtures and the unified planner's digests; `langgraph-routes` now holds the three outer-plan fixtures.
  - **Spike removed (phase 4):** the phase-0 spike (its helper directory and four test suites) is gone. What it alone proved now lives in production suites: LangGraph 1.5.0's two `maxConcurrency` defects (`langgraph-execution-graph-runtime`; the Capability-only preflight grant pinned there went with the preflight hook in phase 11); the raw `AbortSignal` re-run (`langgraph-checkpointer`); the request's tenant, deadline, event sink and run usage reaching every custom Skill call and thread write of two tenants' overlapping custom-Skill graphs (each store call is attributed by the access scope it names and the ambient context there is checked against that request, and no thread record holds the other tenant's user or run id) and no network call (`langgraph-request-context`; until phase 10 this was pinned on the v3 unified graph in `langgraph-unified-graph`, together with four proofs about approval inside a graph that went with that graph: an approval continuing under its own request, a parked node abandoned after a later failure, an approved gate whose live policy stopped asking for approval refused, and an exclusive gated Capability queued behind a running sibling never preflighted, parked or charged once the request is cancelled); a startup claim refused by a pending approval gate (`agent-execution-graph-checkpoint`). The suites that load LangChain themselves import `test/langgraph-support/langsmith-off.mjs` first; every other suite can now reach the LangGraph executor through the default, so `test/run.test.mjs` and `test/coverage-gate.mjs` drop `LANGSMITH_TRACING_VARIABLES` from every test child's environment (`ci-workflow.test.mjs` pins both). A single-file run of such a suite with one of them set fails closed at the guard (`LANGSMITH_TRACING_ENABLED`); nothing is traced.
  - **Planner model calls (phase 5):** `rag/langgraph/planner-model.js` `completePlannerText` is the one call path of the four planners (intent, execution, DAG, replan; the v3 unified graph's planner went with that graph in phase 10). It invokes a cached `ArchiveRagChatModel` (per capability and routeId, loaded by dynamic import on first use, so a process that never plans with a model never loads `@langchain/*` for it) with `{ promptTemplate, responseFormat, temperature }` inside an empty LangChain async context (no root `RunTree`); the `native` direct path went with `AGENT_RUNTIME` in phase 6. It accepts only those five options. Each planner keeps its own JSON parser and validator: never switch to `withStructuredOutput`, whose `parseFirstJsonValue` fails on a fenced JSON reply after prose with brackets, refuses the intent planner's null schema and has no schema-less mode for replan. With a LangSmith tracing variable `"true"` it calls `completeTextWithMetadata` directly, before LangChain is loaded, so the LLM planners never fall back because of the guard. `test/langgraph-planner-model.test.mjs` runs each scenario once through the adapter and holds results, provider inputs, LLMOps events and error objects per planner to goldens frozen from the direct path (`test/fixtures/graph-goldens/langgraph-planner-model/`: `digests.json` plus four whole decisions, `decide-<planner>.json`), checks that no `@langchain` module is loaded before the first adapter call, and that the LangSmith case takes the direct path without running the chat model.
  - **Adapters** (`server/rag/langgraph/`): only `model.js`, used through `planner-model.js` above: `ArchiveRagChatModel` over `completeTextWithMetadata` (`maxRetries: 0`, LangChain `signal`/`timeout` not forwarded, `withRetry`/`withFallbacks` refused, `withStructuredOutput` = strict `json_schema` + `parseFirstJsonValue`, system/human string messages only). The unwired phase-1 adapters are gone: `tools.js` (it could call a Skill outside a graph node) in phase 12, and `embeddings.js`, `retriever.js` and `prompts.js` with their equivalence suites on 2026-11-17, once the LangSmith guard suite (`test/langgraph-langsmith-guard.test.mjs`) ran on the chat model instead of them. `prompt-registry`, `llmops-metrics` and `tracing` suites stay unchanged.
  - **LangSmith never runs**: `server.js` refuses to start when any variable in `LANGSMITH_TRACING_VARIABLES` (`rag/langgraph/langsmith-guard.js`) is `"true"`, every adapter re-checks per call, and `guardRunnableEntryPoints` covers runnables the adapters build or compose. A composition another runnable starts (`RunnableSequence.from`, `other.pipe(adapter)`, `graph.invoke`) configures the tracer before adapter code runs, so every phase-2 outer entry must call `assertLangSmithTracingDisabled()` first. Never enable LangGraph's `messages` stream mode.
  - **Phase 2, the custom-Skill DAG on LangGraph** (the only scheduler since phase 6, see "Native graph scheduler removed" above). Only the scheduler of a guarded custom-Skill graph changed: `agent-custom-skill-stage.js` hands it to `runExecutionGraphOnLangGraph` (`rag/langgraph/run-execution-graph.js`, imported on demand so a process that runs no guarded graph never loads the graph executor, LangSmith refused before the graph checkpoint is written); planning, `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` (then a three-way dial; phase 8 removed `off` and `shadow`), the v1 graph checkpoint, replans and startup recovery are unchanged, and the v3 unified graph stayed native in phase 2 (phase 3 moved it to the same executor; phase 10 removed that graph).
    - **Reuse, not copies:** admission (`admitExecutionGraphRun`) and the run tail (`finishExecutionGraphRun`, sync) are exported from `agent-execution-graph-runner.js`; node states, the skip decision and node execution are `agent-execution-graph-node.js`. `rag/langgraph/compile-execution-graph.js` builds the StateGraph (START into dependency-free nodes, a join edge per node, leaves into END); `rag/langgraph/concurrency-gate.js` keeps the deleted native pass in process (cap, exclusivity, first come first served; a node that will only be skipped takes a turn instead of a slot, decided where the native scan would reach it and recorded after the starts granted ahead of it), because LangGraph 1.5.0 drops tasks when its own `maxConcurrency` is below a superstep's width (invoke passes the node count).
    - **Layer-order queue (phase 5):** the scheduler queues the nodes in the validator's topological layer order, `compiled.order` (Kahn layers, declaration order within a layer), through `orderExecutionGraphNodeStatesByLayer` (`agent-execution-graph-node.js`), and LangGraph enters each layer in its own superstep, in that order. Results, node runs and checkpoints keep declaration order. A held-back node (cap or exclusivity) is therefore never overtaken by a later layer's node. Phase 5 moved the native loop to the same queue before phase 6 deleted it, which removed the delegation path (`canScheduleExecutionGraphOnLangGraph` and the hand-over to `runExecutionGraph`); the goldens were frozen from that layer-order native loop. Against declaration order, only graphs not declared in layer order changed, where the cap or an exclusive node held a ready node back: the earlier layer runs first (a forward reference `[summary after risk, risk, timeline]` under a cap of 1 runs risk, timeline, summary, where declaration order ran risk, summary, timeline, which also changes which node a short budget funds and what a fail_fast failure aborts). In production the only such graph was the deterministic v3 document graph with Web under `AGENT_UNIFIED_GRAPH_ROLLOUT=guarded` (removed in phase 10; the queue order stays) (`web` declared after `follow_up_check` but a layer earlier, document_rag and web nodes exclusive): when both the follow-up and the Web node run, `web` runs before `follow_up_check`, which reorders `agentTrace`, `agentRunSteps` and `agentObservability.runs` and nothing else. Pinned by `langgraph-execution-graph-runtime` (all 120 declaration orders of a five-node graph, held back by cap 1 or by exclusivity, under no pressure, budget and fail_fast: 720 scenarios held to the native digests, LangGraph receipts for every node), and `agent-execution-graph-runner` (the `langgraph-unified-graph` suite, which held that v3 graph to its native golden, went with the graph in phase 10); see the ADR's phase 5 "层序调度".
    - **Names and state:** a LangGraph node is `node_<zero-padded queue index>_<base64url(nodeId)>`; LangGraph enters a superstep's nodes in name order, which makes the gate's arrival order the queue order (pinned). Edges resolve dependencies by the validator's trimmed id; node code looks them up as written, as the native scheduler did (a dependency written `" a"` leaves its node pending). State is one `receipts` channel keyed `node:<nodeId>` (never the bare id: JsonPlusSerializer assigns keys, so `__proto__` would replace the prototype), each `{ status, reason, outputDigest }` and nothing else; a missing or different receipt after invoke fails the run (`AGENT_LANGGRAPH_RECEIPT_MISMATCH`). A node error reaches LangGraph only as `AGENT_LANGGRAPH_NODE_STOPPED` with a code; the original error is what the run throws.
    - **Durability:** the run-store `RunStoreCheckpointSaver` is used only when the stage holds a graph checkpoint, bound to the run the node steps write (`getAgentRunStepLifecycleTarget(stepLifecycle)`, so `agent.js` passes nothing new) and to `graphResumeClaimId`. Every call is a fresh invocation: the previous thread is tombstoned first, reused nodes are seeded from `completedNodeRuns`, nothing resumes from a LangGraph checkpoint. `durability: "sync"`, no `retryPolicy`, `recursionLimit` = depth + 1, explicit `thread_id` (an ephemeral derived one without a run). A graph with nothing left to launch never invokes LangGraph.
    - **Cancellation is the drain:** the request signal only calls `RunControl.requestDrain`; each node passes the safe point on entry and again after the gate admits it (a cancellation there becomes `GraphDrained` and the run's fatal error); a node's gate slot is released only after its outcome is recorded; on any error every node in flight settles first, and a drain maps to the request's `RequestCancelledError` through `resolveAgentRunTermination`. An `AgentRunInterruptError` inside a node keeps the native loop's handling (paused step, `executionGraphNodeRuns`); the node then takes the decision the native loop's next pass took (v2: skipped `aborted_after_failure`).
    - **Known differences from the deleted native scheduler** (superstep barrier, pinned in `test/langgraph-execution-graph-runtime.test.mjs` as projections of the native goldens, for example the same Skill runs in another order): a node is decided when its whole superstep has finished. So (1) a dependant starts only after the slowest node of the superstep before it: steps, run events, trace, Skill calls and per-Skill observations can come in another order, a per-Skill `budgetDelta` (budget others reserved while it ran) can differ, and latency follows the slowest sibling; (2) a fail_fast failure, or a budget that cannot fund every node, settles a race native has (which Skill finishes first) the way native settles it when every node of a superstep finishes first, so LangGraph can skip a dependant native had already launched; (3) a skip caused in the same native pass is recorded one superstep later; (4) a cancellation stops before a superstep native would have settled without launching (a false condition after the cancelled node). Node outcomes never differ without a race in (2).
