A stale worker fenced by a recovery claim fails with the same `AGENT_GRAPH_EXECUTION_FENCED` as natively, but on LangGraph its first fenced write may be the thread's (tombstone or superstep record, "LangGraph checkpoint writer does not own the run's running graph.") rather than a node step's.
    - **Tests:** since phase 6 there is no native side to run. The graph scenarios that used to compare the two runtimes run once on LangGraph and are held to a golden frozen from the native side before the deletion (the exceptions, and what has no golden, are under "Goldens instead of a native side" above), under `server/test/fixtures/graph-goldens/<suite>/` (masked JSON, or for sweeps too large to store the sha256 of the canonical serialization in `digests.json`), through `test/langgraph-support/graph-goldens.mjs` (`assertMatchesGraphGolden`, `assertMatchesGraphGoldenDigest`; the canonical form sorts object keys, keeps array order, and tags `undefined`, `-0`, `NaN`, ±Infinity, bigint, Map, Set and Date instead of dropping them). Golden rules, pinned by `test/graph-goldens.test.mjs`: without `UPDATE_GRAPH_GOLDENS` (or empty) a missing or different fixture fails and nothing is written; `UPDATE_GRAPH_GOLDENS=1` writes from the runtime under test (LangGraph) and only from a call that may write (a side held to a scheduler golden, such as a replay, passes `{ write: false }`), and a run that creates or changes a fixture still fails, naming it: review the diff, explain the intended behaviour change in a comment in the same change, then rerun without the variable; any other value (`native`, `true`, `yes`, `" 1"`) is refused. The superstep and known-difference goldens are only read (`readGolden`), so a deliberate change there means editing the fixture by hand. `test/langgraph-execution-graph-runtime.test.mjs` holds results, node runs, steps, events, trace, budget, per-Skill observations, Skill call order and the v1 checkpoint (only times, durations, uuids and the checkpoint seal masked) to those goldens. `test/agent-execution-graph-runner.test.mjs` (the scheduler contract, 48 tests) runs directly on `runExecutionGraphOnLangGraph` with the assertions written for the native scheduler unchanged, and so does `agent-replanner`. `test/langgraph-runtime-parity.test.mjs` pins the three `skill_graph` trajectory cases (four until phase 8 removed `skill_graph_shadow_comparison` and its fixture with the chain), `planner_dynamic_skill_graph` (mock provider) and the recovery-observability production events: each runs once through the evaluation's own runner, and every check with its detail, the response projection, the recovery report and every run the cases created (events, status, steps, result; masked: UUIDs, ISO timestamps, `durationMs`/`latencyMs`, the checkpoint `digest`) must equal `<case id>.json`. The cases keep their run stores private, so a `module.register` resolve hook (`test/langgraph-runtime-parity/`) redirects the evaluation modules' `rag/agent-runs.js` to a recording stand-in and the stage's executor import to a counting one; each case's LangGraph invocations (node ids, reused ids, run binding) are pinned, so the suite fails if a case stops reaching the graph. Until phase 6, `eval:trajectory`, `eval:planner` and `eval:recovery-observability` reports were identical after masking under both runtimes (10, 1 and 4 graph runs went through LangGraph). The two cross-process cases of `agent-execution-graph-postgres.integration.test.mjs` run on LangGraph (until phase 6 also under `AGENT_RUNTIME=native` with the same assertions; that native run has no golden): the `risk` Skill records the thread's receipts it sees through `RunStoreCheckpointSaver` (in an effect-table column) to show what the resume ran, since the settling save drops the thread; the claim case also runs with the claim between the two nodes and asserts which fenced write stopped the stale worker. `test/langgraph-checkpointer-postgres.integration.test.mjs` (contract and fences on the PostgreSQL store) is part of `test:pgvector`.
    - **Write amplification (wired in phases 3 and 4).** The thread record rides on the run row, so every later revision CAS of the run (node steps, graph checkpoint saves, `completeRun`) rewrites it whole. Phase 2 measured in memory, one guarded stage over a 3-node graph on LangGraph: 48 run reads vs 38 native, 21 plain updates vs 13, largest stored result 9,302 B vs 3,273 B. Phase 3 wired three things: a thread write that changes nothing (the tombstone every invocation writes first on a run that has no thread, a replayed put) is decided on the request's run cursor and costs no read (`mutateStoredRun({ acceptCursorNoOp })`); an event-less thread write does not read the event list back on PostgreSQL (`mutateStoredRun({ reuseBaseEvents })`, `postgres-agent-run-store.update({ reuseBaseEvents })`); and the record is dropped in the same CAS by a graph's settling save (`saveExecutionGraphCheckpoint({ dropLangGraphRecord: true })`, phase completed or partial only; wired for the v3 unified graph in phase 3, for the custom-Skill DAG in phase 4) and by `failRun` (a run that fails or is cancelled inside its graph). Phase 4 wired the rest: the executor's saver keeps `LANGGRAPH_CHECKPOINT_RETENTION` (2) checkpoints per namespace (`RunStoreCheckpointSaver({ retainCheckpoints })`; `test/langgraph-execution-graph-runtime.test.mjs` pins 2 for one invocation, was 3), and the custom-Skill DAG's settling save drops the record, so no finished run carries a thread, only the null tombstone (`agent-run-chat-statement-count` and `langgraph-runtime-parity` check every record as it was written, through store taps; `langgraph-execution-graph-runtime`'s stage harness leaves the drop out so its scenarios can read the thread, and one test forwards it as `agent.js` does). `test/langgraph-checkpointer-write-amplification.test.mjs` proves each rule on its own (retention with an interrupt and resume after pruning included). Measured with pg_stat_statements on PostgreSQL 18 (the 2-node guarded custom-Skill `/chat` of `test/agent-run-chat-statement-count.test.mjs`, owner path), the counts did not change in phase 4: LangGraph adds to the native scheduler's 1 insert, 9 event-carrying CAS, 3 event appends, 1 run read and 1 event-list read exactly 7 event-less CAS, 1 run read and 1 event-list read (since phase 6 the native counts live in that suite's golden); the completed run's result is 11,894 bytes against 11,860 native (17,006 in phase 3, when the 5,116-byte record stayed), and the largest record any write carried is 2,975 bytes. The phase-3 load test passed with the outer plan native (ADR, "压测硬门与外层计划的决定").
  - **Phase 7 (2026-11-13), the V1 outer plan and `/chat/stream` on LangGraph only.** The outer plan was put on LangGraph in phase 3 behind `AGENT_OUTER_PLAN_RUNTIME=langgraph`, with the native loop `runAgentExecutionPlan` as the default; phase 7 deleted the loop, the dial (`getAgentOuterPlanRuntime()`, `resolveAgentExecutionPlanRunner`) and the native branch of `routes/chat.js`, so the Pregel graph below and the `streamAgentRun` generator are the only path.
    - **Decision and cost:** the load tests of phases 3 and 5 (pgvector, owner path, 0 ms model) failed the hard gate (at most +10% CPU per request and p95 against native) and kept the loop: as a StateGraph about +1.2 ms CPU per `/chat` (+29–35% CPU per request, +25% p95 at concurrency 32; LangChain core builds a `RunTree` per node call); on low-level Pregel with pruning +0.28 ms per run in process, and still +17.6% CPU per request (5.68 → 6.68 ms median) and +13% p95 (9.3 → 10.5 ms) at concurrency 1, with concurrency 32 drifting 15–20% between runs of one configuration. What remains is Pregel's fixed per-invocation work (loop, task ids, checkpoint objects), inside the library. On 2026-10-16 the user approved keeping the loop for good; on 2026-11-13 the same user decided the agent has one runtime and accepted the cost. The gate was not re-run and is knowingly not met: measured again before the deletion (in process, 7 alternating rounds of 400 document questions, Node 24.21), one `runAgentRag` costs 0.605 ms CPU on the loop and 0.894 ms on the graph (+0.29 ms); against a real model's 2.4 s per answer it does not show. No load test was run for phase 7, so do not quote a new `/chat` CPU figure. Every process that answers an agent request loads `@langchain/*` once: since phase 12 a process that hosts the agent tier (the monolith without `AGENT_SERVICE_URL`, `ARCHIVE_RAG_ROLE=agent`) imports the graph module right after it listens (`warmAgentExecutionPlanRuntime`), and an in-process app or eval runner still loads it on its first request; measured on 2026-11-13 (Node 24.21, importing the graph module after `agent.js`, RSS after a GC), about 115 ms once and RSS 82 → 143 MB; the default LLM planners already load LangChain core on the first `/chat` (about 70 ms, RSS to 122 MB), so what phase 7 adds there is LangGraph itself, about 50 ms and 21 MB. The api edge, the ingest worker, the retrieval tier and the model gateway still never load it. If Pregel's per-invocation cost falls after a `@langchain/langgraph` or `@langchain/core` upgrade, re-measure with `evaluation`'s load bench; never bring a second scheduler back to win the difference.
    - **The runner:** `agent-execution-plan-runner.js` defines the plan: `createAgentExecutionPlanStages` (validation plus the stage bodies over one closure-owned state object), `hasAgentExecutionPlanResponse` (the one routing decision: a stage set the response, so the plan ends), `tagAgentExecutionPlanError` (an approval interrupt carries `agentExecutionState`), `isAgentExecutionPlanStageStaticallyInert` (pruning), and `runAgentExecutionPlan(options)`, the single entry `agent.js` calls: it imports the graph module on first use (a process that hosts the agent tier, the monolith without `AGENT_SERVICE_URL` or `ARCHIVE_RAG_ROLE=agent`, imports it right after it listens through `warmAgentExecutionPlanRuntime`, which never rejects and logs a warning on failure; the api edge, retrieval, model-gateway and the ingest worker never load LangGraph) and hands every plan to `runAgentExecutionPlanOnLangGraph`. The name is the old loop's, so callers and `test/agent-execution-plan-runner.test.mjs` (13 tests, assertions unchanged) did not move.
    - **The graph** (`rag/langgraph/execution-plan-graph.js`, `runAgentExecutionPlanOnLangGraph` = LangSmith guard, removed-runtime check, `createAgentExecutionPlanStages`, then the scheduling core `runExecutionPlanStagesOnLangGraph`; the removed loop's options, result and errors): built on the public low-level API `@langchain/langgraph/pregel` and `/channels`, not StateGraph (StateGraph wraps every node in several runnables that each build a LangSmith `RunTree`, about 1.1 ms per doc-QA run). One node per scheduled stage in plan order: its trigger channel, a small `Runnable` that calls the stage directly, and one `ChannelWrite` whose mapper writes the receipt and the next stage's trigger, or nothing once the stage set the response, which ends the plan. Compiled once per plan shape (LRU of 64 keyed by the scheduled step ids); every per-request object (state, stages, control, in-flight tracker, event relay) travels in `config.configurable` under one private key (`bindExecutionPlanGraphContext`), never in a closure of the cached graph (`test/langgraph-execution-plan-graph-isolation.test.mjs`: 1,024 requests, 64 in flight, on one compiled graph). Nodes have no ambient `getConfig()`, so the relay gets the config explicitly. Pruning: a stage for which `isAgentExecutionPlanStageStaticallyInert` (`agent-execution-plan-runner.js`) is true is not scheduled: only arxiv_import, workspace_action, research_brief, inventory and document_discovery with condition `selected_skill` whose Skill the same `getSelectedSkill` lookup does not find (a plan of only such stages keeps its first one); custom_skills, document_rag and web_search are never pruned (the removed native loop entered every stage). `test/agent-execution-plan-pruning.test.mjs` proves with recording proxies that those five stage bodies, unselected, touch nothing, and that a walk of every stage body leaves the state the pruned graph returns; keep it passing when a built-in stage changes, and move pruning to run time if the selection ever becomes mutable mid-run. Every scheduled stage has a safe point for a cancelled request at its entry (a pruned stage's safe point is covered by the next node's or by the drain). State is one `receipts` channel keyed by step id, `{ status, reason }` only; after the stream the entered stages must be the scheduled ones in plan order, ending at the last one or at the stage that set the response, or the run fails (`AGENT_LANGGRAPH_PLAN_RECEIPT_MISMATCH`). Event relay: LangGraph 1.5.0 errors its output stream one microtask after a node failure or drain, which discards unread `custom` chunks, so a node settles only after the consumer has forwarded every event it relayed (`createEventRelay`); a stage that emits after its node settled would still lose events. `checkpointer: false`: the V1 outer path keeps its step-based durability and the replay matrix, nothing resumes from this graph, and the explicit `thread_id` (`deriveLangGraphThreadId`, an ephemeral id without a run) only keeps the invocation off an ambient configurable. `durability: "sync"`, no `retryPolicy`, `maxConcurrency` = the scheduled stage count, `recursionLimit` = that count + 1, `assertLangSmithTracingDisabled()` at the entry and in every node. A stage error reaches LangGraph only as `AGENT_LANGGRAPH_STAGE_STOPPED` with a code; the original error object is what the run throws. The plan is never re-derived by the graph.
    - **Cancellation** is the drain of `rag/langgraph/drain-bridge.js` (`bridgeRequestSignalToDrain`, `resolveDrainedInvocationError`, shared by every graph runner here): the request's signal asks `RunControl` to start no further superstep, the stage in flight settles, and the run fails with the request's own `RequestCancelledError` (`AGENT_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` / `AGENT_CLIENT_CANCELLED`) through `failRun`, exactly as the removed loop's safe point threw it (the goldens pin the run records); an outage keeps its 503/504.
    - **Streaming** (`rag/langgraph/agent-stream.js`, no `@langchain` import of its own, statically imported by `routes/chat.js`): the graph always runs through `graph.stream` with `streamMode: ["updates", "custom"]` (never `messages`). Inside a node, `relayAgentEventsThroughWriter(config, work)` points the agent event sink at `config.writer` only when the invocation declared `AGENT_STREAM_RELAY_KEY` in `configurable` and a streaming client is attached (without a client nothing is nested, so no answer-draft channel opens in plain `/chat`; without the declaration a node keeps the outer sink, because LangGraph's writer is a no-op unless `custom` is streamed); the consumer loop hands each custom chunk to the sink bound around the run (`forwardAgentStreamChunk`). `streamAgentRun({ answer, describeError })` is the `/chat/stream` generator, the route's only path: it binds the sink around the whole run (planner steps before any graph, the finalizer after it, and whichever graph answers), yields every event in emission order, then `{ type: "result", body, status }` (identical to `/chat`) or `{ type: "error", body, status }` from the route's `describeAgentRouteError`, then `{ type: "done" }`; `toAgentStreamFrame` gives the `{ event, data }` frames. The outer plan graph relays its stages' events through the writer (a custom-Skill DAG's among them, since it runs inside the `custom_skills` node). The SSE bytes are those of the removed native branch (goldens). Events are not batched until a node ends: measured in process, an event emitted inside the document stage reached the consumer 0–1 ms later. Raw tokens never leave the server and `answer-drafts.js` is untouched.
    - **LangSmith and the goldens:** a LangSmith tracing variable is refused by `streamAgentRun` before the run exists (an `error` frame with the guard's message and status 500, then `done`, no run recorded), and by the graph entry on `/chat` (the run is failed with `LANGSMITH_TRACING_ENABLED`). Since phase 7 that is every `/chat/stream` request (the check runs before the run exists, so a request that would stop before the plan, such as a clarification asked before planning, gets the `error` frame too) and every `/chat` request that reaches the plan (one that stops before it still answers 200, so the two routes differ there); on the removed loop a request that built no graph (a document question) still answered under such a variable on both routes. `server.js` refuses to start under such a variable, so only an in-process caller, or a process whose environment changed after start, sees either. No other difference from the loop is known, and what the loop did is frozen: before the deletion, commit 3313b40b wrote the loop's pass of every scenario the two-pass parity suites compared to `server/test/fixtures/graph-goldens/` (the native pass wrote, the LangGraph pass was held to the same fixture with `write: false`), 32 files: `langgraph-execution-plan-graph` (10 trajectory cases next to the three custom-Skill-DAG fixtures of phase 6, which did not change, and 14 direct scenarios: a research brief, an abstention falling through to Web with and without a standing approval (ADR 0001 phase 13 deleted the with-approval scenario and its golden on 2026-11-17, leaving 13 direct scenarios and 31 files), a dependency outage, a cancellation at the first boundary for both reasons, a cancellation surfaced by the drain after a stage settled, a deadline inside a stage, a burst of events before an error for both reasons, an interleaved plan cancelled before and inside the document stage for both reasons), `langgraph-chat-stream` (the SSE bytes, frames and runs of five streams) and `langgraph-routes` (POST `/chat`, POST `/chat/stream` with GET and POST `/chat`, the V1 Capability approval). Each suite now runs every scenario once on LangGraph against its golden (masked: UUIDs, ISO timestamps, `durationMs`/`latencyMs`/`totalDurationMs`, the checkpoint `digest`): `test/langgraph-execution-plan-graph.test.mjs` (the response or error, every agent event, the runs' events, steps, result and error; the runner's import of the graph is redirected to a tap in `test/langgraph-execution-plan-graph/`, so a scenario that should reach the plan and never scheduled it fails, and the scheduled shapes show the pruning happened), `test/langgraph-chat-stream.test.mjs` (drafts only for sentences the finalizer's claim check supports, the `error`/`done` frames of a deadline, the canceled run of a client that leaves; the route's import of `agent-stream.js` is tapped, so each stream is seen to go through `streamAgentRun`), and `test/langgraph-routes.test.mjs`. `agent-run-chat-statement-count` keeps one pass (the outer plan has no checkpointer, so the pinned store calls did not change). A new outer-plan scenario has no native side to compare with: write it with literal assertions, or add a golden from LangGraph under the `UPDATE_GRAPH_GOLDENS` rules. Before and after the deletion, `eval:trajectory` (19/19), `eval:planner -- --provider mock` (7/7) and `eval:recovery-observability` (8/8) were identical after masking.
  - **Phase 8 (2026-11-13), custom Skills run as a graph only.** The V1 sequential Skill chain was the last agent path that did not run on LangGraph: it answered under `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT=off` or `shadow`, and under `guarded` when the whole graph was rejected before any node ran. The owner asked for a plan first, then chose to delete it (ADR 0001 "阶段 8 结果").
    - **Why the fallback was not needed:** measured on e11ced91 with record points added to the stage in a scratch worktree, the whole suite and the three deterministic agent evals entered the stage 188 times: 133 graph, 27 explicit `off`, 12 `shadow`, 14 rejected-then-chain (all in tests: one pinning the budget fallback, one no-op, twelve runs whose RAG stub had no `getDocument`), one checkpoint replay, one no-Skill no-op; none of the fallbacks was in an eval. With the shipped defaults (at most two custom Skills per intent, a call budget of two that only code can lower, `/chat` verifying each document with the same `getDocument` lookup before the agent runs, all four custom Skills declaring the typed contract) the fallback never executed a Skill in production. There is no production traffic data behind that statement: it is derived from the code and the defaults.
    - **What replaced it:** the custom skill stage bullet above (the deterministic graph is budgeted per node at launch; any other rejection runs nothing and reports each selected Skill; no Skill selected returns an empty result).
    - **Freeze, then delete:** 2c1c6c06 pinned what the chain answered (`test/agent-custom-skill-budget.test.mjs`) and moved the twelve runs that reached the chain only through a stub without `getDocument` onto the graph; 3b3a8719 deleted the chain. Against the frozen outcomes the caller's answer is unchanged with a budget of two, one and no calls (the record differs with one or none: a Skill skipped at launch has no result and is not listed in `agentSkills`, the budget note names the reservation, the graph event says executed/partial); with documents the stage cannot verify, the chain's findings become an abstention, which `/chat` does not reach.
    - **Lost, with no replacement:** retrying one failed custom-Skill step by hand (only chain steps supported it; a graph run refuses a standalone step retry with `AGENT_GRAPH_STEP_REQUIRES_GRAPH_RECOVERY`, and the default path had been the graph since 2026-09-25); the `shadow` comparison; the chain half of the unified-graph case's V1 comparison; the trajectory cases `custom_skill_retry` and `skill_graph_shadow_comparison` with their fixtures (19 cases / 84 checks became 17 / 76, manifest 1.16.0, no threshold changed, no case added). A custom Skill without the typed contract used to run through the chain and now does not run. Running the next Skill after one failed was also chain-only then (the graph skipped a node whose dependency failed), so that was no longer available by setting `off`; phase 12 restored it on the graph (option B, 2026-11-16).
    - **Kept in phase 8, removed in phase 9:** the standalone retry and startup-recovery handlers for `custom_skill` steps, which only runs stored by the chain could reach (see "Phase 9" below). Graph events keep `mode: "guarded"` and `fallback` (always `null` now) because stored runs, the replay guard and the reports read them.
    - **Independent review (2026-11-13):** no high or medium finding; seven low ones, recorded with what was done about each in the ADR's phase-8 "独立复核". Not changed then and worth knowing: with an LLM planner and a call budget lowered below the number of selected Skills, a budget-skipped deterministic graph reaches the replan loop (until phase 12 it also spent one replanner call the budget could not honour, because `hasSpendableBudget` looked at every budget key; phase 12 fixed that, and phase 12 also added a counter for a graph rejected whole); a chain-created run parked at an approval gate and re-entered after the upgrade is expected to fail with `AGENT_GRAPH_EXECUTION_FENCED` (read, not run). Unrelated and older, fixed in phase 12: under `AGENT_PLANNER_ROLLOUT=deterministic|shadow` the custom-Skill graph's planner record said `fallback` ("DAG planner response must contain a non-empty nodes array."), because the rollout wrapper of the deterministic planner was treated as a primary planner and handed the redacted context; the executed graph was right.
    - **No rollback dial:** the way back is `git revert` of 3b3a8719.
  - **Phase 9 (2026-11-13), a `custom_skill` step is never replayed on its own.** A custom Skill runs only as a node of its execution graph. The owner chose to delete what phase 8 had kept for stored runs (ADR 0001 "阶段 9 结果").
    - **The rule:** startup recovery in `auto` mode does one thing by itself, continue an interrupted graph under a claim; every other interrupted run goes to an operator (`explainInterruptedRun` in `agent-run-recovery.js` only names the reason). As before, a run already marked for manual recovery and a graph another worker has claimed are skipped untouched. (Until phase 10 a v3 graph parked at its approval gate was skipped too; a run of that removed graph is now handed to an operator, see "v3 unified graph removed" above.) An operator has two actions, `retry_failed_step` and `cancel`; `resume_from_step` is not an action (400). The step executor has `applyApprovalAction` and `retryStep`, no `resumeStep`, and its handler registry has no `custom_skill` handler.
    - **One source for "can this step be retried":** the replay matrix (`agent-run-step-replay-safety.js`, `isStepRetryAllowed`). It has no "auto replay safe" column and its assessments carry no `canAutoReplay`. `custom_skill` has `replayActions: []` and the reason `graph_node_step`. Four types list `retry_failed_step`: `document_rag`, `follow_up_retrieval`, `research_question` and `capability_call`. The matrix must agree with the step handlers: `web_search` and `arxiv_import` have handlers that always refuse a direct retry (the retry goes through the `capability_call` step an approval queues), so the matrix does not list it for them either (it did until the phase-9 review; `agent-run-step-replay-safety.test.mjs` holds the two together). Both UIs read the matrix: the recovery state offers "Retry failed step" only for a type it allows, and the chat answer's "Failed steps" shows a Retry button only for those types (`isStepRetrySupported` in `src/components/workbenchFormatters.js`, checked against the server matrix by `workbenchFormatters.test.js`). So a failed graph node or a failed web search gets no button; each used to get one that always answered 409.
    - **The replay guard** refuses every `custom_skill` step, with or without a `nodeId` or a checkpoint, wherever a step is retried or generically updated: the executor's pre-check, and `retryStep` and `updateRunStep` inside the run store's CAS. A graph writes its own node steps through the step lifecycle (`recordRunStep`), which asks the guard only when the run holds a checkpoint and the step is not one of its nodes; a graph running without checkpoint persistence depends on that.
    - **What changed for a stored run:** a chain-shaped `custom_skill` step (no `nodeId`, no checkpoint) that was interrupted is handed to an operator with `graph_node_step` and can only be cancelled; a failed one is not offered a retry and the retry route answers 409 `AGENT_GRAPH_STEP_REQUIRES_GRAPH_RECOVERY`. Before, each of these ran the Skill outside any graph. Fixed on the way: an interrupted graph-node step whose run has no checkpoint used to be tried and refused at every startup, leaving the run `running` and unlisted; it is now handed to an operator once.
    - **Freeze, then delete:** 7a2e3d99 pinned ten cases as literals in `test/agent-skill-step-replay.test.mjs` through the real run store, step executor and recovery services; be353b62 deleted the code and its diff of that file shows each changed expectation.
    - **Evaluation and gate:** `eval:recovery-observability` has 8 cases and 27 checks (was 29; `resume_after_partial_step_recorded` and `resume_step_recorded` went with the feature) and report version 1.2.0. Its startup fixture is two interrupted runs with no graph to continue (a chain-shaped `custom_skill` step and a web search); the builder asserts both were handed to an operator before it returns an event. The `autoReplay*` counters and the gate's three "auto replay" thresholds kept their names and now count only an interrupted graph continued automatically, which the graph startup-resume probe provides. The gate lost the `recoveryStepResumeCount` minimum. Manifest 1.17.0; one golden rewritten (`recovery_observability_skill_graph_signal.json`, its 17 graph events byte-identical). No threshold changed and no case was added or removed.
    - **Not changed, on purpose:** the two branches of synthesis and response building split by `customSkillGraphExecuted` (the "no graph executed" side serves every request without a custom Skill and a graph rejected whole, so it is not chain-only); a connector manifest's own `replaySafety.autoReplaySafe` field (a separate schema that rejects `true`); the report's graph-fallback counters (they must stay 0); a stored `resume_step` or `resume_from_step` trace event still counts as an event, under its action name and as a failure when it failed, with no counter of its own. (`rag/langgraph/tools.js`, which could wrap a Skill as a LangChain tool outside a graph node and was handed to nothing in production, was deleted in phase 12.)
    - **Independent review (2026-11-13):** no high finding; two medium ones, both older than the change (the matrix listing a retry the `web_search` / `arxiv_import` handlers refuse, and three stale sentences in `docs/agent-rag.md`), and several low ones. What was done about each is in the ADR's phase-9 "独立复核".
    - **No rollback dial:** the way back is `git revert` of be353b62.
  - **Phase 3, the v3 unified graph on LangGraph: removed in phase 10 (2026-11-14).** The graph, its stage, its approval continuation and its startup recovery are gone; the ADR's "阶段 10 结果" has the list. What phase 3 added to the executor for it (a node its preflight parked called LangGraph's `interrupt()`, and an approval continued on the same thread) went in phase 11: nothing in the runtime parks a node or resumes a thread.
    - **Thread lifecycle:** the record lives only in `run.result.__langGraphCheckpoint`, always in LangGraph's root namespace (`""`), although the custom-Skill graph is invoked from inside the outer plan graph's `custom_skills` node (the executor invokes under an empty LangChain async context; `test/agent-run-chat-statement-count.test.mjs` and `langgraph-runtime-parity` pin the namespace and the store calls), and never in a public projection. It is dropped by the custom-Skill DAG's settling save and by `failRun`, and tombstoned by the next invocation's fresh start. `test/langgraph-routes.test.mjs` drives the routes on the outer plan.
- Prompt template identity: every prompt sent to a chat model has a descriptor `{id, version, fingerprint}` from `definePrompt` in `server/rag/prompt-registry.js`, defined next to its template and collected in `server/rag/prompt-catalog.js`. Pass it as `promptTemplate` to `completeText` / `completeTextWithMetadata`; it reaches the LLMOps event, the model span, `agentObservability.promptTemplates`, LLM planner decisions, and every evaluation report's `evidence.promptTemplates` (the release gate's `prompt-lineage` check requires one prompt set across the release reports). The field is named `promptTemplate` on purpose: LLMOps events must never carry prompt text (`test/llmops-metrics.test.mjs` asserts `prompt` stays undefined). A new model call needs a descriptor and a catalog entry. `test/prompt-registry.test.mjs` pins every `id@version` to its fingerprint: when a template changes, give it a new version label where its descriptor is defined and pin that, rather than updating the fingerprint under an old label.
- OpenTelemetry tracing: `server/rag/tracing.js` depends only on `@opentelemetry/api` (a no-op until an SDK is registered); `server/otel.js` registers the SDK when `OTEL_TRACING_ENABLED=true`, exporting OTLP/HTTP as protobuf by default (Phoenix rejects JSON) or JSON via `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL`. Spans follow the GenAI semantic conventions: `invoke_agent archive_rag` per run (in `agent.js`), `agent.plan *` per planner, `execute_tool <skillId>` in `executeObservedSkill`, and `chat|embeddings <model>` in `runWithLlmOpsMetric`; agent steps are `agent.step` events. Never put questions, prompts, model output, or document text in span attributes or events; `test/tracing.test.mjs` checks for leaks. `agentObservability.traceId` appears only while a span is recording. `npm run trace:demo [-- --real] [-- --otlp]` prints one run's span tree.
- Prometheus metrics (`server/rag/metrics*.js`, no client library; off by default).
  - **Listener:** `METRICS_ENABLED=true` (exactly `true`, any case) makes every role serve `GET`/`HEAD /metrics` on a listener of its own (`startMetricsFromEnv` in `metrics-server.js`, started by `server.js`, `startServiceRole` and `ingest-worker.mjs`), never on the app port (the app treats `/metrics` like any unknown path). `METRICS_PORT` (default 9464, `0` = OS-assigned; a bad value or a busy port refuses the start), `METRICS_HOST` (default `127.0.0.1`; `0.0.0.0` in a container, which logs a warning without a token), `METRICS_TOKEN` (bearer, constant-time compare, 401 otherwise). Off, every hook is one boolean check and nothing listens.
  - **Families:** HTTP RED per app server (`archive_rag_http_requests_total` / `_request_duration_seconds` / `_requests_in_flight` by `method`, Express route template, `status_class` with `429` and `aborted` apart), agent runs by outcome and reason and steps by type and status, model calls / duration / tokens / estimated cost by `metering` (`direct`, `gateway`, `mirror`; sum with `metering!="mirror"`), model-call guard in-flight / waiting / circuit state, gateway quota rejections, retrieval route duration and candidates, rerank degradations, semantic cache lookups, ingest queue depth (read at most every 15 s) and stage durations / failures, PostgreSQL pool clients and statement errors by SQLSTATE class, read-replica reads / fallbacks / lag, service-client calls / failovers / replicas, per-tenant refusals by stable code only (`archive_rag_tenant_refusals_total{reason}`, `rag/metrics-tenant.js`; alert `ArchiveRagTenantRefusalsSustained`), PDF parsing (`rag/metrics-pdf.js`: `archive_rag_pdf_pages_total{route,parser}`, `archive_rag_pdf_docling_requests_total{route,outcome}`, `archive_rag_pdf_docling_request_duration_seconds{route}`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_fallbacks_total{reason}`; `rag/metrics-pdf-pool.js`: `archive_rag_pdf_parse_pool_size`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_workers{state}`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_queued_tasks`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_tasks_total{outcome}`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_worker_restarts_total`), the Office parse pool (`rag/metrics-office-pool.js`: `archive_rag_office_parse_pool_size`, `archive_rag_office_parse_workers{state}`, `archive_rag_office_parse_queued_tasks`, `archive_rag_office_parse_tasks_total{outcome}`, `archive_rag_office_parse_worker_restarts_total`), and process CPU, memory, heap and event-loop p99.
  - **Cardinality rules:** a family declares its labels once; label names that could identify a tenant, a person, a document, request content, a URL or a host are refused at registration, and so are `role`, `instance` and `job`, which come from the scrape target. Each family caps at 1000 label sets; past it observations go to one `_overflow` series and `archive_rag_metrics_series_overflow_total{metric}` counts them. Recording never throws. Collectors (pool sizes, queue depth, guard state, replica snapshot) run under a timeout and failures count in `archive_rag_metrics_collector_errors_total`. The replica collector reads only in a process that searches in process (the same condition as `checks.readReplicas`), because reading the snapshot starts the lag monitor.
  - **Rules:** `deploy/prometheus/` holds `recording-rules.yml` (the `/chat` availability and latency SLIs over 5m/30m/1h/6h, edge only: `role=~"all|api"`), `alert-rules.yml` (multi-window burn-rate alerts for a proposed 99.5% availability and 95%-within-20 s objective, plus dead letters, model circuit, replica lag, target down and quota rejections, each with a runbook annotation) and `prometheus.example.yml` (one `role` target label per process). The latency SLI matches the bucket as `le=~"20(\\.0)?"` because Prometheus 3 stores `le="20.0"`; keep `HTTP_DURATION_BUCKETS` holding 20. `test/metrics-rules.test.mjs` parses the YAML with its own subset parser and checks that every metric and label a rule names exists; the rules have never been loaded by promtool or a real Prometheus. `docs/operations.md` explains each alert.
  - **Tests:** `metrics`, `metrics-server`, `metrics-instrumentation`, `metrics-entry-points`, `metrics-rules` and `metrics-monolith.e2e` (a real `node server.js` with a fake model, scraped before and after one `/chat`), all `server/test/*.test.mjs`; `test/metrics-exposition.mjs` is a parser helper, not a suite.
- Run-level usage ceilings live in `server/rag/run-usage.js`: tokens, estimated cost, and wall-clock time per `runAgentRag` / graph-resume invocation (`AGENT_RUN_MAX_TOKENS`, `AGENT_RUN_MAX_COST_USD`, `AGENT_RUN_MAX_DURATION_MS`, or `maxRunTokens` / `maxRunCostUsd` / `maxRunDurationMs` in `agentBudget`; 0 disables). Usage is charged in `recordLlmOpsMetric` from successful model calls only, and the run is found through `AsyncLocalStorage`, so do not thread a usage object through call sites. Enforcement stays inside `consumeBudget` / `reserveBudget` / `getRemainingBudget` so every tool degrades through its existing `budget_limit` path; `traceSteps` is exempt. Keep the meter under `budgetState.run` and `agentObservability.budget.run`: `budget.limits` / `budget.used` and the graph checkpoint owner budget must keep their shape.
- `POST /chat/stream` streams agent progress over SSE through an `AsyncLocalStorage` sink (`server/rag/agent-event-stream.js`), which the route's one generator (`streamAgentRun` in `rag/langgraph/agent-stream.js`) binds around the run; emit only compact step summaries (never step detail). Raw answer tokens never leave the server: `server/rag/answer-drafts.js` streams the primary document answer from the model, runs the finalizer's lexical claim check (`evaluateClaimSupport` + heading normalization, never the claim judge) on each completed prefix, and sends only supported claims (a sentence can stream in part) as `answer_draft` events, formatted with the finalizer's `formatSupportedClaim`; a retried or failed-over model call sends `answer_draft_reset`. A stream that carries an error object, or ends with neither a `finish_reason` nor `[DONE]`, is a failed call (retriable 502 in `fetchChatStream`, `rag/openai-client.js`), never a complete answer; the model gateway's streamed finish event carries the backend's own `finish_reason` or null, not a default "stop". A sentence counts as complete only after its trailing `[Source N]` labels and the next character arrive. Drafts are opened only by `runWithAnswerDraftChannel` around the primary `document_rag` call and only when a streaming sink is attached, so `/chat`, tasks, evals other than `eval:answer-drafts` (which attaches a sink), Skill-internal calls, and follow-up retrieval never draft. Drafts are provisional (the loop may ask for clarification or replace the answer); the final `result` event must stay identical to the `/chat` status and body. `npm run eval:answer-drafts` measures draft timing and retention with a real model.
- Agent run-scoped working memory lives in `server/rag/agent-working-memory.js`; preserve query dedupe, claim support updates, unresolved/resolved gap handling, and execution loop counters there rather than adding state mutation back to `server/rag/agent.js`.
- Agent skill execution observability lives in `server/rag/agent-skill-observability.js`; keep per-skill attempts, duration, citations, abstain, retry/follow-up, budget, run records, and skill trace detail construction there.
- Agent answer synthesis lives in `server/rag/agent-synthesis.js`; keep direct answer mode detection, skill-chain answer composition, and finalizer eligibility there.
- Agent finalization flow lives in `server/rag/agent-finalization-flow.js`; keep agent mode resolution, source selection, answer synthesis/finalizer invocation, finalizer trace, final response assembly, and final agent trace recording there.
- Agent chat response assembly and evidence clarification payloads live in `server/rag/agent-response-builder.js`; preserve `/chat` response fields, status code behavior, and error wording there rather than rebuilding response objects in `server/rag/agent.js`.
- AgentRAG query planning lives in `server/rag/agent-query-planner.js` (intent, topK); the query templates live in `server/rag/agent-retrieval-queries.js` (`buildAgentRetrievalQueries`, text only, so the rag core can import it without the skills registry). document/custom skills should pass `retrievalPlan` through to `ragService.chat`, and RAG observability should preserve `agentRetrievalPlan` plus actual `retrievalQueries`. When session memory rewrote the question (`memoryApplied`), `chat()` rebuilds a `primary` plan's queries around `resolvedQuery` with the same templates, keeping the intent (classified from the user's words) and the retrieval options; a follow-up plan is never rebuilt. The RAG trace shows the searched (rebuilt) queries, followed by any cross-language rewrite (`translation-<script>`); the agent's query-planner trace step, the `document_rag` step detail and working memory's `checkedQueries` still show the planned ones (a `shortcut:` in `chat()`). A question memory did not rewrite searches exactly the planned queries.
- Agent self-check claim support and structured gap analysis live in `server/rag/agent-self-check.js`; final answer filtering lives in `server/rag/agent-finalizer.js`. Keep both deterministic and preserve `claimSupport`, `gap_analysis`, and follow-up loop metadata in agent trace, feedback records, and feedback corpus metadata.
- Clarification responses are built by `returnClarification` in `server/rag/agent-run-context.js`; when the agent needs user input it returns `agentMode: "clarification"` with `clarification.reason`, `clarification.question`, and a `clarification_gate` trace step instead of throwing for ordinary scope issues.
- Agent working memory is run-scoped and must not be written to long-term memory unless explicitly requested; preserve `agentWorkingMemory` and `agentObservability.workingMemory` when changing execution, feedback records, or feedback corpus metadata.
- Skill chaining is whitelist-only (the chains are fixed in `server/rag/agent-intent-rules.js`); preserve `agentObservability.skillChain`, the `skill_chain` trace step, accessScope propagation, and per-skill budget checks when adding or changing chains.
- Agent trace UI lives in `src/components/RenderQA.jsx`; preserve the display of selected skills, skill chains, retrieval queries, evidence gaps, unsupported claims, and finalizer-removed claims when changing `/chat` response shape or frontend rendering.
- Trajectory eval lives in `server/evaluation/trajectory-eval.js`; preserve checks for skill selection, follow-up retrieval, clarification gate, accessScope propagation, and budget limits when changing agent orchestration. The three `skill_graph` cases in `server/evaluation/trajectory/cases/skill-graph.js` read the `skill_graph_planned` run event; they no longer set `AGENT_SKILL_GRAPH_ROLLOUT` (their ids and descriptions keep the word "guarded", the one value left and the mode every graph event records). The default suite is 15 cases and 63 checks; ADR 0001 phase 8 removed `custom_skill_retry` (it set the rollout to `off`; a graph run refuses a standalone step retry) and `skill_graph_shadow_comparison`, with the `retry` category. `server/evaluation/planner/cases/index.js` also pins a `planner_dynamic_skill_graph` case: compare-only upstream intent, independent atomic compare+risk DAG, scope and observability checks; the `real` provider exercises the LLM DAG planner. Every trajectory/planner case id, check id, and response projection is pinned in `server/evaluation/quality-current-suite-manifest.js`, so adding or renaming a case or check means updating that manifest and bumping its version in the same change.
- Observability reporting lives in `server/evaluation/observability-report.js` and `server/evaluation/build-observability-report.mjs`; preserve support for both `traceType: "agent"` events and lower-level RAG trace events. The `recovery` block counts `skill_graph_planned` run events (planned, executed, fallback, fallback-after-execution, reused nodes, applied replans) and guarded graph startup resume signals. `server/evaluation/recovery-observability-eval.js` produces the replan signal from a real guarded run and the `skill_graph_startup_resume` case from a checkpoint plus re-instantiated services over one in-memory store. That case exercises the production continuation API but does not prove an OS process restart or PostgreSQL durability; `quality-recovery-gate.js` fails when recovery checks fail or a graph falls back to V1 after a node has executed.
- Quality gate assembly is split across `server/evaluation/quality-*.js`; keep `quality-report.js` as the compatibility export surface, result file I/O in `quality-result-reader.js`, run summaries in `quality-run-summary.js`, per-eval gates in their matching `quality-*-gate.js` modules, and final gate composition in `quality-combined-gate.js`.
- AgentRAG optimization order is documented in `docs/agent-rag.md` under "AgentRAG 优化路线"; continue in that order unless the user explicitly reprioritizes it.
- `/chat` returns `agentObservability` with per-skill selected status, attempts, duration, citations, abstain, retry/follow-up, budget, execution loop, working memory, skill chain, clarification gate, and error metrics. Preserve it when changing agent execution, feedback records, or feedback corpus metadata.
- API auth is controlled by `API_AUTH_ENABLED` plus either `API_AUTH_TOKEN` for single-token local use or `API_AUTH_TOKENS` for per-user/per-workspace token mapping; the frontend can send `VITE_API_AUTH_TOKEN`, which becomes an `x-api-key` header.
- When auth is enabled, document list/chat/delete/file access is filtered by the scope `resolveUserId` and `resolveWorkspaceId` (`server/auth.js`) resolve: the token's own `userId` and `workspaceId` only where the token has them (the single shared `API_AUTH_TOKEN`, for example, takes the user and workspace the request names); keep new document routes scoped the same way.
- Browser edge (2026-11-16): helmet sends `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` (since 2026-11-17; nothing frames the app or an API URL from another origin, and the PDF preview frames a `blob:` URL), so another site cannot frame the SPA and click "Clear workspace" for the user; the sidebar also asks before deleting a document or clearing the workspace. `server/cross-site-guard.js` runs right after CORS in `createApp`, ahead of the static frontend, the probes and every route. With auth off, every request (browser or not: a rebinding page's same-origin GET carries no Origin, and browsers send no `Sec-Fetch-*` to a non-localhost http URL) must name loopback, an IP address or an `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` hostname in Host and in a trusted `X-Forwarded-Host`: 403 `HOST_NOT_ALLOWED` (DNS rebinding). A browser request (`Origin` or `Sec-Fetch-Site` present) with a method other than GET/HEAD/OPTIONS needs a same-origin or `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` Origin (`null` never; no Origin + `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` is refused): 403 `CROSS_SITE_REQUEST_REFUSED`. No GET may change data: GET `/chat` was removed for that reason (an `<img>` on `http://0.0.0.0:5001` sends neither header). An empty `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` gives CORS `LOOPBACK_ORIGIN_PATTERN`, not every origin. `server/.env.example` ships `API_AUTH_TOKEN=` empty; with auth on, `assertApiAuthTokenConfiguration` refuses the start for the old public placeholder `change-me-for-local-dev` (`API_AUTH_TOKEN_PLACEHOLDER`) and an HS256 secret under `MIN_SERVICE_KEY_SECRET_LENGTH` (`API_AUTH_JWT_SECRET_TOO_SHORT`); HS256 JWTs need a numeric `exp` (numeric `nbf`/`iat` when present). Per-IP limits classify with `classifyTenantLimitRequest` (one route-cost table; the per-IP destructive budget applies to `TENANT_DESTRUCTIVE_CLASS_ROUTES` only, so a single DELETE keeps the global limit). `/health` and `/ready` share one in-flight build and reuse a report for `HEALTH_REPORT_REUSE_MS` (1 s). Tests: `test/cross-site-guard.test.mjs`, `test/ip-rate-limit-app.test.mjs`, `test/system-health-memo.test.mjs`, `test/auth-jwt.test.mjs`. No flag turns any of this off.
- Who may choose the user per request (`x-user-id` / `userId` in a header, body, or query): only auth off, the single `API_AUTH_TOKEN`, and an `API_AUTH_TOKENS` entry with `"allowClientUserId": true` (the JSON literal only). A principal with its own userId (token entry, JWT, OIDC) acts as that user and ignores a client value; any other principal without one gets 403 when the request names a user (the `GET /admin/audit?userId=` filter and the target `userId` of the tenant erase and export, `TENANT_TARGET_PATHS`, are exempt). The decision is `resolveUserId` in `server/auth.js`; routes must use `resolveRequestUserId` (via `resolveScopedUserId` in `routes/helpers.js`) and trigger dispatch uses `resolveTriggerUserId`, never a raw body/query/payload userId. Tests: `test/auth-user-id.test.mjs`, `test/auth-user-id-trigger.test.mjs`.
- An empty or whitespace-only numeric env value (`VAR=` in `.env`) counts as unset and the default applies; an explicit `0` still means 0. `toPositiveNumber` / `toNonNegativeNumber` in `server/rag/config.js` go through `parseEnvNumber`; use them for new numeric settings. `test/config-empty-env.test.mjs` sets every variable `config.js` reads to `""`, `"   "` and `"\t"` and compares every getter with the unset result.
- Tenantless scopes: with auth on, `requireApiAuth` refuses (403) an authenticated request whose scope names neither a user nor a workspace (`requireTenantScope` in `server/auth.js`), because the document registry, feedback and ingest-job filters and `toDatabaseTenant` all read an empty scope as every tenant (unscoped context, owner path) (owner path, no RLS). Example: an `API_AUTH_TOKENS` entry with two `workspaces` and no userId, sent without `x-workspace-id`. Exceptions: the single shared `API_AUTH_TOKEN` (single-tenant setup, whole archive as with auth off), `GET /auth/me` for every principal (it reads no tenant store; a credential with only a workspace list asks it without `x-workspace-id` to learn that list), and, for a principal without a workspace list only, the platform paths in `TENANTLESS_PLATFORM_PATHS` and `TENANTLESS_PLATFORM_PATH_PREFIXES` (for example `GET /admin/status|/admin/audit|/admin/index-versions` and `POST /admin/actions/:action`). `createApp` refuses to start on an `API_AUTH_TOKENS` entry with no userId, workspaceId, workspaces, roles or permissions and no `allowClientUserId` (`assertApiAuthTokenConfiguration`, `API_AUTH_TOKEN_WITHOUT_SCOPE`). A new platform-wide route must join `TENANTLESS_PLATFORM_PATHS` deliberately. `API_AUTH_REQUIRE_WORKSPACE=true` waives its workspace requirement on exactly those paths for a principal without a workspace list, and on `GET /auth/me` for every principal, so the SPA's workspace switcher can read a list before one is chosen (`waivesWorkspaceRequirement`), and the probes' permission decision never asks for one; before, the setting refused every platform admin, so no workspace could be registered (`test/admin-tenants-routes.test.mjs`, `test/health-access.test.mjs`). `test/tenant-isolation-route-sweep.test.mjs` walks `RBAC_ROUTE_TABLE` with such principals and checks cross-tenant object access per route.
- Probes and tenant-data caching: with API auth on, `GET /health`, `/ready` and `/livez` (public, status codes unchanged, `Cache-Control: no-store`) return only `{status}` unless the caller presents the single shared `API_AUTH_TOKEN` (`presentsSingleTenantCredential`; it already acts for the whole archive) or holds `admin.status.read` (decided like `GET /admin/status`, RBAC enforce included, not audited; `resolveOptionalAccessScope` in `auth.js`); auth off is unchanged. Probes read only status codes. The retrieval tier's `/health` and `/ready` and the model gateway's `/health` answer the same status codes to everyone but return the report (vector store, index versions, query adapter, topology; replicas, guard state, quotas; key ids) only to a valid internal identity token for that tier from an issuer `SERVICE_CALL_POLICY` lets probe it, system tokens included (`createOptionalServiceIdentityReader` in `service-identity.js`: requireServiceIdentity's probe checks, binding and replay cache, never answering itself); anyone else, the image HEALTHCHECK and compose healthchecks included, gets `{status}`. A caller that needs the details signs (`createServiceClient(...).request({ path: "/health", system: true })`); internal ports still stay off public networks. Every response after `requireApiAuth` sends `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and `Vary: Authorization, x-api-key, x-workspace-id, x-user-id` (`noStoreTenantData`, mounted app-wide in `app.js` after `bindDatabaseTenant`; `setTenantDataCacheHeaders` in `routes/helpers.js` for file sends); the static frontend and the probes are mounted before it. Input bounds: `/chat` and `/chat/stream` answer 400 for a question over `CHAT_MAX_QUESTION_CHARS` (8000), more than `CHAT_MAX_DOC_IDS` docIds (default: no limit in a process that retrieves itself, because the frontend sends every document in scope; 1000 with `RETRIEVAL_SERVICE_URL`, and with `AGENT_SERVICE_URL`, so an api edge refuses what its agent tier would instead of forwarding it; 0 = no limit), an id over `CHAT_MAX_ID_CHARS` (256) or non-string ids. The task, trigger and agent-run routes (`routes/tasks.js`) take the same bounds through exported validators (`validateAgentTaskRequest`, `validateTaskActionRequest`, `validateTriggerDispatchRequest`, ...) that `AGENT_EDGE_ROUTES` runs too: the agent-task question by `CHAT_MAX_QUESTION_CHARS`, its docIds as `/chat`'s, every id (path parameters, gate, session, user and idempotency ids with their `x-idempotency-key` / `x-request-id` headers, the `type` / `status` / `enabledOnly` list filters) by `CHAT_MAX_ID_CHARS`, at most 64 `userPreferences`, and every free-form JSON (action bodies, a trigger's body, an agent task's fields but docIds) by `TASK_MAX_PAYLOAD_BYTES` (256 KiB as JSON, 0 = no limit) and `MAX_PAYLOAD_DEPTH` (32, `routes/validation.js`); 400 `{ error }` as `/chat`. A new task route needs a validator in the same table. Both sparse routes score at most the first 64 distinct query terms, selected once in `rag/sparse-query-terms.js` (`selectSparseQueryTerms`; pgvector's `buildTsQuery` and the local BM25 store's `searchSparseDocuments`), so local/pgvector parity holds for any query length. No question in `server/evaluation` corpora or cases has more than 37 distinct terms, so the cap changed no deterministic eval; the agent's follow-up repair queries can pass 64 and is now cut on the local store as on pgvector. Chunked upload sessions are capped per (user, workspace) (`UPLOAD_SESSION_MAX_PER_TENANT` 200, `UPLOAD_SESSION_MAX_BYTES_PER_TENANT` 10 GiB, both well above a bulk drop because the workbench opens one session per file at once; 429 `UPLOAD_SESSION_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, resumes never refused, per-process count) and swept every `UPLOAD_SESSION_CLEANUP_INTERVAL_MS` (15 min; `startUploadSessionSweeper`, stopped by `app.locals.stop`). The archive MCP server exits with `ARCHIVE_MCP_SCOPE_REQUIRED` when neither `ARCHIVE_MCP_USER_ID` nor `ARCHIVE_MCP_WORKSPACE_ID` is set under a multi-tenant auth configuration (its own environment). Claim-judge verdict keys start with a hash of the tenant (the call's `accessScope`, else the active database tenant), in process and in Redis, and Redis stores only `{supported}`.
- OIDC and RBAC (both off by default; with `API_AUTH_OIDC_ENABLED` and `RBAC_MODE` unset, auth is exactly static tokens / HS256 JWT / off). Variables are listed in `docs/configuration.md` ("OIDC", "RBAC"); IdP setup in `docs/deployment.md`.
  - **OIDC** (`server/rag/oidc.js`, needs `API_AUTH_ENABLED=true`): `requireApiAuth` (`auth.js`) also accepts access tokens from `API_AUTH_OIDC_ISSUER` (exact match with discovery; https, http for loopback only) with `aud` = `API_AUTH_OIDC_AUDIENCE`, `azp`/`client_id` = `API_AUTH_OIDC_CLIENT_ID` when set, `exp` required, `typ at+jwt` when `API_AUTH_OIDC_REQUIRE_TYP=true`. Only RS256/PS256/ES256/EdDSA, checked against the JWK's kty/crv/alg; HS*, none, oct keys, `crit` and RSA under 2048 bits are refused. Dispatch: a static token match wins; with OIDC on, an HS* JWT goes to the HS256 verifier while `API_AUTH_JWT_ENABLED` is on and every other JWS goes to OIDC. The HS256 provider id stays `"jwt"` (not `jwt_hs256`). Failures are 401 `Unauthorized.`, 503 when no key was ever fetched, 500 for configuration errors; bodies never carry the token. The existing revocation lists apply through `getAuthTokenRevocation` (`auth-jwt.js`).
  - **JWKS cache**: discovery and keys live for `API_AUTH_OIDC_JWKS_TTL_MS`; an unknown `kid` triggers at most one refresh per `API_AUTH_OIDC_JWKS_MIN_REFRESH_MS` (concurrent requests share it), so a rotated-in key is picked up at once; a key removed from JWKS keeps verifying until the cached set expires (one TTL); an unreachable IdP keeps the last good keys indefinitely. The default verifier is rebuilt (empty cache) only when the OIDC configuration changes; tests call `resetDefaultOidcVerifier()`.
  - **Claim mapping**: user (`sub`), `workspace_id`, `workspaces`, `roles` plus groups mapped through `API_AUTH_OIDC_GROUP_ROLE_MAP`, `permissions`, and `workspace_roles` (`{ws: [roles]}` or `[{workspaceId, roles}]`), all renameable, dotted paths allowed (a claim name that itself contains a dot cannot be addressed). `accessScope.workspaceRoles` (lowercased) is set only when non-empty, from that claim or from static `API_AUTH_TOKENS` entries (`workspaceRoles`/`workspace_roles`). Workspace selection is the unchanged `resolveWorkspaceId`: a token with neither `workspaces` nor `workspace_id` may pick any workspace through `x-workspace-id`. Workspace ids are authorized case-insensitively: when the principal lists allowed workspaces, `resolveWorkspaceId` returns the canonical lower-cased id as the database tenant (a header "ACME" for a token limited to "acme" lands in "acme"); without a list the header is the tenant as sent, and `workspaceRoles` keys (stored lower-cased) apply only when the tenant equals them exactly, so a role granted in "acme" never applies in "ACME". `normalizeWorkspaceRoles` builds through a Map so a workspace named like an Object.prototype member is ordinary data. `test/auth-security-review.test.mjs` pins all three.
  - **RBAC** (`server/rag/rbac.js`, `RBAC_MODE=off|enforce`, anything else refuses the start through `assertRbacConfiguration` in `createApp`): permissions `documents.read/write/delete`, `chat.ask`, `tasks.run`, `memory.read/write`, `quality.feedback` plus every `ADMIN_PERMISSION_IDS` value (not redefined); roles `workspace.viewer` (read, chat), `workspace.member` (+ write, tasks, memory, feedback), `workspace.admin` (+ delete, and `admin.status.read` only as a global role), the existing `admin.*` roles, `admin.owner` = everything. `RBAC_POLICY_JSON` / `RBAC_POLICY_FILE` replace or add roles; an unknown permission id refuses the start. Effective permissions = global roles/permissions ∪ the active workspace's `workspaceRoles` (workspace permissions only; `admin.*` never comes from a workspace role) ∪ `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE` (default `workspace.member`, `none` disables) only when the principal has no workspace permission of its own, so it never raises an explicit viewer. RBAC grants actions, not visibility: documents stay owner/workspace filtered.
  - **Route table**: `RBAC_ROUTE_TABLE` in `rbac.js` maps every method + Express path to a permission, `public` or `authenticated`; `/admin/actions/:action` resolves per action. `test/rbac-routes.test.mjs` walks `app._router.stack` and fails for any mounted route without an entry that resolves to itself, and for any `AGENT_EDGE_ROUTES` entry missing from the table, so a new route needs a table entry in the same change. In enforce mode a path outside the table is 403 (not 404).
  - **Enforcement and audit**: `createRbacMiddleware` is mounted app-wide after `requireApiAuth` / `bindDatabaseTenant`; off mode is `next()`. A denial is 403 `{code: "RBAC_PERMISSION_DENIED", error: "Forbidden.", permission: <id>|null}` and is recorded through `adminAuditService.recordAuthorizationDecision` with ids only (reasons `rbac_*`). `admin-authorization.js` additionally allows an admin permission RBAC grants in enforce mode only (`allowed_by_rbac`). `GET /auth/me` (`routes/auth-me.js`) returns `{userId, workspaceId, workspaceIds, roles, permissions, authProvider, rbacMode}` for the active workspace with `no-store`; `GET /auth/config` (`routes/auth-config.js`, public, mounted before the rate limiter and auth) returns `{mode, oidc}` with no secrets.
  - **Split mode**: the api edge enforces RBAC before forwarding; the agent tier does not mount RBAC and trusts the edge's signed identity, which does not carry `workspaceRoles`. Do not start enforcing ordinary RBAC permissions on the agent tier without forwarding that field.
  - **Dev IdP**: `server/dev-oidc-provider.mjs` (`npm run oidc:dev`, `startDevOidcProvider({ port: 0, users })` in tests) is for development and tests only: 127.0.0.1, refuses `NODE_ENV=production`, auto-approves `login_hint`, PKCE S256 required, single-use codes, rotating refresh tokens, `POST /admin/rotate-keys` behind a per-start secret. Never wire it into compose or the image.
  - **SPA** (`src/auth/`): `GET /auth/config` wins over `VITE_OIDC_*`; Authorization Code + PKCE as a public client; access/ID tokens in memory, a refresh token only in sessionStorage; one refresh-and-retry on a bearer 401; UI gating by `/auth/me` permissions in `src/auth/permissions.js` (keep its ids aligned with `rbac.js`). Workspace switcher (`src/auth/WorkspaceProvider.jsx`, `WorkspaceSwitcher.jsx`, `workspaceSelection.js`): shown only when a header-less `/auth/me` lists more than one workspace and names no fixed one (never with auth off); the choice goes as `x-workspace-id` on every request through `buildApiRequestConfig` (`omitWorkspaceHeader: true` skips it), is kept in localStorage (`archive-rag.workspace-id`, every access guarded; a stored id `/auth/me` no longer lists is dropped), and a switch unmounts the app, rotates the chat session id, calls AuthProvider's `reloadMe` under OIDC, and remounts it keyed by workspace so nothing of the previous workspace stays on screen. With `/auth/me` `workspaceRequired` the no-workspace choice is disabled and, without a valid remembered choice, the first listed workspace is selected and remembered (`describeWorkspaceChoice().fallback`). An id with a character above U+00FF is never offered, remembered or sent (a header must be a ByteString; fetch throws otherwise). A logical request reads the workspace once (`sendWithAuth`, `streamChat`) and passes it as `buildApiRequestConfig({ workspaceId })` to the retry after a 401 too, so a switch during the token refresh never redirects the retry; only Authorization is rebuilt.
  - **Tests**: `test/oidc.test.mjs`, `test/oidc-dev-provider.test.mjs`, `test/auth-security-review.test.mjs`, `test/rbac.test.mjs`, `test/rbac-routes.test.mjs`, and `test/oidc-rbac.e2e.test.mjs` (dev IdP + the real server as a child process with a local vector store and a fake model: PKCE login over HTTP, viewer/member/workspace-admin decisions, `/auth/me`, key rotation with the JWKS TTL, expired and ID tokens rejected, and the same `/chat` through an api edge in front of an agent tier). Frontend: `src/auth/*.test.js(x)`.
- PostgreSQL row-level security (migration `013_enable_tenant_row_level_security.sql`, `POSTGRES_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY=enforce` by default) backs those filters. `bindDatabaseTenant` (mounted after `requireApiAuth`) puts the request's access scope in `server/rag/postgres-tenant.js`'s `AsyncLocalStorage`; `server/rag/postgres.js` then runs each scoped statement in a short transaction that `SET LOCAL ROLE`s to the tenant role and sets `archive_rag.user_id` / `archive_rag.workspace_id`. Do not pass scopes into the database layer or open a raw session under a tenant (`withPostgresClient` throws). Background work binds the record's own scope with `runWithDatabaseTenant` (task runs, per-run startup recovery). Work that is system-wide by nature must be wrapped in `runAsDatabaseSystem`: migrations, filling process-wide caches (the document registry map), pgvector schema verification and status, and cross-tenant recovery listings; otherwise a tenant request would, for example, load only its own documents into the shared registry. A middleware that resumes the chain from a stream callback loses the context (multer memory storage does), so re-mount `bindDatabaseTenant` after it. Under RLS a non-leakproof operator such as full-text `@@` cannot be an index condition, so a tenant's multi-document `ts_rank_cd` search with pruning off (`RAG_SPARSE_PRUNE_DF_FRACTION=off`) goes through `<chunks table>_sparse_rank` (migration 014): a `SECURITY DEFINER` function owned by the table owner, `search_path` pinned, `enable_indexscan = off`, `ROWS 20`, EXECUTE for the tenant role only. (BM25, the default, and a multi-document `ts_rank_cd` search with pruning, which is on by default, go through migration 030's `<t>_sparse_search` instead, `usesPgvectorSparseSearchFunction`; see the BM25 bullets below.) `buildTenantSparseSearchSql` hands it only the doc ids the tenant can see in the documents table, then joins its chunk ids back to the chunks table as the tenant, so another tenant's chunks can neither take a place under its LIMIT nor come back. A single document keeps the plain statement under `ts_rank_cd`. The health check's `rowLevelSecurity` entry fails when the tenant role cannot execute the function (or, when BM25 or pruning sends searches there, `<t>_sparse_search`), when the tenant role is SUPERUSER or BYPASSRLS, when it has the privileges of any table's owner in the schema (owners skip ENABLE-only policies), and when it holds any privilege on a table without a `tenant_isolation` policy (allowlist: migration 016's index version registry and pointer; relations an extension owns, such as the PUBLIC-granted `pg_stat_statements` views, are left out of both scans). Admin audit events (migration 032) are under RLS too: a tenant reads its own workspace's events (its own user's without a workspace) and may only INSERT events naming itself; UPDATE and DELETE are revoked, so the retention purge runs under `runAsDatabaseSystem`, and `GET /admin/audit` for a platform admin (no workspace in the scope and no workspace list) reads on the owner path after the permission check. Inside a tenant request the PostgreSQL audit store writes the bound tenant's own ids into `user_id`/`workspace_id` (the principal JSON keeps the compacted ids), because `compactAdminAuditEvent` collapses whitespace runs and the tenant settings only trim. Do not widen what the function returns beyond chunk ids and ranks, and do not call it outside that query. A new application table needs a `GRANT` to the tenant role in its migration and, if tenant-owned, a `tenant_isolation` policy; `checks.rowLevelSecurity` in the health report probes the role switch and every covered policy, and `test/postgres-row-level-security.integration.test.mjs` runs as a non-superuser owner in its own throwaway database.
- Tenant context states (`rag/postgres-tenant.js`): `tenant` (SET LOCAL ROLE tenant), `system` (`runAsDatabaseSystem`), `unscoped` (`runWithDatabaseTenant` / `bindDatabaseTenant` with a scope naming no tenant: auth off, the single shared token, platform admin paths) and `unbound` (nothing bound). `getActiveDatabaseTenant()` is null outside `tenant`. `POSTGRES_TENANT_CONTEXT=strict` (default `lenient`; unknown values refuse) makes an `unbound` statement throw `ARCHIVE_RAG_TENANT_CONTEXT_MISSING` under RLS enforce instead of running as the owner; every start-up, background, CLI, metrics and health path that queries PostgreSQL binds `runAsDatabaseSystem` (or the record's tenant) so strict mode runs clean. A new such path must bind too; `test/postgres-strict-tenant-context-sweep.test.mjs` (fake PostgreSQL) and `test/postgres-strict-tenant-context.e2e.test.mjs` (real `node server.js`, needs `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL`) catch misses. Evaluation runners query unbound, so never set strict in a `.env` that evals read. Tenant statements and transactions also carry `POSTGRES_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS` / `POSTGRES_IDLE_IN_TRANSACTION_TIMEOUT_MS` (default 0 = server value) as bind values `$4`/`$5` of the same settings step (NULL keeps the current value; same round trips); `POSTGRES_POOL_MAX` (10) and `POSTGRES_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT_MS` (0) size the primary pool. `withPooledClient` keeps an error listener on a leased client, and both the runtime and the owner pool carry a pool-level `error` listener (`watchIdleClientErrors`) for idle clients, so a server-ended session (restart, failover, `pg_terminate_backend`) cannot crash the process.
- PostgreSQL TLS (`rag/postgres-tls.js` `buildPostgresSslOption`, used by the runtime and owner pools and the dedicated LISTEN client in `rag/postgres.js` and by the replica read and poll pools in `rag/postgres-replicas.js`; a new pool or `pg.Client` in server code must take its `ssl` from there): with `POSTGRES_SSL_ENABLED` off nothing changes (no `ssl` option; the URL's own `sslmode` applies as node-pg reads it). With it on, `POSTGRES_SSL_MODE` is `verify-full` (the default, an intended change from the old unconditional `rejectUnauthorized: false`), `verify-ca` (chain only through a `checkServerIdentity` that accepts any name; requires `POSTGRES_SSL_CA_FILE`) or `no-verify` (the old option, byte-identical); `POSTGRES_SSL_CA_FILE` (PEM) is read when a pool is built, and every certificate in it must parse (`X509Certificate`; a truncated, damaged or unterminated one is `POSTGRES_SSL_CA_FILE_INVALID`, so health no longer passes a bundle every handshake would fail). The replica configuration (TLS option and CA PEM included) is cached by the variables' text and cleared by `resetReadReplicas` (so by `resetPostgresPool`). An unknown mode refuses even with TLS off (`POSTGRES_SSL_MODE_INVALID`), and with TLS on a connection string (runtime, migration or replica URL) carrying `ssl`, `sslmode`, `sslrootcert`, `sslcert`, `sslkey` or `uselibpqcompat` is refused (`POSTGRES_SSL_CONNECTION_STRING_CONFLICT`), because node-pg's `ConnectionParameters` lays the parsed URL over the explicit options and would silently replace the mode. Replica TLS errors are configuration errors (every read-only statement throws, `checks.readReplicas` is error). `checks.postgresTls` appears only with TLS on or an invalid mode: ok for `verify-*`, warning for `no-verify`, error when a connection would be refused. Evaluation runners' own `pg.Client`s on disposable clusters do not use it. Tests: `test/postgres-tls.test.mjs` (option building, the node-pg override it guards against, every pool; no real TLS server).
- Migrations run on one owner session under the session advisory lock `archive_rag:schema_migrations` (`rag/db-migrations.js`, since 2026-11-19): replicas started together for a release wait for the first one instead of failing on a duplicate key; concurrent callers in one process share one run. The owner connection must be direct or session-pooled (a PgBouncer transaction pool can split the lock and the unlock across server sessions). Split roles write the tenant usage ledger right after `app.locals.stop`, before the span flush (`role-server.js`).
- Database role split (opt-in, `POSTGRES_MIGRATION_DATABASE_URL`; default off and byte-identical on the wire): the owner login (migration URL) runs migrations and every DDL (`queryPostgresAsOwner`, `withPostgresOwnerClient`, `withPostgresOwnerTransaction` in `rag/postgres.js`, `getPgvectorRuntime().withOwnerTransaction`; owner pool of at most 2 connections, `system` context only, else `ARCHIVE_RAG_OWNER_WORK_CONTEXT`), and `POSTGRES_DATABASE_URL` becomes a runtime login that owns nothing and holds no table privileges. Routing with the split: `tenant` as before; `system` and `unscoped` prepend `set_config('role', <POSTGRES_SYSTEM_ROLE>, true)` in the same round trip (BEGIN's round trip for a transaction); with RLS off tenant chains also run as the system role; `unbound` runs as the runtime login and fails with 42501 (strict mode throws first). `withPostgresClient` refuses under the split (`ARCHIVE_RAG_ROLE_SPLIT_RAW_SESSION`) so no pooled session can keep acting as the system. Migration 035 creates the NOLOGIN system role (default `archive_rag_system`, must differ from the tenant role) even with the split off; a migration login without CREATEROLE fails it with a hint until a DBA runs `CREATE ROLE archive_rag_system NOLOGIN` (an upgrade step for least-privilege deployments; skipping instead was rejected because every version table's DDL grants the role), grants it the owner path's DML (audit: SELECT/INSERT/DELETE; feedback: SELECT/INSERT), and adds `system_access` policies on every RLS table; chunk tables go through the owner-only `<base>_install_system_access(table)`, which version builds call. The migrator grants the tenant and system roles to the runtime login (`session_user` of a runtime connection) `WITH INHERIT FALSE, SET TRUE` on every run with the split on. `checks.rowLevelSecurity.roleSplit` is an error when the runtime login is SUPERUSER/BYPASSRLS, a member of an owner, or can use a table without switching role, or a covered table lacks `system_access`; without the split it is a `warning` field that never changes the entry's status. A new table needs a system-role grant and a `system_access` policy in its migration. `deploy/postgres/create-split-roles.sql` creates the two logins for a fresh database; compose defaults are unchanged. The split stops a lost context or a forgotten binding, not injected SQL (the runtime login can still switch into the system role). Tests: `test/postgres-role-split.test.mjs`, `test/postgres-role-split.integration.test.mjs` (needs `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL`).
- Tenant registry and quotas (tenant isolation phase 3; everything opt-in or unlimited by default unless noted):
  - **Registry and membership** (migration 036, `rag/tenant-registry.js`, `routes/admin-tenants.js`, `npm run tenant:registry`): `rag_workspaces` (status active|suspended|deleting, plan, region, retention_policy jsonb) and `rag_workspace_members` (roles text[]); the tenant role may only SELECT its own workspace row and its own memberships, writes go through the system path. `TENANT_MEMBERSHIP=enforce` (default `off`, unknown value refuses start) makes `requireApiAuth` refuse a workspace the user is not a member of (403 `WORKSPACE_MEMBERSHIP_REQUIRED`, same answer whether the workspace exists), a suspended/deleting/unregistered workspace (`WORKSPACE_SUSPENDED` / `WORKSPACE_DELETING` / `WORKSPACE_NOT_REGISTERED`), and a workspace a principal without a workspace list names by header; a registry fault is 503 `TENANT_REGISTRY_UNAVAILABLE`, never allow. `GET /auth/me` under enforce appends the user's active memberships to `workspaceIds` for a credential that fixes no workspace and lists none (`listRequestRegistryWorkspaces` in `auth.js`, `registry.listMemberWorkspaces`: memberships read bound to the user without a workspace, statuses through `lookupWorkspace`, nothing as the system; a registry fault is 503); with API auth on and `API_AUTH_REQUIRE_WORKSPACE` it adds `workspaceRequired: true` (absent otherwise, so other configurations answer as before). New workspace ids must be printable ASCII (`normalizeWorkspaceInput`, creation only: they travel in the `x-workspace-id` header, a ByteString; ids registered before keep working). Membership roles merge into `accessScope.workspaceRoles` (more than 32 for the workspace is 403 `WORKSPACE_ROLES_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, the forwarded-scope bound). A platform principal (no fixed workspace, no list) naming a workspace on `GET /admin/audit` or `/admin/usage` only narrows the view and is not membership-checked (`WORKSPACE_FILTER_PLATFORM_PATHS`). Not checked: auth off, the single shared token, user-only scopes, platform admins that name no workspace or name one on `GET /admin/audit` or `/admin/usage`, MCP servers. Lookups are cached per process (`TENANT_REGISTRY_CACHE_TTL_MS`, 5000); `TENANT_REGISTRY_CACHE_SCOPE=tenant` (default `shared`) caches workspace lookups per requesting user, so a non-member's 403 timing tells nothing about other principals' requests. Admin API `/admin/workspaces[/:workspaceId[/actions/:action|/members[/:userId]]]` (plus `PATCH /admin/workspaces/:workspaceId` for the retention policy) behind `admin.tenants.read|write` (role `admin.tenant_manager`; admin.operator does not hold them); only platform principals create workspaces or change status or retention policy.
  - **Per-tenant HTTP limits** (`rag/tenant-limits.js`, mounted after `noStoreTenantData`, before RBAC): `TENANT_RATE_LIMIT_ENABLED` fixed windows per tenant key (workspace, else user, else IP) and cost class (chat / ingest (uploads, upload status, replace, restore, arXiv imports) / destructive (every DELETE plus the documents clear and the tenant erase) / default), 429 `TENANT_RATE_LIMITED`; `TENANT_MAX_CONCURRENT_RUNS` per-process cap on agent-running requests, 429 `TENANT_CONCURRENCY_LIMITED`; shared through Redis under `RAG_SHARED_STATE=redis`. `TRUST_PROXY` (hops, true/false or an address list; invalid refuses start) feeds `trust proxy`; `true` trusts the client-written X-Forwarded-For.
  - **Model fairness and quotas**: the model-call guard's wait queue is fair per tenant (least recently served, FIFO within a tenant; the default, identical for one tenant), waiters observe their abort signal, `RAG_LLM_MAX_QUEUE` bounds one tenant's queue (429 `MODEL_CALL_QUEUE_FULL`); ingest document embeddings share the endpoint's guard by default and get their own lane, cap and circuit only when `RAG_LLM_INGEST_MAX_CONCURRENCY` is set (`resolveModelCallLane`); the fair queue remembers each tenant's last grant across idle spells (up to `FAIR_QUEUE_MAX_TRACKED_TENANTS` 1024) so a returning tenant is not treated as new; breaker snapshots carry hashed `openedBy`. `MODEL_QUOTA_ENABLED=true` applies the gateway's per-workspace quotas (`MODEL_GATEWAY_QUOTA_*`, engine `rag/model-quotas.js`) in process to calls not routed to the gateway (429 `MODEL_GATEWAY_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`, `quotaEnforcedBy: "process"`). The ingest embedding batcher charges each job's tenant, for quotas and for the usage ledger (`runWithCallerAttributedModelUsage` keeps the merged call's system event out of the ledger and records per-tenant shares).
