  - **Usage ledger and storage/task caps** (migration 037, `rag/tenant-usage.js`, `GET /admin/usage` behind `admin.usage.read`): hourly per-tenant aggregates of model tokens and cost (mirror events skipped), chat requests, ingested bytes and documents, flushed asynchronously every `TENANT_USAGE_FLUSH_MS` (60000) on the system path and on shutdown of every entry point that records usage (`stopTenantUsageLedger`, or `flushTenantUsageOnExit` in a command that ends on its own: server.js/app.js, `startServiceRole`, ingest-worker.mjs, archive-ingest.mjs, the archive MCP server on stdin close, SIGTERM and SIGINT (`createArchiveMcpShutdown` in `archive-mcp-tools.js`, bounded at 5 s), vector-index.mjs, vector-reindex.mjs and `run-runtime-smoke.mjs`; a new entry point that makes model calls or ingests must too, `test/usage-ledger-shutdown-flush.test.mjs`; the model gateway needs the database for its ledger, which `compose.services.yml` now gives it) (`TENANT_USAGE_LEDGER`, default on, inert without a database; adds no statement to a /chat). `TENANT_MAX_DOCUMENTS` / `TENANT_MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES` per workspace (else user) are checked inside `commitDocument`'s write transaction under an advisory lock taken after the index write and content-hash locks (`<documents>_storage_totals`, SECURITY DEFINER), and early at upload; 429/413 `TENANT_STORAGE_QUOTA_EXCEEDED`. With a quota set, commits of one quota scope wait for an in-process turn before they take a connection, so a burst holds one pooled connection, and the quota count runs on the transaction's own client. `RAG_INGEST_MAX_PENDING_JOBS|BYTES_PER_WORKSPACE`, `TENANT_MAX_RUNNING_TASKS` (excess waits queued) and `TENANT_MAX_QUEUED_TASKS` (429 `TENANT_TASK_LIMITED`).
  - **Refusal accounting** (`rag/tenant-refusals.js` `recordTenantRefusal`, codes in `rag/tenant-refusal-reasons.js`): every per-tenant refusal (the rate/concurrency limits, the six membership codes in `requireApiAuth` (not the probes' optional scope), storage quota, model quota in process and at the gateway (each counted once, where it refused), `MODEL_CALL_QUEUE_FULL` per refused attempt, task cap, upload-session cap, and the ingest pending caps, now coded `INGEST_PENDING_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` / `INGEST_WORKSPACE_PENDING_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`) increments `archive_rag_tenant_refusals_total{reason}` and adds one `refused_<code in lower case>` row to the usage ledger for the tenant the refusal was decided for (`toDatabaseTenant`; an IP-keyed refusal is `('', '')`). A membership refusal keeps its (user, workspace) pair only when it shows the requester belongs to the workspace (`WORKSPACE_SUSPENDED`, `WORKSPACE_ROLES_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`: `resolveMembershipRefusalTenant` in `rag/tenant-registry.js`) and the registry still holds it and it is not `deleting` (`resolveRefusalLedgerScope` in `auth.js`). Every other one (a non-member of a registered workspace too, `WORKSPACE_NOT_REGISTERED`, `WORKSPACE_DELETING`, `TENANT_REGISTRY_UNAVAILABLE`) is counted for the user alone `(userId, '')`, and without a user in the metric only (`recordTenantRefusal(..., { metricOnly: true })`), so a made-up `x-workspace-id` never becomes ledger rows, a non-member's user id never shows in the named workspace's usage view, export or erase, and an erased workspace's id does not come back with a former member's refused request (`test/tenant-refusals.test.mjs`, the hardened e2e). Rows written before this rule keep their pair until erased or swept (`TENANT_USAGE_RETENTION_DAYS`). No migration: the ledger is already one row per (tenant, metric, hour), so the flush, the tenant policy, erase and export cover the rows unchanged. Refusals may hold at most `TENANT_USAGE_MAX_PENDING_REFUSAL_ENTRIES` (10000) pending keys, and an id over 256 characters or with U+0000 is dropped and counted (`unstorableEntries`) instead of poisoning every flush batch. `GET /admin/usage?metric=refusals[&reason=<code>]` ranks tenants and adds `refusals` (count per code) to each row; other metrics keep their exact shape. A new per-tenant refusal must call the hook and add its code to the reasons module (`test/tenant-refusals.test.mjs` pins every code to its source constant).
  - **Split deployments**: the signed scope carries `workspaceRoles` of the active workspace only (bounded by `MAX_FORWARDED_WORKSPACE_ROLES` 32 / `MAX_FORWARDED_ROLE_ID_LENGTH` 128; an older edge sends none), and the agent tier mounts `requireForwardedTenantScope` (403 `AGENT_TENANT_SCOPE_REQUIRED` for an authenticated scope naming neither user nor workspace, except admin actions and the edge's `tenantlessScope: "single_tenant_credential"` claim); upgrade the edge before the agent tier.
- Tenant lifecycle and audit (tenant isolation phase 4):
  - **Erase and export** (`rag/tenant-lifecycle.js`, `rag/tenant-records.js`, `POST /admin/tenants/erase`, `GET /admin/tenants/export`, `npm run tenant:erase|tenant:export`; permissions `admin.tenants.erase|export`, admin.owner only, platform principals only, API auth required): erase walks every store in a fixed order (`TENANT_STORE_IDS`: ingest jobs and temp files, upload sessions, tasks, agent runs, documents with chunks in every version table, artifacts, feedback, session and long memory, Redis judge keys, semantic cache, then the usage ledger (rows and unflushed totals) and the registry last) as system work; a workspace erase first marks the workspace `deleting` (so `TENANT_MEMBERSHIP=enforce` refuses its requests) and deletes its memberships and row last, and a failed erase leaves it `deleting` for a rerun; dry run by default (`dryRun: false` / `--apply`), refuses running work with 409 `TENANT_ERASE_ACTIVE_WORK` unless forced, keeps audit events and writes an `admin.tenant.erased` tombstone (ids and counts in the `details` column of migration 038, which also grants the system role DELETE on feedback). Export streams NDJSON of the tenant's records (document bytes base64) under the tenant's RLS. Selector: workspace only = every user of it; workspace + user = that pair; user only = the user's records outside any workspace; long memory under `LONG_MEMORY_SCOPE=user` by every row of a named user, and for a workspace-only selector by the rows that carry the workspace (written while the scope was `workspace`; before, they outlived a workspace erase after a switch back to `user`; the `userWide` table option of `rag/tenant-records.js`, whose export reads the user's rows per workspace they carry, each bound to that tenant), under `workspace` by the (user, `workspace_id`) pair like the rest (a user-only selector takes the rows of no workspace: user-level and personal; `longMemoryAppliesToTenantSelector` is true for every selector); a userId that is given but blank is 400 `TENANT_SELECTOR_INVALID` (only an absent one means the whole workspace). The CLI leaves documents of a local vector index or a non-PostgreSQL registry out (a running server would write them back) unless `--servers-stopped`. An export the client aborts still records `admin.tenant.exported` with outcome `aborted`. Other processes' in-memory caches are not reached.
  - **Retention** (`rag/retention.js`, `rag/retention-sql.js`; all default 0 = keep forever): `AGENT_RUN_RETENTION_DAYS` (finished runs with events and snapshots; never running/waiting ones), `TASK_RETENTION_DAYS` (finished tasks), `WORKSPACE_ARTIFACT_RETENTION_DAYS` (archived artifacts only), `FEEDBACK_RETENTION_DAYS` (PostgreSQL only; migration 039 grants the delete), `RAG_OBSERVABILITY_RETENTION_DAYS` (daily-rotated JSONL segments), `TENANT_USAGE_RETENTION_DAYS` (usage ledger rows of every tenant and metric, `refused_*` included, by the start of their hour; API tier, no workspace policy key; `deleteExpiredTenantUsage` in `rag/tenant-usage.js`, under the system role's existing migration 037 grant). Swept every `RETENTION_SWEEP_INTERVAL_MS` (1 h) in batches of `RETENTION_SWEEP_BATCH_SIZE` (500) under `runAsDatabaseSystem` with a per-table `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` and `SKIP LOCKED`; runs and tasks by the agent tier, the rest by the API tier; a policy source that cannot be read (including a lost grant) makes the pass delete nothing from the database (`skipped: workspace_policies_unavailable`), never fall back to the global cutoffs; per-workspace overrides come only from the registry's `retention_policy` (`rag/workspace-retention-policy.js`: keys `agentRuns`, `tasks`, `workspaceArtifacts`, `feedback`, whole days 0-36500; the registry refuses any other key or value). It is set at creation and replaced whole by `PATCH /admin/workspaces/:workspaceId` (`{retentionPolicy}`, `admin.tenants.write`, platform principals only, any other body field refused, 409 while `deleting`) or `npm run tenant:registry -- set-retention <workspaceId> --retention-policy '<json>'` (`registry.setWorkspaceRetentionPolicy`; migration 036 already grants the system role UPDATE). Each sweep pass reads the policies from the store itself, never through the registry's lookup cache, so a change applies on every instance from its next pass (`TENANT_REGISTRY_CACHE_TTL_MS` does not delay it).
  - **Document deletion cascade** (`rag/document-deletion-cascade.js`, migration 040; after the delete commits, never fails it): always cancels the document's queued, retry-waiting and dead-letter ingest jobs (`canceledReason: "document_deleted"`) and removes its chunks from every live index version table (matters for PUT replace; delete and clear reach them by FK cascade). Always deletes the deleting scope's arXiv recommendation task for the document (`external_recommendation:arxiv:<docId>`); an import still running on it does not bring it back (a queued import job loses its claim, a direct import finds its task gone and writes no end state), and neither does an arXiv search that returns after the delete (`publishSuggestion` in `rag/arxiv-enrichment.js` answers 404 and writes no task or snapshot, and removes a task whose write landed after the cascade). The search's task write is a conditional upsert (`expected` in both task stores, `matchesExpectedTask` in `rag/tasks.js`, one statement in PostgreSQL): it lands only while the task still has the status and `updatedAt` the search read before it called arXiv, so an import confirmed meanwhile, queued, running or already ended, is never overwritten (409, which a search also gets when another search's result landed first; only two tabs can do that). The other way round is accepted: a confirmation checks its token against the task it read and then transitions on status alone, so a search that publishes during the confirmation (its registry read) is replaced by that import of the older offer, which the server issued too. A move into the trash does both of these at once. `DOCUMENT_DELETE_REDACTION=on` (default off) replaces copies of its text with `[source document deleted]` in the deleting tenant's settled runs (checkpoint included), cited artifacts (`source_deleted`) and feedback citations (system role may UPDATE only `citations`).
  - **Document trash** (migration 042, `DOCUMENT_TRASH_RETENTION_HOURS`, default 0 = immediate hard delete; `rag/document-trash.js`, the trash parts of `rag/index.js` and `rag/doc-registry.js`): above 0, DELETE and clear move documents into the trash in the delete's transaction order (index write lock, row FOR UPDATE, chunks out of every live version, then `deleted_at` / `deleted_by_user_id` / `deleted_by_workspace_id` set; bytes kept) and the cascade runs with `redact: false` (jobs cancelled, nothing redacted). Every active read adds `deleted_at IS NULL` whatever the setting (registry `list`/`listByIds`/`getFile`/`findByContentHash`/`lockForContentReplace`/`delete`/`clear`, the async enqueue's duplicate check, the index builder's pending list, source read, FOR SHARE lock and counts); only the trash calls, `listForTenant`/`eraseMany` (erase and export) and the storage quota see trashed rows, and `upsert` clears the mark. `GET /documents/trash` (documents.read) and `POST /documents/:docId/restore` (documents.delete, ingest cost class) are scoped to the caller; a restore re-parses the stored bytes and commits through `commitDocument` mode `restore` (pgvector only, content version +1 for the replica guard and the builder, no quota check or usage, 409 `DOCUMENT_RESTORE_DUPLICATE` under the hash lock when dedup is on and the tenant stored the same bytes again, 415 `DOCUMENT_RESTORE_RESERVED_PREFIX` when the stored plaintext begins with an envelope's or a working file's magic; an upload of trashed bytes creates a new document). The retention sweep's `document_trash` type (API tier, hours, no workspace policy, so a policy read failure does not block it) purges in system transactions (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, try-lock, chunks in every existing table, then the rows), then per deleting scope and in its database context runs the cascade with redaction and records `document.purge`. Data events: `document.trash`/`documents.trash` (destructive), `document.restore`, `document.purge`. Anything but pgvector with the PostgreSQL registry refuses the start (`DOCUMENT_TRASH_UNSUPPORTED`); setting it back to 0 leaves the trash hidden, restorable and unpurged. Tests: `test/document-trash.test.mjs`, `test/document-trash-postgres.integration.test.mjs`.
  - **One connection per document write**: everything inside a commit, delete, trash or restore runs on the write transaction's client (or, on local / Qdrant with the PostgreSQL registry, the content-hash lock's transaction, which the registration now rides); the registry map is loaded before any lock (`ensureDocumentRegistryInitialized`), the delete's scope check reads metadata on the caller's client, and `ensurePgvectorSchema` inside a write trusts the transaction's own write targets when they match the verified version instead of re-reading the pointer on the pool. A second pooled connection there deadlocks once `POSTGRES_POOL_MAX` writes wait for one (`POSTGRES_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT_MS=0` waits forever); `test/document-commit-pool.integration.test.mjs` pins pool max 2 with 6 concurrent writes.
  - **Audit hardening** (migration 041, `rag/admin-audit-chain.js`, `rag/data-access-audit.js`, `npm run audit:verify`): every audit row extends its chain (one per workspace, or per user without one, which is what 032 lets a tenant read) with `row_hash = sha256(prev_hash || canonical row)` under a per-chain advisory transaction lock in a transaction that pins READ COMMITTED in its BEGIN (`withPostgresTransaction(cb, { isolationLevel })`); `details` is hashed only when present; rows before 041 have no hash; `audit:verify` scans each store in one snapshot (a cursor in one transaction) and reports the first break, counting unhashed rows written by pre-041 instances during an upgrade window (`--upgrade-grace-hours`, default 24 from migration 041's `applied_at`; `--upgrade-window-end <ISO>`) as `upgradeRows`, not breaks. `ADMIN_AUDIT_DATA_EVENTS=on` (default off) records `data.access` events (document download/delete/clear/replace, memory and session clears, a platform admin's cross-tenant audit/status reads, tenant erase) with ids and counts only; destructive ones record an intent first and answer 503 `AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE` when it cannot be written. `API_AUTH_OIDC_ALLOW_PLATFORM_ROLES=false` (default true) drops admin.* roles and permissions an OIDC token or group mapping carries, and `describeEffectiveAccess` grants an OIDC principal no admin permission through its global roles either (the global `workspace.admin` -> `admin.status.read` path, custom RBAC roles).
- Tenant encryption at rest (tenant isolation phase 5; `TENANT_ENCRYPTION=on|off`, default off, any other value refuses the start; off writes exactly what it wrote before, and reads always accept both forms):
  - **Envelopes** (`rag/tenant-encryption.js`): per object a random data key, AES-256-GCM, wrapped by the tenant key; layout `\0ARTENC` + version 1 + header length + JSON header `{a, k: keyId, v: keyVersion, p: provider, w: wrappedKey}` + nonce + tag + ciphertext. Both GCM operations carry the record's context as AAD (owner workspace and user, object kind, object id), supplied by the reader from the row itself, so a ciphertext copied into another tenant's or another document's row fails (`TENANT_ENVELOPE_KEY_MISMATCH` / `TENANT_KEY_UNWRAP_FAILED`); the payload AAD leaves the header out, so `rewrap` changes only the header. Legacy plaintext (no magic) passes through unchanged. Every failure is a coded 500 (`rag/kms/errors.js`: `TENANT_ENVELOPE_AUTH_FAILED|MALFORMED`, `TENANT_KEY_DESTROYED|NOT_FOUND`, ...), never garbage.
  - **Key scope** (`rag/kms/key-scope.js`): one key per workspace, else per user outside any workspace, else one `unscoped` key (auth off, the single shared token); key ids are `tk_` + a hash of the scope, never raw ids. A workspace erase destroys the workspace key, a user-only erase the user's key; a workspace+user erase deletes rows only (the key is shared, reported as `kept`).
  - **KMS** (`rag/kms/index.js` interface: getOrCreateKey, wrap/unwrapDataKey, rotateKey, destroyKey, describeKey, listKeys, status): only `TENANT_KMS_PROVIDER=local` exists (`rag/kms/local-provider.js`, demo-grade): `TENANT_KEYSTORE_DIR` (default `<dirname of RAG_DATA_DIRECTORY>/tenant-keystore`, 0700, `<keyId>.json` per key, 0600) wrapped by `TENANT_KMS_MASTER_KEY_FILE` (default `<same root>/tenant-kms-master.key`, created 0600 on first use, refused when group/other can read it). First use creates with an exclusive `link()`, changes take an O_EXCL lock file and `rename()`, and a change that finds no key file creates it with the same exclusive link and reruns on EEXIST (a rename there could replace a first version a lock-free first use had just sealed with); a key another process changed is seen at the next operation (stat signature). Destroy zeroes the file and leaves a tombstone of version numbers (a later read fails as destroyed; new writes continue at the next version); on copy-on-write filesystems that is best effort. Outside PostgreSQL on purpose, so database backups hold no usable key. A cloud KMS/HSM is the documented production replacement, not implemented. Every process that reads or writes tenant files (api, ingest workers, agent tier, CLIs) must see the same keystore and master key.
  - **Encrypted**: `rag_documents.file_bytes` (and the file-backed registry's files), `rag_ingest_jobs.file_bytes` and every `rag_ingest_jobs_outputs.payload` (the PDF keeps the job's context when parse moves it in SQL), upload session chunk files. Working files of one request or attempt (multer's upload via `routes/working-file-storage.js`, the merged chunked upload, `ingest-tmp/`, arXiv downloads, reindex and index-build temp copies) use `rag/working-files.js`: a key held only in this process's memory, no KMS, unreadable once the process ends. Readers: `getFile`/download, the ingest stages (`copyJobFile`/`copyOutputFile` stream-decrypt; `readOutputs`), `vector:reindex --from documents`, index-version builds, the tenant export (plaintext), and `readWorkingFile` in pdf-loader, docling, `rag/index.js`, `resolveFileBuffer`, uploads, the format check (`readDocumentBytesForDetection` in `rag/document-formats.js`) and `hasPdfMagicBytes`. `content_sha256` and `file_size` stay the plaintext's; ingest pending byte caps count stored bytes. Reserved prefixes: plaintext that begins with either magic (`startsWithReservedFileMagic` in `rag/working-files.js`) would be taken for a damaged envelope by every later reader, so the upload's byte check (`detectDocumentFormatAtPath`; `hasPdfMagicBytes` before Office formats) refuses it like any file of no supported format (400 `DOCUMENT_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`, and under `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS=pdf` the old 400 "not a valid PDF" with no code: direct and chunked uploads, PUT replace, the async enqueue behind them, `archive-ingest`), a working file this process cannot decrypt is no supported document there either (it used to be a 500 with encryption off), and a trash restore of such stored bytes is 415 `DOCUMENT_RESTORE_RESERVED_PREFIX`; arXiv downloads must already begin with `%PDF-`. The tenant export exports bytes that do not decrypt as null with the code in the record's `unreadableBytes` (`decryptTenantRecordBytesForExport`), counts them in the summary's `unreadableRecords` and ends `partial`, instead of stopping at the first such document. The PostgreSQL ingest store reads a job's owner under its fence only for an envelope or an encrypted write, so the off path sends no extra statement. Not encrypted: chunk text, embeddings, tsvectors, run snapshots, feedback, memory, the in-memory ingest store; erase deletes them, but older database backups keep them, so backup retention must stay bounded.
  - **Erase** (`rag/tenant-lifecycle.js`): after every store went without a failure, an applied erase destroys the selector's key; result and tombstone (`details.tenantKeys`, whitelisted in `normalizeAdminAuditDetails`) carry key ids and versions only; a dry run lists what would go; a failed erase leaves the key for the rerun. With encryption off and no key on file there is no `tenantKeys` field at all.
  - **Ops**: `npm run tenant:keys -- status|rotate|rewrap|encrypt-existing|destroy` (`server/tenant-keys.mjs`, `rag/tenant-encryption-maintenance.js`; dry run unless `--apply`, destroy refuses without it, encrypt-existing `--apply` needs the setting on; a row per short `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` transaction on the system path, running ingest jobs skipped). A value counts as an envelope only when its framing and header parse (`isParseableTenantEnvelopeHead` over a head of 12 + header bytes read in SQL); one that only begins with the magic is `malformed` in status and in encrypt-existing's report and is never changed (encrypting it would hand the damaged bytes out as plaintext). `checks.tenantEncryption` (keystore and master key readability; error for keys without a usable master key, a damaged key file, an exposed master key) appears only with the setting on, last, on tiers hosting api or agent. Tests: `tenant-envelope`, `tenant-kms-local` (child processes race), `tenant-encryption-stores`, `working-file-upload-route`, `tenant-encryption-postgres.integration` (needs `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL`).
- Hardened tenant-isolation demo (`docs/demo-tenant-isolation.md`): `deploy/demo/hardened.env` turns every switch above on at once (static per-user tokens, `API_AUTH_REQUIRE_WORKSPACE`, RBAC and membership enforce, strict context, the role split, data events, redaction, the document trash (72 h), every per-tenant limit and quota, retention, `TENANT_ENCRYPTION=on` with the local KMS, `RAG_EMBEDDING_CACHE_SCOPE=tenant`, `TENANT_REGISTRY_CACHE_SCOPE=tenant`, `LONG_MEMORY_SCOPE=workspace`, `API_AUTH_OIDC_ALLOW_PLATFORM_ROLES=false`). It leaves `TENANT_KEYSTORE_DIR` / `TENANT_KMS_MASTER_KEY_FILE` to their defaults on purpose (git-ignored `server/data/` locally, resolved from the source directory); `compose.demo.yml` sets them to the app volume (`/data`). Its tokens and database passwords are fixed public demo values, as the file says; never reuse them. `compose.demo.yml` (an override of `docker-compose.yml`, with `--profile app`) loads it alone (`env_file: !override`, so `server/.env` never reaches the demo), runs as its own compose project (`name: luc1ferxx-archive-rag-demo`) with its own container names and volumes (`pgdata-demo`, `appdata-demo` at the app's `/data`, where the keystore and master key live), so `... --profile app down -v` removes only the demo's data; it creates the split logins with `create-split-roles.sql` on first init of `pgdata-demo`, connects the app as the runtime login and adds a `demo-model` service (the fake model, compose network only, `init: true`, and the command stops on SIGTERM/SIGINT); `test/deployment-contract.test.mjs` pins it, and it was built and started once (2026-10-18: the image built and all three containers were healthy; `docs/tenant-isolation.md`). `npm run demo:seed|demo:walkthrough|demo:fake-model` (`server/demo-tenants.mjs`, logic in `server/demo/tenant-demo.js` and `server/demo/fake-model.js`) work over HTTP against a running server and never touch the database; the walkthrough prints a PASS/FAIL table, exits 1 on a FAIL, leaves the server as the seed made it, and its `audit_chain` check is always SKIP (`npm run audit:verify` is CLI-only). `test/demo-tenants.test.mjs` requires every profile variable to be read by the server and documented, the server's parsers to read each switch as on, and the profile's tokens to equal `DEMO_PERSONAS`; `test/tenant-isolation-hardened.e2e.test.mjs` (needs `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL`, skipped without) runs `node server.js` on the profile against a throwaway database with the split logins, plus a dev OIDC IdP, and asserts seed, two walkthroughs, stored `file_bytes` are envelopes (one key per workspace, bound to its row, `tenant:keys status`), the concurrency cap, health (`tenantEncryption` too), the trash purge and its deferred redaction, retention deletes, `audit-verify.mjs`, the operator CLIs and a real erase (the workspace key destroyed: a kept copy of alpha's ciphertext fails `TENANT_KEY_DESTROYED`, beta still decrypts and downloads; a re-registered alpha writes under a newer key version). The walkthrough's trash, workspace-memory, refusal-accounting and switcher (`/auth/me` without a header) checks restore what they change, except gamma's quota probes, which stay in the trash and keep counting. Change a check, a persona or the profile together with those suites and the doc's tables. A real model key never goes into that tracked profile: it comes from the shell (compose also keeps it in the container's configuration, which `docker inspect` prints; the local method listens on every interface with public demo tokens), with the compose overrides in the untracked `compose.demo.local.yml` ("换成真实模型" in that doc).
- More RLS coverage: migration 033 recreates every chunk table's `_sparse_rank` through the owner-only `<base>_install_sparse_rank(table)` so that, under the tenant role, it first narrows the doc ids to the documents the documents policy shows (a direct call with another tenant's ids ranks nothing; owner calls are unchanged); `renderIndexVersionChunkTableDdl` calls the installer for new versions. Keep the caller's narrowing too (a lagging replica may still hold 014's body). Migration 034 moves answer feedback into PostgreSQL (`rag_feedback`, `FEEDBACK_POSTGRES_TABLE`, `rag/postgres-feedback-store.js`; SELECT/INSERT-only tenant grant, exact-pair policy, `json` columns so records read back byte-exact); the JSONL file store serves only the no-database profile, the record identity comes from the access scope only, and `npm run feedback:import -- [--input <file>] [--apply]` copies a pre-034 file (dry run by default, idempotent). `resolveFeedbackReadScope` reads a scope naming neither user nor workspace as every record, like the registry (requireTenantScope keeps such scopes off the route except the single shared token). `feedback:corpus` reads every tenant through the store on the owner path. With API auth on, `POSTGRES_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY=off` makes `checks.rowLevelSecurity` an error unless `POSTGRES_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY_OFF_CONFIRMED=true` (then `disabled`, `acknowledged: true`).
- Session memory is tenant-scoped (the client-chosen `sessionId` alone is no tenant boundary).
  - **Key:** with API auth on (or an `authenticated` scope), `resolveSessionMemoryKey` in `rag/memory.js` stores a session under `tenant:` + sha256(userId \0 workspaceId \0 sessionId), in every session store (PostgreSQL and the standalone in-memory store, which the evaluation stores reuse). Read (`resolveQueryWithSessionMemory`), write (`recordSessionTurn`, also through `recordConversationTurn`) and clear (`clearSessionMemory`, `DELETE /sessions/:sessionId` passes `getRequestAccessScope(req)`) all derive it from the caller's `accessScope`, else from the active database tenant, so tasks and the agent tier reach the same session as the chat that created it. Keep a new session caller on that one derivation.
  - **Auth off:** the raw id stays the key (a client may name a user on a chat and not on the session DELETE; the web UI sends no userId at all). A tenant-less caller's id that starts with the reserved `tenant:` prefix names no session, because its statements run on the owner path past the policy.
  - **Database:** migration 031 adds `owner_user_id` / `workspace_id` (NOT NULL, default ''), the tenant GRANT and a `tenant_isolation` policy on the exact (user, workspace) pair; the PostgreSQL `append` fills the owner columns from the active tenant and keeps an existing row's owner. A store has `append`, not `upsert`: `recordSessionTurn` sends only the new turn, and the store appends it and keeps the last six messages in one step (PostgreSQL: one `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` with `jsonb_path_query_array(messages || new, '$[last - 5 to last]')`, so the row lock orders writers; in memory: no await between read and write), so turns of one session recorded at the same time all stay. `checks.rowLevelSecurity` covers the table. A tenant append that meets a row the policy hides (42501) skips the turn with a warning instead of failing the answer.
  - **Legacy rows:** rows written before 031 have empty owners, so no tenant sees them; tenants lose pre-upgrade session context. They stay readable on the owner path only, and the 6-hour TTL purge runs under `runAsDatabaseSystem` so it removes them.
  - **Split deployments:** every tier needs the same `API_AUTH_ENABLED`; a task's stored scope carries no `authenticated` flag.
  - **Tests:** `test/session-memory-tenant-isolation.test.mjs` (in-process: two tenants with one id, two workspaces, auth off, the DELETE route, the reserved prefix), `test/standalone-profile.test.mjs` (concurrent turns all kept, the last three turns kept) and the session cases in `test/postgres-row-level-security.integration.test.mjs` (direct SQL under the tenant role, the pinned policy text, health coverage with the policy dropped and restored; three concurrent appends under the tenant role, the trim, the owner columns).
  - **One derivation:** the tenant rule lives in `rag/conversation-memory-tenant.js` (`resolveConversationMemoryTenant`); long-term memory under `LONG_MEMORY_SCOPE=workspace` uses the same function.
- Long-term memory per workspace (`LONG_MEMORY_SCOPE=user|workspace`, default `user`, unknown value refuses start; `rag/long-memory.js`, migration 045). Under `user` nothing changed: store calls, SQL and rows are those of before 045, and a user's notes and agent experience follow the user into every workspace.
  - **Workspace scope:** notes, agent experience and every free-text row are written with the active workspace and read only in it; a read in W sees the user's rows of W plus the user-level rows (`workspace_id ''` and not `personal`). Only the fixed-enum preferences (`reply_language` zh|en, `answer_style` concise|detailed, `isUserLevelPreference`) are stored user-level, and with their fixed text (`buildPreferenceMemoryText`), source `user_explicit` and confidence 1, never the caller's text, source or confidence (POST /memory accepts any). delete/clear act on what W sees, user-level rows included.
  - **Personal scope** (migration 046): a user-only tenant (no active workspace) writes its free text with `workspace_id ''` and `personal` TRUE (the store's `remember({ workspaceId: '', personal: true })`), which no workspace reads, deletes or clears; a read there sees the rows of no workspace (user-level and personal). With auth off there is no tenant and rows stay user-level, not personal. Before 046 such notes had the user-level marker and were recalled in every workspace; rows written that way cannot be told apart and stay visible until deleted, cleared or erased. No workspace id can serve as the marker (any string a token or header names may be one), hence the column.
  - **Derivation:** `resolveLongMemoryWorkspace(accessScope)` = the conversation-memory tenant's workspace (caller's scope, else the active database tenant; '' with auth off or a user-only tenant); null under `user`, in which case the store gets no `workspaceId` argument at all. Callers pass `accessScope`: `chat()` (`getLongMemoryContext`, `recordLongMemoryFromUserMessage`), `routes/memory.js`, and the long-memory-backed experience store (`toExperienceAccessScope` from the user and workspace the experience module resolved; prune passes its workspace to `delete`). MCP tools never touch long memory.
  - **Database:** migration 045 adds `workspace_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` and a partial index; migration 046 adds `personal BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE` with a check that a personal row has `workspace_id ''`, and replaces the `tenant_isolation` policy: same user AND (`workspace_id = archive_rag.workspace_id` OR (`workspace_id = ''` AND NOT `personal`)), USING and WITH CHECK alike, whatever the setting (all-'', non-personal rows under `user` make it equal to 013's). A tenant in W can neither read nor write a personal row, nor turn a user-level row personal. No new grant: 013/035 already cover the table.
  - **Legacy rows:** rows from before the scope have '' and stay visible in every workspace of the user until deleted, cleared from any workspace (`DELETE /memory`), or erased with a user-only tenant selector. Switching back to `user` leaves workspace rows hidden from other workspaces under RLS.
  - **Tests:** `test/long-memory-workspace-scope.test.mjs` (two workspaces, one user, the default's exact store calls, routes, experience), the long-memory case of `test/postgres-row-level-security.integration.test.mjs` (policy text, direct SQL under the tenant role including personal rows, the real store, erase/export selectors in both scopes), and the workspace case in `test/tenant-lifecycle.test.mjs`.
- Neural reranking: `RAG_RERANK_ENABLED=true RAG_RERANK_PROVIDER=cross-encoder RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_ENDPOINT=http://127.0.0.1:8081/rerank` takes `topK * RAG_RERANK_CANDIDATE_MULTIPLIER` fused candidates per retrieval query; a retrieval step merges its queries' candidates and a cross-encoder service speaking `{query, texts}` -> `{scores}` (the Hugging Face TEI `/rerank` shape) scores the merged pool once against the primary query (once per document on the per-document route; `rerankRetrievalStep` in `rag/document-rag-execution.js`) and keeps `topK` times the query count, blending with `RAG_RERANK_WEIGHT`. The agent's follow-up step is the exception (`isFollowUpRetrievalQuery`, `rag/agent-retrieval-queries.js`): its queries look for evidence for unsupported claims, so each reranks its own candidates against its own text (merging them failed n1 in docs/evaluation.md RR2). Measured as RR3 on 2026-11-21 (docs/evaluation.md "重排降本第二版", all seven pre-declared rules passed as measured): rerank requests per question 3.57 -> 2.10 (2.72 HTTP requests since they are capped at 32 texts, over c1's 2.5), scored passages at most 107 -> about 65, answerable answered unchanged at 84/176, answerable F1 -0.0085 [-0.0203, +0.0019] (no significant drop detected, which does not show there is none), multilingual 22/25 (ML-RR 18/25; its corpus is 11 chunks, all of them candidates every time, so it cannot show lost recall). One rerank is sent at once (not in turn: overlapping reranks would requeue and time out together) as requests of at most 32 texts (TEI's default `--max-client-batch-size`; a multiple of the endpoint's batch size 8, so its scores are unchanged) that share one `RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_TIMEOUT_MS`; any failed request fails the whole attempt and closes its other requests (a gateway with several rerank upstreams resends all of it to the next one after a refused connection) (`requestCrossEncoderScores`, `test/reranker-request-batches.test.mjs`). The process caches scores by tenant, transport, model, score scale, query and passage text (at most 20,000, each for ten minutes, `rag/reranker.js`), so the follow-up's question query sends only passages the first step did not score; the gap plan's one-query searches still rerank on their own. Tests: `test/rerank-once-per-step.test.mjs`, `test/reranker-score-cache.test.mjs`. `npm run rerank:cross-encoder:setup` / `rerank:cross-encoder` run `evaluation/neural-cross-encoder-endpoint.py` (set `RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_MODEL=BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3`, the measured model); it serializes inference with a lock because concurrent sub-query requests crashed MPS. Every rerank goes through `rerankResultsOrKeepOrder`, so an unreachable or failing reranker degrades to the fused order (each query's own fused top-K) with a warning (and an error rerank metric) instead of failing; `rerankResultsWithProvider` itself still throws, which the timeout tests pin. It is not on by default because it needs that service. Reranked results keep the service's raw `crossEncoderScore` next to the `rerankScore` min-maxed within one rerank call, and when every QA candidate carries one, `assessQaConfidence` decides whether to answer from the reranker's relevance probability (`RAG_QA_MIN_RERANK_PROBABILITY`, default 0.02, `off` for the lexical gate; `RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_SCORES` says whether the service returns logits or probabilities) instead of query-term coverage, still requiring anchors; the confidence trace records `gate: rerank|lexical`. Without scores (reranker off or failed) the lexical gate decides as before. `eval:abstention-gate` with the reranker on reports the reranker AUC, a probability grid with the cost rule, and the pick.
  - **Re-measured at top-K 10 (2026-11-12, rules pre-declared in ee640eb6, docs/evaluation.md "重排在 top-K 10 下重测"; weight 0.6 and pool x3 not re-tuned):** the cost rule, with the lexical gate on the same reranked candidates competing, still picks 0.02 on train, so the default stays. Rerank probability AUC 0.648 against coverage 0.596 on train, 0.724 against 0.658 on dev.
    - **Retrieval on dev:** the reranker adds evidence among the 10 candidates (+0.067 [+0.045, +0.089]) and in the top 3 (+0.050 [+0.018, +0.083]); rank 1 shows no detected difference.
    - **Gate on dev:** with the 0.02 gate, 0.893 of answerable questions pass, against 0.600 without the reranker, and 0.337 of unanswerable ones are caught, against 0.589.
    - **Answers** (the last 116 clean dev questions, qwen2.5:7b at temperature 0 with the 1024 cap): official F1 +0.039 [−0.018, +0.095]; answerable questions answered 61 -> 93; unanswerable answered 8 -> 9 of 15; answer-level cost 0.535 -> 0.277.
    - **Guardrail:** `verify:quality` 18/18 with the reranker. A rerun sent the reranker 8 requests, counted from its log while nothing else called it.
    - **Recorded only, not rule-picked:** at 0.2 the reranker stack's unanswerable catch on dev shows no detected difference from the default lexical stack: 0.589 for both as point estimates, a paired difference of 0 [−0.105, +0.105], and different questions caught. It passes +0.140 [+0.108, +0.172] more answerable questions. 0.2 was never answer-checked.
    - **Report fields:** the rule's costs from whole counts are recorded as `rerankGate.candidates[].exactCost`. The grid's own `cost` column comes from rounded rates, and its "(earlier rule's pick)" mark is `rerankPick`. On dev, 0.05 was inside the 0.01 tolerance by only 0.00008.
- One-click reranker: `docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f compose.rerank.yml --profile app --profile rerank up -d --build` adds the `reranker` service (profile `rerank`, build context `server/evaluation`, `cross-encoder-service/Dockerfile`, CPU torch). It runs the measured `neural-cross-encoder-endpoint.py` with the pinned `neural-reranker-requirements.txt`, `BAAI/bge-reranker-v2-m3`, and the endpoint's own defaults (max length 384, batch 8). It returns logits and mounts `server/evaluation/generated/huggingface` as `HF_HOME`. `compose.rerank.yml` points the app at `http://reranker:8081/rerank` with `RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_SCORES=logits` and waits for the reranker's health. Keep rerank variables out of the base `app` service's `environment`, which overrides `server/.env`. `test/deployment-contract.test.mjs` pins all of this, including that the image's ENV equals the endpoint's defaults. The image was built once on linux/arm64 (2026-11-21: about 258 MB downloaded, 46 s, 1.58 GB); x86_64 has not been built. Measured in that machine's Docker Desktop (docs/evaluation.md "重排容器的 CPU 实测"): about 12 s of rerank per question on 15 cores and 25 s on 4 cores; on 4 cores a 90-passage rerank (three requests under one timeout) takes about 34 s, over the 30 s RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_TIMEOUT_MS default, so raise it (90000) on small machines, and with a separate retrieval tier raise RETRIEVAL_SERVICE_TIMEOUT_MS above it (120000): that budget (default 60000) includes rerank, and when it ends first the query fails with 504 instead of keeping the fused order; a timed-out step also falls back to the lexical QA gate.
- Uploads use multer 2 (`routes/uploads.js`). multer 2 rejects a file only when it exceeds `limits.fileSize` (1.x did so on reaching it), so the limits are the public maxima themselves (`MAX_DIRECT_UPLOAD_SIZE`, `MAX_CHUNK_UPLOAD_SIZE` in `upload-policy.js`); one byte over is `LIMIT_FILE_SIZE` -> 413. `test/upload-multipart-hardening.test.mjs` pins the 413 and that an oversized or cut-off request leaves no file in the uploads directory. The image still installs with `--legacy-peer-deps`; the upgrade was not tried in a docker build.
- Chunking (`server/rag/chunker.js`): `RAG_CHUNK_STRATEGY=structured` (default), `simple`, or `sentence`. `sentence` (`chunkPageWithSentences`, splitter in `server/rag/sentence-splitter.js`) keeps the structured chunker's heading rules (same `isLikelyHeading` lines, kept heading-like lines, no page crossing) and packs whole sentences: a chunk's whole `pageContent`, heading included, never exceeds `RAG_CHUNK_SIZE`; only a sentence longer than the room is cut (clause, then word, then hard boundary); overlap is the trailing whole sentences that fit `RAG_CHUNK_OVERLAP`, never a cut piece; list items and table rows are their own blocks. Keep `findUnindexedPageText` empty for it (`test/chunker-sentence.test.mjs` also checks reading-order coverage on generated pages). It is opt-in until a retrieval comparison shows it pays; switching re-chunks only new ingests.
- Every ingest and re-parse path (uploads, PUT replace, the staged parse stage and the async worker's one-step ingest, `archive-ingest`, the arXiv importer, `vector:reindex --from documents`, index-version builds, the trash restore) reads a file through `loadDocumentPages` in `server/rag/document-loader.js`. PDF text extraction still goes through `loadPdfPages` in `server/rag/pdf-loader.js`, which that loader calls: a read that accepts PDF alone hands the file straight to it with no format detection, as every path did before Office formats, and Word, PowerPoint and Excel files go to their own loaders ("Office documents" below). `PDF_PARSER` is `pdfjs`, `docling` or `auto`; an unknown value fails at ingest. `pdfjs` (default) parses with pdfjs-dist 6 (pinned exactly; the 5.x line has an arbitrary-JavaScript-on-open advisory, and the 5.6.205 -> 6.4.299 upgrade extracted byte-identical text from 55 local PDFs: 30 QASPER arXiv papers and 25 synthetic evaluation documents), in the calling thread unless `PDF_PARSE_WORKERS` > 0 (next bullet); `docling` posts the file to docling-serve (`server/rag/docling-parser.js`) and flattens the DoclingDocument into the same `[{ pageNumber, text }]` pages: body items in reading order, page headers/footers dropped, a table as its caption plus one line per row with every cell labelled by its (stacked) column header, figures as captions only; `auto` keeps pdf.js text for most pages and sends only table, scanned and garbled pages to docling-serve (next bullet). On failure `docling` falls back to pdf.js for the whole document with a warning unless `DOCLING_FALLBACK=none`. `checks.pdfParser` in the health report probes docling-serve under `docling`, and under `auto` while a route is enabled.
  - `PDF_PARAGRAPH_DETECTION` (default on; `false` restores the legacy one-line-per-visual-line text; pdf.js path only, never Docling or `rag/chunker.js`) rebuilds paragraphs from pdf.js line geometry in `server/rag/pdf-paragraphs.js`: a vertical gap above 1.3x the page's median line spacing, a font size or uniform font change, a first-line indent, a list marker, or a short line ending a sentence starts a paragraph (blank line between). Inside a paragraph a break becomes a space only under the shared soft-wrap rule (`server/rag/wrapped-lines.js`, also used by `joinWrappedLines`), and a line-end hyphen is dropped only when the word before it is a plain lowercase-ending word and the document never spells the hyphenated form without the joined one (`state-of-the-`/`art`, `GPT-`/`based`, `follow-`/`up` keep it). A line with a blank stretch of at least 1.5 font sizes between ink runs (not after `.,;:!?`) counts as a table row and is never joined to its neighbours, even when a row starts in lowercase. Page statistics (median line spacing and width) are computed once per font size; do not move them back into the per-line break check, which made a page quadratic in its lines. Off is byte-identical to the legacy text (checked on the 55 local PDFs); on changes only whitespace and removed line-end hyphens (`test/pdf-paragraphs.test.mjs`). Never joined: a heading, a list item to the line before it (a lowercase hanging-indent continuation joins its own item), a line after `:` or `;`, a table row, a line ending in `.!?` (or the CJK forms), and a next line that does not start with a lowercase ASCII letter (so uppercase, digits and scripts without case, such as Chinese, stay apart). It became the default because the 30-paper QASPER `pdfjs,pdfjs-paragraphs` run met the pre-declared non-inferiority rule (docs/evaluation.md "pdf.js 段落识别"): all 149 questions, answer F1 -0.0001 [-0.0185, +0.0204], gold span in retrieved chunks 0 [-0.0465, +0.0465] (n = 86, passed only through the CI-contains-0, non-negative-mean branch); no CI excludes 0, so do not describe it as better. Side effect: whole paragraphs are now chunker units, so the structured chunker's paragraph overlap can carry a whole paragraph and a chunk can exceed `RAG_CHUNK_SIZE` (55 local PDFs: 183 of 2,338 chunks above 900, longest 1,910, 21% more chunk text; measured with the structured chunker before 1dcc8467, which since then never exceeds `RAG_CHUNK_SIZE`, see "Chunk profile" below); the synthetic corpora parse identically either way, so deterministic evals and the pinned baseline are unchanged. Switching the flag changes the text and chunks of every re-parsed PDF, and existing archives keep their old chunks until re-chunked: `npm run vector:reindex -- --from documents --apply` (or a `PUT /documents/:docId` replace per document).
- Per-page PDF routing and parallel pdf.js (both opt-in: `PDF_PARSER=auto`, `PDF_PARSE_WORKERS` > 0; with `PDF_PARSER=pdfjs` and `PDF_PARSE_WORKERS=0` the pdf.js calls, their order and every byte of output are what they were before; measurements in docs/evaluation.md "按页分流和并行解析").
  - **Signals** (`extractPdfPageRange({ collectSignals })` in `rag/pdf-page-extraction.js`, which also holds the page-text code `pdf-loader.js` used to): per page `charCount` (non-whitespace), `itemCount`, `lineCount`, `tabularLineCount`, `tableCaption` (a line starting "Table N" or "Tab. N", arabic or roman, or "表 N"), `imageCount` and `imageCoverage` from `getOperatorList` with annotations disabled (every image a paint, repeat or group operator draws counts once; footprints clipped to the page box and summed, not unioned, capped at 1, so coverage can only err high), and `garbledCharRatio` (U+FFFD, private-use and C0/C1 code points over non-whitespace characters). A tabular line is `pdf-paragraphs.js`'s (ink runs at least 1.5 font sizes apart, not after `.,;:!?`); a displayed equation is not one: exactly two runs whose second is a bare number (`(3)`, `(4a)`, `(2.1)`, `(3-2)`, `(A1)`, `(A.1)`; lines now carry `cellCount` and `lastCellText`), while a row ending in `212 (48)` still counts. The operator list decodes every image, which makes a signal pass about 2.8x the text-only one, so nothing collects signals unless asked.
  - **Router** (`classifyPdfPage` in `rag/pdf-page-router.js`, a pure function of the signals and `resolvePdfAutoRoutingConfig()`), first match wins: `ocr` for garbled text (at least `GARBLED_MIN_CHARS` = 20 characters and ratio >= `PDF_AUTO_GARBLED_RATIO`, 0.3), `ocr` for an image without text (fewer than `PDF_AUTO_MIN_TEXT_CHARS`, 200, and coverage >= `PDF_AUTO_IMAGE_COVERAGE`, 0.3), `layout` for `PDF_AUTO_TABLE_MIN_ROWS` (4) tabular lines or a table caption with 2, else `text`. Reasons are `PDF_PAGE_ROUTE_REASONS` codes. `PDF_AUTO_ROUTES` (`layout,ocr`, either one, or `none`) disables routes: such a page falls through to the next rule and its reasons add `ocr_route_disabled` / `layout_route_disabled`. An empty `PDF_AUTO_*` value is the default; an out-of-range or non-numeric one throws before any parsing (the upload fails, `checks.pdfParser` is an error). The defaults were chosen on the same 30 QASPER papers the routing counts come from (equation numbers were the main false tabular lines, hence the exemption and the minimum of 4), so those counts are not an independent test of the router.
  - **Requests** (`planDoclingBatches` in `rag/pdf-page-router.js`, called by `rag/pdf-auto-parser.js`): pages on one Docling route form one run while at most `DOCLING_MERGE_GAP_PAGES` (default 0 = contiguous only; 2 cut 30 papers' requests from 55 to 34 and was 4.5% faster at `DOCLING_MAX_CONCURRENCY=1` but about 6% slower at the default 2, measured ABBA) text pages separate them (a page on the other Docling route always ends a run); each run is one request, split at `DOCLING_PAGE_BATCH_SIZE` (0 = unsplit) so every request starts and ends on a routed page, all issued at once. Gap pages are converted but keep their pdf.js text (a failed request marks only its routed pages `docling_failed`); merging is safe only because cross-page paragraphs are split by page (below), else a gap page would duplicate or lose text. `layout` sends `do_ocr` = `DOCLING_OCR`, `ocr` sends `do_ocr` and `force_ocr`. `page_range` goes as the form field twice (docling-serve's FastAPI tuple), and every request uploads the whole file. `mapRangePages` (`docling-parser.js`) keeps pages numbered as in the original file (dropping any outside the range), shifts a result numbered exactly 1..N into the range, and refuses one with no page inside it (`DOCLING_PAGE_RANGE_MISMATCH`); docling-serve 1.37 numbers a ranged conversion's pages as in the original file (checked on 3 papers). `doclingDocumentToPages` splits a text item Docling merged over a page break back by page (`splitDoclingTextByPage`: each provenance's `charspan`, moved to the nearest whitespace within 40 characters so no word is cut; spans that are missing or go back keep the item on its first page, as before), in every mode: before, a paragraph's continuation was attributed to its first page, so `PDF_PARSER=docling` page numbers of cross-page paragraphs change and a ranged conversion now reads like the same page of a whole one (30 papers: 250/266 pages identical between 4-page ranges and whole documents, min token Dice 0.998, was 230/266 and 0.881). `PDF_PARSER=docling` with `DOCLING_PAGE_BATCH_SIZE` > 0 splits a longer document the same way (`convertPdfPageRangesWithDocling`, route `document`; a PDF whose pages cannot be counted goes whole).
  - **Limiter** (`docling-parser.js`): every docling-serve request of the process (`docling`, its ranges and `auto`) waits FIFO for one of `DOCLING_MAX_CONCURRENCY` slots (default 2, re-read at every grant, matching docling-serve's default 2 local workers, `DOCLING_SERVE_ENG_LOC_NUM_WORKERS`; per process, so instances sharing one docling-serve add up). This cap is new for `PDF_PARSER=docling` too. `DOCLING_TIMEOUT_MS` now bounds one request and starts once it has a slot; queue time is not bounded (pinned by a test). The form is built only after the slot, and a document's requests share one Blob (`createDoclingPdfBlob`), so queued requests hold no copy of the PDF; keep both. `onFailure` runs before a failed request frees its slot, so a cancellation lands before a queued sibling can be sent. Failures are `DoclingParseError` (502) with a `DOCLING_ERROR_CODES` code (`DOCLING_TIMEOUT`, `DOCLING_UNREACHABLE`, `DOCLING_CONVERSION_FAILED` with `httpStatus`, `DOCLING_REQUEST_ABORTED`, `DOCLING_PAGE_RANGE_MISMATCH`).
  - **Merge and fallback:** Docling's page text replaces pdf.js's, except a page Docling returns empty while pdf.js had text, which keeps pdf.js text (`docling_empty`). Under `DOCLING_FALLBACK=pdfjs` a failed request's pages keep pdf.js text (`docling_failed`, one warning per document with page ranges and codes), and a timeout or unreachable server also drops the document's requests still waiting for a slot (outcome `aborted`; requests already sent finish), so a stalled server costs a document about one timeout; under `none` the first failure cancels the document's other requests, queued or in flight, and the upload fails with that error.
  - **Routing report** (`loadPdfPagesWithRouting`; `loadPdfPages` keeps only the pages and nothing persists the report): `{ parser: "auto", pageCount, routes: [{ pageNumber, route, reasons, parser, fallback? }], batches: [{ route, firstPage, lastPage, outcome, durationMs }] }` (`durationMs` includes the wait for a slot). Codes and numbers only: never page text, file names or URLs; keep it that way.
  - **Metrics** (fixed-enum labels): `rag/metrics-pdf.js` counts `archive_rag_pdf_pages_total{route,parser}` (`route` is `unrouted` under pdfjs and docling), `archive_rag_pdf_docling_requests_total{route,outcome}` (`document`/`layout`/`ocr`; `ok`/`error`/`timeout`/`aborted`; a request cancelled before its slot is counted, not timed), `archive_rag_pdf_docling_request_duration_seconds{route}` (from the slot) and `archive_rag_pdf_parse_fallbacks_total{reason}` (`docling_failed`, `docling_empty`, `docling_document` for a whole-document docling fallback); `rag/metrics-pdf-pool.js` reads the pool snapshot on scrape (`archive_rag_pdf_parse_pool_size`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_workers{state}`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_queued_tasks`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_tasks_total{outcome}`, `archive_rag_pdf_parse_worker_restarts_total`) and never starts the pool. Both are imported by `metrics-server.js`; docs/operations.md has the queries.
  - **Health** (`checkPdfParserHealth` in `health.js`, api and agent tiers): `pdfjs` is unchanged (ok, no probe); `docling` and `auto` add `doclingMaxConcurrency` and `doclingPageBatchSize`; `auto` validates `PDF_AUTO_*` first (error on an invalid value), adds `routes`, and answers ok without a probe under `PDF_AUTO_ROUTES=none`; otherwise docling-serve unreachable is an error whose message says whether routed pages fall back or fail.
  - **Worker pool** (`PDF_PARSE_WORKERS`, default 0 = in the calling thread, capped at `MAX_PDF_PARSE_WORKERS` = 64; `rag/pdf-parse-pool.js`, `rag/pdf-parse-worker.js`, and `parsePdfBytesWithPdfjs` in `rag/pdfjs-document.js`, which `loadPdfDocument` uses, so pdfjs, the docling fallback and auto all go through it): the first task parses pages 1..`PDF_PARSE_PAGES_PER_TASK` (32) and learns the page count, then `workers` lanes take the remaining ranges in page order, one range per lane at a time. A document therefore never holds more than `workers` tasks in the pool's single FIFO queue, and a later document waits behind at most one range per worker of it; do not go back to queueing every range at once. Ranges come back unrendered (lines or legacy text, plus signals) and are rendered over the whole document (the hyphenation vocabulary spans every page), so text and signals are byte-identical to in-process: `deepStrictEqual` on 38 local PDFs, paragraph detection on and off, workers 1/2/4 with ranges of 32/3/2 and 4 with 32. Each task posts its own transferred copy of the bytes when it starts; the caller's buffer is never touched.
  - **Pool errors:** pdf.js exceptions come back with name, message, code and status and are rethrown as their class (`restorePdfjsError`), built-ins by name. After a failed range no further range is queued, waiting ones are dropped (`PDF_PARSE_TASK_CANCELLED`, 499, internal), running ones finish, and the first failed range in page order is thrown; an incomplete page set is `PDF_PARSE_WORKER_FAILED`. A worker that exits, crashes or runs past `PDF_PARSE_WORKER_MAX_OLD_GENERATION_MB` (`resourceLimits.maxOldGenerationSizeMb`, 0 = Node's default; a process-wide `--max-old-space-size`, on the command line or in `NODE_OPTIONS`, replaces it in every worker, and the pool warns once, `findProcessHeapLimitFlag` in `rag/worker-task-pool.js`) fails its task with `PDF_PARSE_WORKER_FAILED` (500) and is replaced; `PDF_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS` (0 = none; per running task, queue excluded) terminates the worker and fails the task with `PDF_PARSE_TIMEOUT` (504). The async ingest queue retries both as 5xx.
  - **Pool lifecycle:** the scheduling lives in `rag/worker-task-pool.js` since 2026-11-13, shared with the Office parse pool; `rag/pdf-parse-pool.js` keeps its exports, errors and messages. Workers spawn on demand up to the size, are `ref()`'d only while running a task (an idle pool never keeps a process alive) and start with `execArgv: []` (no inherited `--import` preloads or `--input-type`). A changed size or memory limit starts a new pool; the old one finishes its tasks, then terminates. `stopPdfParsePool()` runs in `createApp`'s `app.locals.stop` (after requests and the ingest worker have stopped) and at the end of `pdf:route`; tasks still queued or running fail with `PDF_PARSE_POOL_STOPPED` (503), which the ingest queue retries. Other entry points rely on idle workers being unref'd. A single-file executable bundle has no worker file: keep `PDF_PARSE_WORKERS=0` there (the Docker image is a plain directory and can use it). Gains come only from parsing several PDFs at once (38 at once: 0.80-0.96 s in process, 0.30-0.48 s on 4 workers, longest main-thread event-loop stall 21-33 ms vs 7-9 ms); one at a time is no faster and the first pass pays about 250 ms of worker start-up.
  - **CLI:** `npm run pdf:route -- <file.pdf|dir> [...] [--json] [--thresholds-from-env]` (`server/pdf-route.mjs`) parses with pdf.js (on the pool when configured) and prints per document the page count and pages per route, per page the route, reasons and signals; never page text, never a docling-serve call. Default thresholds unless `--thresholds-from-env`; dotenv is loaded only when it runs as a command (`DOTENV_CONFIG_PATH` honoured); exit 0, 1 (a file could not be parsed; the rest are still reported) or 2 (bad arguments or an invalid `PDF_AUTO_*`).
  - **Eval arm:** `eval:layout-parsing -- --parsers <base>,auto` (`createLayoutParsers`) ingests what `PDF_PARSER=auto` would and adds `routing.auto` to the report (settings, pages per route, Docling pages, fallback pages, requests and failed ones, in total and per paper). Its pdf.js part follows `PDF_PARAGRAPH_DETECTION` while the `pdfjs` arm forces it off, so `pdfjs-paragraphs,auto` isolates the routing.
  - **Tests:** `pdf-page-extraction`, `pdf-page-router`, `pdf-auto-parser` (injected signals and a fetch stub that reads each multipart form; in-flight requests held on deferred promises, never timing), `pdf-auto-end-to-end` (real pdf.js on a generated PDF, the real router and limiter, a docling-serve stand-in, and the same result on the pool), `pdf-parse-pool`, `pdf-route-cli` (a child process; no page text in its output), `pdf-parser-health`, `docling-parser`, `pdf-loader` and `layout-parsing-eval`; `test/pdf-fixture-builder.mjs` is a PDF-bytes helper, not a suite.
  - **Measured against a real docling-serve** (docs/evaluation.md "真实 docling-serve 上的验证"; docling-serve-cpu 1.37.0 on CPU, 30 QASPER papers, 266 pages): ranged requests come back with the original page numbers; after the cross-page split, 89/105 of `auto`'s Docling pages are identical to a whole-document conversion and the rest differ by a word or two at the cut (min token Dice 0.998); `auto` took 252-267 s against 330 s for whole-document `docling` (it sends 39% of the pages, but every request uploads the whole file and pays a fixed cost); no request failed. One hand-made scanned PDF (3 pages rendered at 150 dpi) routed to `ocr` with token recall 0.93-0.97 against the original text. Answer quality shows no difference (above). Not measured: real scanned archives, garbled text layers, other docling-serve versions or a GPU. With the defaults the 38 local PDFs route 105 of 290 pages to `layout` (every QASPER paper has at least one, the synthetic documents none) and none to `ocr`. Do not describe `auto` as better.
- Structured chunker (`chunkPageWithStructure` in `server/rag/chunker.js`) never drops text. Each line of a page is one unit (with `PDF_PARAGRAPH_DETECTION` on, one paragraph; off, one rendered line), and table rows and "Label:" lead-ins pass `isLikelyHeading`. A heading-like line replaced by the next one before any body text is kept in `leading`, in reading order in front of the heading in force. The chunk's `sectionHeading` stays the heading in force, and `leading` counts toward `chunkSize`. When `leading` does not fit with the next body paragraph, the heading lines become a chunk of their own and the body chunk looks exactly as before. Heading lines at the end of a page are appended to that page's last chunk when they fit, or become a chunk of their own. A heading never carries over to the next page. `findUnindexedPageText(page, chunks)` lists a page's tokens that no chunk of the page contains; `test/chunker-lost-text.test.mjs` uses it. It is a token-set check: a dropped repeat of a word that occurs elsewhere on the page goes unseen. On the 55 local PDFs (pdf.js, structured/900/180) the old chunker dropped 612 paragraph units, 7,866 non-whitespace characters, on 119 of 336 pages in 28 documents; now none (docs/evaluation.md "切块丢字"). The seven synthetic corpora (34 documents) chunk byte-identically, so deterministic evals and the pinned baseline are unchanged. The simple (fixed-window) strategy is unchanged. Existing archives keep their old chunks until they are re-chunked: run `npm run vector:reindex -- --from documents --apply` (a `PUT /documents/:docId` replace also re-chunks that document; re-uploading the same bytes does not, because `RAG_INGEST_DEDUP` resolves it to the stored document). An index-version build re-parses each document's stored bytes and re-chunks it with the builder process's settings (`buildDocumentChunks`), but its change check compares only `uploaded_at` and `content_version`, so a chunker or settings change alone does not mark a document as changed (the chunk profile below records it and warns). `rag/gap-planner.js` strips the heading line (`stripSectionHeading`: at the start, or as a whole line further down) to get body-only text, but `leading` lines still count as body text.
- Chunk profile and strategy validation (`rag/chunk-profile.js`; chunk text and boundaries are unchanged by it). `RAG_CHUNK_STRATEGY` must be one of `CHUNK_STRATEGIES` (structured, simple, sentence): blank counts as unset, any other value throws at ingest (as `PDF_PARSER` does), `chunkDocumentWithConfig` throws on an unknown explicit strategy, and `checks.chunking` (error) appears only then, only on the api and agent tiers, after `postgresTls` and before `tenantEncryption`, so the monolith keeps its 16 checks (`test/service-roles-server.test.mjs`).
  - **Profile:** `describeChunkProfile()` = `{id, chunkerVersion, strategy, size, overlap, pdfParser, paragraphDetection}`, id = first 12 hex of sha256 over the fields as sorted-key JSON (a Word, PowerPoint or Excel document's profile names its extractor instead of the PDF settings: "Office documents" below). `CHUNKER_VERSION` is 2 (1 = the structured chunker before 1dcc8467, 2026-10-04, whose overlong-paragraph split and paragraph overlap differed; that commit said "byte-identical" and was not: docs/evaluation.md "按句子装块"). Bump it whenever chunk text or boundaries change for the same pages and settings, even for a fix, and pin the new digests in `test/chunk-profile.test.mjs` under the new version, keeping the old entries; that suite pins each strategy's output over the synthetic corpora plus generated pages, and the default profile id.
  - **Stored chunks** carry `metadata.chunkProfileId`, set by `chunkDocumentPages` (ingest, PUT replace, trash restore), index-version builds and `vector:reindex --from documents`. Like `documentVersion` it never reaches a response (citations, traces, `/chat`, MCP and eval projections copy named fields; `rag.test.mjs` checks): new code that spreads chunk metadata into a response must drop it. Chunks stored before this have none.
  - **Index versions:** `createIndexVersion` records the profile in `index_params.chunkProfile`, which `normalizeIndexParams` ignores (so verification keys, DDL and `configuration_differs_from_active` are unchanged); version 1 and older versions are "unknown". `describeIndexVersions` warns `chunk_profile_differs_from_active` (or `chunk_profile_differs_from_version` for a building version or a ready one in its dual-write window) with recorded, current, fields and the settings that would restore it: a warning, never a problem, and in the health report only on the api and agent tiers (the CLI and `GET /admin/index-versions` always compare). After `vector:reindex --from documents --apply`, a version that records a profile gets the run's profile when the run covered every document, else `{mixed: true}`. The builder still chunks with its process's settings, not the recorded ones.
  - **Staged ingest:** the parse output carries `{pdfParser, paragraphDetection}` (an Office document's parse metadata instead) as envelope `meta`, the chunk output its profile. An attempt resuming at embed or index with chunks whose chunker version, strategy, size or overlap (`CHUNK_PROFILE_RECHUNK_FIELDS`) differ moves the job back to the chunk stage (fenced `advanceStage`, no output) at most once per job (`rechunked`); outputs without a profile are reused. Workers on one queue must still share chunking settings.
  - **Eval reports:** `eval:qasper-retrieval`, `eval:qasper-answers` and `eval:layout-parsing` record `config.chunking` (`describeChunkingConfig`: strategy, size, overlap, chunkerVersion) and `config.git` (`describeEvaluationCheckout`: sha, dirty, changedPaths, ignoring the eval's own outputs; null without git), layout-parsing also `config.pdf`; per row `admittedChars` (qasper-retrieval, plus `summary.admittedCharsMean`) or `contextChars`; `--compare` adds `comparison.configDifferences` (an older report counts as unknown) and a paired `admittedChars` delta where both runs measured it. Since 2026-10-28 `eval:qasper-retrieval` also records `config.retrieval` (`describeRetrievalConfig`, every setting of the QA route), per row `docKey`, `evidenceRequirementCount`, `hitDenseRank` / `hitSparseRank` and `sparseOnlyCandidateCount` (candidates the sparse route returned and the dense route did not; always 0 under the default fusion, where the final list is a subset of the dense top max(top-K, sparse top-K), 10 by default), and `timing`; its `--compare` takes `--iterations` (default 4000), refuses a report of another corpus, seed or case count (`assertComparableSample`), and gives every recall metric `flips` (+won / -lost) and, when every paired row names its paper, `ci95ByPaper` (`bootstrapClusterMeanInterval` in `paired-bootstrap.js`: resamples papers with all their questions, drawn after the question-level intervals so those are unchanged; dev has about three questions per paper). Since 2026-10-29 `eval:qasper-answers` records `config.retrieval` as well and `eval:abstention-gate` records `config.retrieval`, `config.chunking` and `config.git`, so an arm of a sweep is provable from any of the three reports, and `compare:qasper-answers` (and `--compare`) also pairs `answeredBothF1` (official F1 over the answerable questions both runs answered, free of the composition change an answer-rate shift brings) and `contextCharsBothAnswered` (the characters of context the model read on those questions: a retrieval setting's cost per answer). No gate requires these fields.
  - **Evaluation archives:** every runner that ingests into a throwaway archive sets it with `useEvaluationDataDirectory` (`evaluation/eval-data-directory.mjs`), never `process.env.RAG_DATA_DIRECTORY` alone: `rag/storage.js` fixes its directory on first import and falls back to an existing `server/data/rag`, so a runner whose static imports loaded it first ingested into the local archive and computed BM25 over it (2026-10-18: dev candidate coverage 0.663 from the main checkout against 0.704 clean; 664 leaked QASPER papers were removed). `test/eval-data-directory.test.mjs` refuses a runner that sets the variable alone. Compare retrieval numbers only from runs made this way or in a clean worktree.
- Office documents (Word, PowerPoint, Excel): `.docx`, `.pptx` and `.xlsx` are ingested next to PDF (`rag/document-formats.js` detection, `rag/document-format-errors.js` refusals, `rag/document-loader.js` the one loader entry point, `rag/office/` the archive guard and the three loaders). With `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS=pdf` every entry point checks, answers and names its stored files exactly as before Office formats (51cb6aec); under every setting a PDF's parse, chunks, chunk profile, citations, context block, prompts and SQL are unchanged (one statement aside: the index-version build's `index_versions:document_source` SELECT also reads `mime_type`), and the new fields appear only on non-PDF objects, never as null or undefined keys.
  - **Settings** (`rag/config.js`; blank = unset): `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS` (comma list of `pdf`, `docx`, `pptx`, `xlsx`, any case, duplicates ignored; default all four; an unknown entry or a list naming nothing throws `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS_INVALID`) gates only what new uploads, PUT replacements, the async enqueue behind them and `archive-ingest` accept. `RAG_OFFICE_MAX_UNCOMPRESSED_BYTES` (268435456), `RAG_OFFICE_MAX_ZIP_ENTRIES` (10000), `RAG_OFFICE_MAX_COMPRESSION_RATIO` (200, for entries inflating to more than 1 MiB), `RAG_XLSX_MAX_CELLS` (2000000), `RAG_OFFICE_MAX_XML_BYTES` (16777216: the XML one parser holds at once, see "Parser bounds") and `RAG_OFFICE_MAX_TEXT_CHARS` (67108864: the text one file may extract to) must be whole numbers >= 1 (the ratio any number >= 1; a zip-bomb guard has no `0 = no limit`), else `RAG_OFFICE_LIMIT_INVALID`. `assertDocumentFormatSettings` in `createAppServices` refuses the start (monolith, api and agent tiers, every eval that builds an app); `archive-ingest` reads `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS` before any file; a value made invalid at run time makes the upload routes answer 500 with its code. `vector:index` and `vector:reindex` never read `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS` and meet a bad limit only when they read an Office file; `worker:ingest` checks all seven at its start (`assertDocumentFormatSettings`), since it reads `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS` to read a queued file as a PDF-only server queued it (`resolveQueuedDocumentFormats`, Re-parse paths below), so give workers the API's value.
  - **Detection** (`detectDocumentFormatAtPath` / `inspectDocumentBytes`, on the plaintext after working-file decryption, reserved envelope and working-file magic refused first): the bytes decide. A name or client MIME type only gets a file past multer (`filterUploadFile`: with an Office format enabled, any supported extension or MIME type, so a disabled format meets its 415 rather than a name refusal) and the chunked upload's name check; a `.doc`, `.docm` or `.ppsx` name is refused there with 400 `DOCUMENT_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`, so the 415 legacy and variant codes answer bytes behind a supported name or MIME type, typically a password-protected .docx. PDF = `%PDF` in the first 1024 bytes, except that with any Office format enabled a file beginning with a ZIP local header (`50 4B 03 04`) or an OLE2 header is never a PDF. OOXML = a ZIP whose `_rels/.rels` officeDocument relationship (transitional or Strict) names a main part with the docx, pptx or xlsx main content type of `[Content_Types].xml`; a ZIP without `[Content_Types].xml` is unsupported, another `.main+xml` type (macro-enabled, template, slide show, Visio, add-ins, binary workbooks) a variant. Stored MIME types are `DOCUMENT_MIME_TYPES` (`application/pdf` and the three OOXML document types). With PDF alone no ZIP is opened and nothing new is refused.
  - **Refusals** (`DocumentFormatError`: `status`, `code`, `expose: true`, a short message that never quotes the file; routes answer `{ code, error }`): 400 `DOCUMENT_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED` (with PDF alone the old "The uploaded file is not a valid PDF." with no `code`; reserved-prefix plaintext and a working file this process cannot decrypt land here too), 415 `DOCUMENT_FORMAT_DISABLED`, 415 `DOCUMENT_FORMAT_LEGACY_OFFICE` (OLE2: a .doc, .ppt or .xls, or any password-protected Office file, which encryption wraps in OLE2), 415 `DOCUMENT_FORMAT_OFFICE_VARIANT`, 413 `DOCUMENT_ARCHIVE_TOO_LARGE`, 400 `DOCUMENT_ARCHIVE_INVALID`, 413 `DOCUMENT_SPREADSHEET_TOO_LARGE`; pages without text stay 422 without a code and say "document" instead of "PDF" for an Office file (`createNoExtractableTextError`). With an Office format enabled a file part multer dropped is 400 `DOCUMENT_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`, a request without one 400 "A document file is required.", a refused chunked-upload name 400 `DOCUMENT_FORMAT_UNSUPPORTED`, and stored working names keep the original name's extension (`createStoredFileName`). A coded refusal thrown past the byte check (a loader's 413 or 400 in a synchronous ingest) adds `code` to the route's error body (`describeDocumentFormatErrorCode`); an async job's `error` keeps its message (`expose: true`).
  - **Archive guard** (`scanOfficeArchive` in `rag/office/zip-guard.js`, on yauzl): runs at the byte check and again inside `loadDocumentPages`, so a synchronous Office upload inflates twice, both times bounded. Declared sizes come first, so a bomb is refused before anything inflates; then every entry is inflated once and its real bytes counted against the same caps, stopping at the chunk that crosses one, and its size and CRC-32 checked against the directory. 413: more entries than the cap (read from the end record before any directory record), more inflated bytes (declared or actual), a higher ratio, a `[Content_Types].xml` or `_rels/.rels` over `PACKAGE_PART_MAX_BYTES` (4 MiB, fixed: the guard parses both at every byte check; a real one is under 1.5 MB at 10000 entries). 400: not a readable ZIP; an unsafe name (`..`, absolute, a drive letter, NUL, a backslash, an Info-ZIP Unicode Path rename); an encrypted entry; a method other than store and deflate; two names equal ignoring case; an ambiguous end record (trailing bytes included), ZIP64, or local headers that disagree with the directory; any `.xml` or `.rels` part declaring a DTD (`<!DOCTYPE` / `<!ENTITY`, any case, UTF-8 or UTF-16). The scan returns `partSizes` (each file entry's verified inflated size, lower-case name). It is stricter than OOXML needs on purpose: JSZip (inside mammoth) and yauzl read the central directory while SheetJS trusts the local headers, so every archive they could read differently is refused, although SheetJS now only ever reads a workbook rebuilt from verified parts (XLSX below). `readWorkbookParts` (`office/xlsx-plan.js`) also refuses an .xlsx holding `META-INF/manifest.xml`, `objectdata.xml` or `Index/Document.iwa`, the parts of a disguised ODS or Numbers file.
  - **Parser bounds:** mammoth builds trees of about 60-115 times the XML it reads and the PPTX reader 25-50 times one slide part (measured: a 3.9 MB .docx with an 85 MB document.xml, or an 18 MB slide, exhausted the heap), so the total cap alone let a few-MB file abort the process. `assertOfficeXmlWithinLimit` (`zip-guard.js`, called by `inspectDocumentBytes` once the format is known, so already at the upload check) applies `RAG_OFFICE_MAX_XML_BYTES` from the verified sizes: a Word document's `.xml`/`.rels` parts together (mammoth reads them all before converting), each part of a deck on its own; for a workbook `readWorkbookParts` applies it to the workbook part, its relationships, shared strings and styles, while worksheets are bounded by their cell elements (XLSX below). 413 `DOCUMENT_ARCHIVE_TOO_LARGE`. At 16 MiB mammoth may still need up to about 2 GB of heap: fine for the default V8 heap of a 64-bit host with 16 GB, too much for a small container, where the setting should be lowered. `RAG_OFFICE_MAX_TEXT_CHARS` bounds the text each loader writes (`createTextBudget` in `office/table-text.js`; 413 `DOCUMENT_ARCHIVE_TOO_LARGE`): table lines are spent as they are written (labels repeat on every row), an XLSX cell's raw text before it is copied (shared strings and formatted text repeat), then the pages. A header cell over `MAX_HEADER_LABEL_LENGTH` (200) labels nothing in any format (its column letter does; the header line keeps the text once): a 25 KB workbook with a 32,000-character header used to spell out 96 MB. Nesting: `MAX_OFFICE_XML_NESTING` (256, `office/xml-nesting.js`) bounds how deep an XML part may nest, the PPTX reader through fast-xml-parser's `maxNestedTags`, a Word document through `assertDocxXmlNesting` (`docx-loader.js`, a one-pass count over every `.xml`/`.rels` part before mammoth runs; 400 `DOCUMENT_ARCHIVE_INVALID`): mammoth recurses on nesting, and without the cap the depth a document could reach depended on the parsing thread's stack (on Node 24, nested smart tags were read at 1,500 levels on both threads and refused at 3,000 on the main thread while a parse worker, whose stack is larger, read them). Loops replace every argument spread over file-sized lists (headings, notes lines, `mc:AlternateContent` children, a page's chunks), which past about 125,000 items threw an uncoded RangeError the ingest queue retried.
  - **Pages** (`{ pageNumber, text, locationUnit?, locationLabel? }`; pageNumber a positive integer, deterministic for the same bytes and library versions because feedback records and workspace artifacts persist it; `assertOfficePages` throws on a loader that breaks the shape). PDF: unchanged, no location keys.
    - PPTX (`office/pptx-loader.js`, this repo's reader on fast-xml-parser with entity processing off and namespaces matched by URI, so Strict files read): one page per visible slide that has text, in `p:sldIdLst` order through the presentation's relationships; pageNumber = the slide's deck position (hidden slides, `show="0"` or `"false"`, keep their numbers); `slide`; label = the title or ctrTitle placeholder's text. Text: the title, then the other shapes by their own `a:off` (y, then x; shapes without one, including placeholders whose position the layout gives, after them in document order; groups flattened), one paragraph per line (runs, fields and `a:br` joined), bulleted, numbered or indented paragraphs as `- ` lines, tables one line per row labelled by the first row, then `Notes:` and the speaker notes (slide-number, date, footer and slide-image placeholders skipped).
