A slide paragraph's bullet follows PowerPoint's inheritance (`createInheritanceReader`): its own `a:pPr`, its shape's `a:lstStyle`, the layout placeholder with its `idx` (else its type), the master placeholder of its kind (title, body, date/footer/number), the master's `p:txStyles` (title, body, other for non-placeholders); the first that says anything decides (`a:buNone` or a bullet), so an ordinary content slide's body text, which takes its bullets from the master, reads as list lines; a layout or master that is missing or unreadable only loses the inherited bullets. Speaker notes use their own and their shape's list style only. The loader passes the presentation part the guard classified (`presentationPart`), and the reader also gets `_rels/.rels` to find it on its own. `mc:AlternateContent` reads the first `mc:Choice`, else the fallback; charts, SmartArt, pictures and equations add nothing; external relationship targets are never read.
    - XLSX (`office/xlsx-loader.js`, SheetJS): one page per visible worksheet with a non-empty cell, in workbook order; pageNumber = the sheet's workbook position (hidden, very hidden and chart sheets counted, never emitted); `sheet`; label = the sheet name; first line `Sheet: <name>`. Cells by displayed text (their number format applied, else the value), formulas by their cached value, never evaluated. Header row = the first of the first 20 non-empty rows with at least two non-empty cells and at least half as many as the widest of those rows; rows above it and the header row itself are plain lines (cells joined by `; `), every later row `Header: value; ...` (a cell under an empty header labelled by its column letter); a merged range keeps its value in the top-left cell only (coalesced intervals, binary search: 400,000 ranges used to take 27 s); empty rows are skipped. More than `RAG_XLSX_MAX_CELLS` non-empty cells across the visible sheets refuses the workbook, never truncates it. SheetJS never sees the uploaded ZIP: `extractXlsxPagesFromArchive` hands it a stored ZIP rebuilt from verified parts (`office/xlsx-plan.js`: the visible worksheets under `xl/worksheets/sheetN.xml`, the shared strings the workbook's relationships name (else the first `[Content_Types].xml` override of their type, as SheetJS found them), and a `[Content_Types].xml`, workbook part, relationships and styles written there), because SheetJS chose its own parts (the first workbook override in `[Content_Types].xml`, tags inside comments, worksheets of any name), read comments, drawings, charts and external links, and gave a stub to every cell of a hyperlink's range. A first pass reads the original workbook part and styles alone, for the sheet list, `date1904`, the number formats and each cell format's number format id: a format over 255 characters (SheetJS tokenizes it for every cell: a 9 KB workbook with a 100,000-character format exhausted the heap) or with more than one `@` in a section is 400 `DOCUMENT_ARCHIVE_INVALID` (`assertReadableNumberFormats`). The second pass gets styles written from that view (`buildStylesXml`: number formats and cell formats only, plus `SHEETJS_IMPLICIT_NUMBER_FORMATS`, the formats SheetJS loads for ids a workbook uses without defining, checked against the installed SheetJS by a test) and runs with `cellText` off and `cellNF` on: every cell keeps its format string and is formatted one at a time by SheetJS's own formatter (`rawCellText`), giving the text eager formatting gave (a test compares both over built-in, implicit, custom and remapped formats), while eager formatting kept every cell's text alive at once (a 310 KB workbook of 1.9M cells under a 254-character date format needed some 5 GB; now 0.8 GB RSS before the text cap refuses it). Before the second pass `prepareWorksheets` counts every worksheet's cell elements as SheetJS's own patterns find them (`<c` with any prefix, row ends, merged ranges; UTF-16 rewritten as UTF-8 first, a part SheetJS would read as UTF-16 refused) and refuses more than `XLSX_CELL_PRECHECK_FACTOR` (2) times the limit, styled empty cells included, with 413 `DOCUMENT_SPREADSHEET_TOO_LARGE`; hyperlink tags are renamed. Sheet names go back with `_xHHHH_` escapes protected, so labels are what SheetJS read before. SheetJS holds every parsed cell in memory (about 130 bytes a numeric cell, measured).
    - DOCX (`office/docx-loader.js`, mammoth with `externalFileAccess: false`, images dropped unread and the document's embedded style map ignored; its HTML read by a small parser of mammoth's own output grammar): headings, paragraphs, list items as `- ` lines, tables like XLSX (first row as header, also kept as a plain line), footnotes and endnotes as `[n] text` lines after the body. One page per section, cut at the highest heading level the document uses (the smallest hN present), each starting with its heading line; `section`; label = the heading's text. Text before the first such heading is section 1 without a label; a document without headings is one page without a label. mammoth does not convert page headers and footers, and its default style map leaves comments out, so neither is extracted. The row and label helpers are shared by all three (`office/table-text.js`).
  - **Parse metadata and chunk profile:** an Office parse is `{ format, extractor, extractorVersion }`: `mammoth` with its package.json version, `sheetjs` with `XLSX.version`, `ooxml-pptx` with `PPTX_EXTRACTOR_VERSION` (2 since bullets follow the layout and master; bump it whenever the PPTX text or page numbers change for the same bytes). `describeChunkProfile(officeParse)` = `{id, chunkerVersion, strategy, size, overlap, format, extractor, extractorVersion}` (`OFFICE_CHUNK_PROFILE_FIELDS`), so Office chunks carry a `chunkProfileId` of their own; a PDF's fields and id are unchanged, and so is `CHUNK_PROFILE_RECHUNK_FIELDS`. The staged parse stage stores the parse as `meta.parse`, the chunk stage reads it back (`readStoredDocumentParse`), and the chunk output adds the `mimeType` the index stage commits. Gaps: a change to the DOCX or XLSX flattening code moves no id (only a library version does; add a layout version like the PPTX one if that matters), and `index_params.chunkProfile` still records the building process's PDF profile, so no `chunk_profile_differs_*` warning ever names an Office extractor.
  - **Chunks, citations and registry:** `chunkDocumentWithConfig` copies a page's `locationUnit` and `locationLabel` onto every chunk cut from it, after the strategy cut the page (text and boundaries unchanged, `CHUNKER_VERSION` stays 2); the label is stored already cut by `normalizeDocumentLocationLabel` (one line, 200 code points), since every chunk of the page repeats it (a 100,000-character heading over 3,000 chunks stored 300 MB of metadata). `pickDocumentLocation` (`rag/citations.js`: units slide, sheet and section only; a label only with a unit, on one line, without control characters, at most 200 code points) feeds every projection, which adds the fields only when present: `buildCitation`, the answer writer's retrieved contexts, MCP `formatCitation` / `formatEvidence` / `formatAskResult` (the `ask` tool description documents them), RAG traces, feedback `sanitizeCitation`, workspace-artifact citation manifests, `citation.verify` checks, the report export (`slide 3 · Title`, `sheet Revenue`, `section 2 · Heading`) and the research brief's summary (`on slides 1, 3`). `buildContextSection` prints `Slide: N`, `Sheet: <name>` (else `Sheet: N`) or `Section: N` instead of `Page: N`, and the answer writer screens the sheet name, the only label that line prints, for prompt injection like the file name. Location is not identity: dedupe and evidence-identity keys are unchanged. pgvector's metadata JSONB and the local stores keep whole metadata, Qdrant's payload whitelist carries both fields (`copyDocumentLocationFields`, and `vector:reindex --from qdrant` copies them back, `toQdrantChunkEntry`), the inventory Skill counts slides, sheets and sections by the MIME type (`<n> pages` for a PDF as before), deletion redaction covers `locationLabel`, and `FILE_EXTENSION_TERMS` gained ppt, pptx, xls and xlsx so a shared extension is never a document alias. `commitDocument({ mimeType })` records the type for an Office document only (a PDF's registration is unchanged; the registry's default is `application/pdf`); `GET /documents/:docId/file` sends the stored type, inline for a PDF, `attachment` otherwise. Prompt templates still say "uploaded PDFs" on purpose: changing them would move every prompt fingerprint and the pinned baselines.
  - **Re-parse paths** read a stored document as its registered MIME type (`resolveStoredDocumentFormats`: `application/pdf`, a missing or an unknown type = PDF alone, straight to `loadPdfPages`), whatever `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS` says now: index-version builds (`loadStoredDocument`; `index_versions:document_source` also selects `mime_type`), `vector:reindex --from documents`, and the trash restore (`chunkStoredDocument`, which keeps the restored row's type). Queued jobs (the staged parse stage's `loadQueuedDocument` and the worker's one-step path) read every format, since the enqueue checked the bytes, with one exception (`resolveQueuedDocumentFormats` in `document-loader.js`): jobs record no format, and a file that begins with a ZIP or OLE2 header yet holds `%PDF` in its first 1024 bytes is a PDF to `RAG_DOCUMENT_FORMATS=pdf` and Office otherwise, so while the setting is PDF alone every file its PDF check passes is read as a PDF (as at 51cb6aec; the Office parsers never see bytes a PDF-only server queued). Only such a polyglot queued under one setting and parsed under the other is read differently from its enqueue. A stored Office file the loader refuses (a limit lowered since its upload) is an unreadable source like a TENANT_* one: `unreadable_source DOCUMENT_*` in builds (held back by the gate unless `--allow-unreadable-sources`), skipped with its chunks kept by `vector:reindex`. `ingestDocument` reads PDF alone unless its caller passes `formats` (the upload routes and `archive-ingest` pass the detected format, the worker every format), so the arXiv importer is unchanged.
  - **Dependencies and runtime:** mammoth 1.13.0 and yauzl 3.4.0 pinned exactly, fast-xml-parser ^5.12.0, and SheetJS CE 0.20.3 as the vendored tarball `server/vendor/xlsx-0.20.3.tgz` (`"xlsx": "file:vendor/xlsx-0.20.3.tgz"`, from cdn.sheetjs.com, sha256 `8dc73fc3b00203e72d176e85b50938627c7b086e607c682e8d3c22c02bb99fe8`), imported as `xlsx`. Never install the npm-registry `xlsx` package instead: it is frozen at 0.18.5 with CVE-2023-30533 and CVE-2024-22363, and the fixes ship only on the vendor's CDN. The Dockerfile copies `server/vendor` before `npm ci`, `.dockerignore` must not leave it out, and `test/deployment-contract.test.mjs` pins both. The image was built once (2026-11-11, from 4db859c6): npm 10.9.9 in the image installed the tarball under `--legacy-peer-deps`, and in the container (Node 22.23.4) the loaders parsed a .docx, .pptx, .xlsx and a PDF exactly as on the host (Node 24). Against pgvector and the demo fake model the container answered uploads with 201 (415 for OLE2 bytes under a .docx name), returned slide, sheet and section citations through `/chat` and MCP `ask`, and sent the stored bytes back (an Office file as an attachment, a PDF inline). The Office loaders are imported on first use, so a PDF-only process never loads mammoth or SheetJS; since 2026-11-13 they run on a parse worker by default (next bullet). The image has not been rebuilt since the worker came in (its files sit next to `rag/document-loader.js`, which the image copies with the rest of `server/`).
  - **Parse workers (2026-11-13; `RAG_OFFICE_PARSE_WORKERS`, default 1, 0 = the calling thread as before, capped at `MAX_OFFICE_PARSE_WORKERS` = 64):** `loadDocumentPages` reads and decrypts the file in the calling thread (the working-file key lives there), then hands bytes that begin with a ZIP header to `runOfficeParseTask` (`rag/office-parse-pool.js`), whose worker (`rag/office-parse-worker.js`) runs the same `parseDocumentBytes` (`rag/office/office-document-pages.js`: `inspectDocumentBytes` with the archive guard, then mammoth, SheetJS or the PPTX reader) the calling thread runs with 0. That module must never import pdf.js (`test/office-parse-pool.test.mjs` walks the worker's import graph); a PDF it recognizes goes back to the caller's `loadPdfPages`.
    - **Same result:** pages, `parse` and refusals are the calling thread's, and the old loader's (before/after check below). Where a reader's own limits depend on the thread (stack depth), they are made explicit so they do not: the Word XML nesting cap (Parser bounds above) came from the review of this change. A refusal comes back as a `DocumentFormatError` (status, code, message, `expose`, its cause's name and message), anything else through `restoreSerializedError`. Workers share the process's environment (`SHARE_ENV`), so a `RAG_OFFICE_*` or `RAG_XLSX_MAX_CELLS` value changed at run time applies on the worker at once. Each task posts its own transferred copy of the bytes. A caller that stands in a PPTX reader (`importPptxReader`, tests) parses in the calling thread.
    - **Failures:** a worker that runs out of memory (`ERR_WORKER_OUT_OF_MEMORY`, under `RAG_OFFICE_PARSE_WORKER_MAX_OLD_GENERATION_MB`, 0 = Node's default) refuses its document with 413 `DOCUMENT_ARCHIVE_TOO_LARGE`, which the ingest queue does not retry: one task runs on a worker at a time, so the document is what needed the memory. Any other worker death is `OFFICE_PARSE_WORKER_FAILED` (500), `RAG_OFFICE_PARSE_TIMEOUT_MS` (0 = none) `OFFICE_PARSE_TIMEOUT` (504), a stopped pool `OFFICE_PARSE_POOL_STOPPED` (503, `app.locals.stop` stops it with the pdf.js pool), a cancelled queued task `OFFICE_PARSE_TASK_CANCELLED` (499); the queue retries the 5xx. The upload's format check (`detectDocumentFormatAtPath`) still runs the guard in the request thread: it inflates and checks, it builds no document tree.
    - **Scheduling:** `rag/worker-task-pool.js` (`createWorkerTaskPool`), shared with the pdf.js pool, which was moved onto it unchanged (its 16 tests pass as they were): FIFO, workers spawned on demand, `ref()`'d only while running a task, `execArgv: []`, a new pool on a changed size or memory limit. Metrics: `rag/metrics-office-pool.js` (`archive_rag_office_parse_pool_size`, `_workers{state}`, `_queued_tasks`, `_tasks_total{outcome}`, `_worker_restarts_total`).
    - **Measured (2026-11-13, Node 24, Apple Silicon; one-off scripts kept in the ignored `evaluation/generated/office-parse-worker/`):** `deepStrictEqual` results in both modes on 72 files (the 64 native files of the Office-vs-PDF corpus, the 5 Office-written fixtures, 3 generated large ones), four rounds each, order alternated. Longest event-loop delay while a load ran: the generated 3,000-paragraph .docx 56 ms in the calling thread against 2 ms on the worker, the 300-slide .pptx 65 against 2, the 200,000-cell .xlsx 244 against 2; the corpus files at most 6 against 2. Over two runs the worker added 0.1 to 0.9 ms per corpus file on average for each format (single files -3.4 to +4.7 ms, slower and faster alike) and 6 to 25 ms (+4% to +69%) per generated large one; posting the bytes and pages is under 1 ms of that, the rest is the parse itself running slower on the worker thread by an amount that varied between runs (cause not isolated). With only `RAG_OFFICE_PARSE_WORKER_MAX_OLD_GENERATION_MB=48` (no process flag) the .xlsx was refused with 413 and the next document parsed on a new worker; with the process heap at 48 MB (`--max-old-space-size=48`) the same parse in the calling thread aborted the process (V8 heap out of memory, exit 134); with no limit both modes read it. Before/after: the loader of 5600a849 (before the worker) and the current one in both modes give the same result on all 72 files, `deepStrictEqual` and `JSON.stringify` alike.
    - **Memory limit caveat:** Node applies a process-wide `--max-old-space-size` (command line or `NODE_OPTIONS`) to every worker in place of its `resourceLimits.maxOldGenerationSizeMb` (Node 24.21: a worker given 64 MB under `--max-old-space-size=512` got the main thread's 704 MB limit). The limit then does nothing, and each worker may grow as large as the main thread: budget (1 + workers) heaps or leave the flag off. Both pools warn once (`findProcessHeapLimitFlag`); the image, compose files and CI set no such flag.
    - **Tests:** `test/office-parse-pool.test.mjs` (parity on the fixtures and the Office-written files with 0, 1 and 2 workers, every refusal compared field by field, Word documents nested just under and over the cap and 3,000 deep, a setting read live, the overridden-memory-limit warning in a child process, out of memory, exit, uncaught error, timeout, cancel, stop, the import graph, an idle pool letting its process exit, metrics) and `test/office-xml-nesting.test.mjs` (the count); every other Office suite runs on the worker by default.
  - **Frontend:** the uploader accepts `.pdf,.docx,.pptx,.xlsx` (extension or browser MIME type; the server decides) and shows the server's refusal message (`getUploadErrorMessage`). `src/documentLocation.js` labels a citation by its unit ("Slide N · title", "Sheet name" else "Sheet N", "Section N · heading", zh too) and decides inline preview (`hasInlineDocumentPreview`: a PDF or a missing MIME type); any other type shows the file name, the cited text and an authenticated download (`useAuthenticatedDocumentDownload`), never a page-1 fallback.
  - **Not yet:** MCP `archive_list_documents` carries no `mimeType`; claim checks and the verdict override read `sectionHeading`, never `locationLabel`; no legacy formats and no OCR of pictures. Retrieval and answers have been measured once, on synthetic workbooks and decks and on QASPER papers set in Word ("Office files against Office's own PDF export" under Tests And Evaluation).
  - **Tests:** `office-format-detect` (settings, every detection rule, the PDF-only rule against `hasPdfMagicBytes` on the same bytes), `office-zip-guard`, `office-docx-loader`, `office-xlsx-loader`, `office-pptx-loader`, `document-loader`, `office-document-ingest` (every entry point and parse site over the local store and file registry, the async staged pipeline, the restore, PDF-only mode, the start-up refusals), `archive-ingest-office` (the CLI as a child process with an empty dotenv file), `office-citation-location` (projections, the chunker's label cut, the inventory wording) and `office-citation-location-stores` (Qdrant, local and a fake pgvector runtime), `office-documents-postgres.integration` (needs `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and is skipped without it; it is not in `npm run test:pgvector`, whose file list `ci-workflow.test.mjs` pins, so CI runs it through the coverage gate and locally `TEST_FILES=test/office-documents-postgres.integration.test.mjs bash scripts/run-pgvector-integration.sh`), the vendored-tarball case of `deployment-contract`, both settings in `reserved-file-prefix`, `working-file-upload-route` and `app`, and the re-parse cases in `vector-reindex` and `vector-store-pgvector-version-lifecycle`. Fixtures come from `test/office-fixture-builder.mjs`, a helper, not a suite. Its packages are minimal (PowerPoint repairs its decks before it opens them).
    - **Office-written fixtures:** `office-real-files` loads five files that Word, PowerPoint and Excel 16.115 for Mac wrote themselves (`test/fixtures/office-real/`; its README says how each was made). It covers what the builder leaves out: themes, body bullets inherited from the master, notes pages, a hidden slide, Word's own tables, footnotes and endnotes, Excel's merged cells, number formats, formula cache and hidden sheet, and Office's zip layout. Each file's pages must equal the expected pages exactly. The suite also checks that `docProps/app.xml` names the application that wrote the file.
    - **Scrubbing:** `scrub-office-package.mjs` rewrote only two things, and every other entry is byte for byte what Office wrote. It set the author fields in `docProps/core.xml` to `Archive RAG test`, and Excel's saved-folder path (`x15ac:absPath` in `xl/workbook.xml`) to `/Users/test/Documents/`. The suite fails if a part names another `/Users/` folder or an author field holds anything else. Scrub any new Office-written file the same way before committing it.
    - **Frontend:** `src/documentLocation.test.js`, `PdfUploader`, `PdfPreview`, `RenderQA`, `archiveWorkspace`, `evidenceSpine`, `useAuthenticatedDocumentPreview` and `App`.
- Embedding task prefixes (`server/rag/config.js`): models documented to need them get them from `EMBEDDING_TASK_PREFIXES` (nomic-embed-text: `search_query: ` / `search_document: `), and `RAG_EMBEDDING_QUERY_PREFIX` / `RAG_EMBEDDING_DOCUMENT_PREFIX` override the table (an empty value turns one off). `openai.js` adds them on the real embedding client only, never for a configured stand-in provider, so deterministic evals are unchanged. Vectors embedded under one document prefix are another embedding space, so every store records `getEmbeddingIndexIdentity()` (the model, plus `#<document prefix>` when one applies): pgvector stores it in `embedding_model`, filters search by it, and fails closed on other identities at schema verification; the local and Qdrant stores record `embeddingIdentity` per chunk and give other identities no dense score (a chunk without one counts as the bare model). `vector:reindex --apply` is the one caller allowed past foreign identities (`ensurePgvectorSchema({ allowForeignEmbeddings: true })`), because it rewrites each document under the current identity. Changing the prefix table changes the identity of existing archives: say so in the change and point to `npm run vector:reindex -- --from documents --apply`.
- The pgvector dense route (`searchPgvectorDocuments`) sets `hnsw.iterative_scan` (`RAG_PGVECTOR_ITERATIVE_SCAN`, default `relaxed_order`; `ivfflat.iterative_scan` for IVFFlat) with `set_config(..., true)` sent as the search's `queryPostgres` prelude (the role step, the setting and the search run in one implicit transaction ended by one Sync: one round trip, on the primary or a replica; pg-native falls back to BEGIN ... COMMIT), or on the caller's transaction client, and restores strict distance order through a materialized CTE (`ORDER BY distance + 0`). Without it, a document-filtered query that the planner sends through HNSW returns fewer than topK rows, because the filter applies after the index returns its `ef_search` candidates. It needs pgvector 0.8+: `supportsPgvectorIterativeScan` checks the version that schema verification read, and older servers keep the plain statement, because they reserve the `hnsw.` prefix and setting it there is an error. `off` is the kill switch.
- Async upload ingestion: `RAG_INGEST_MODE=async` (default `sync`, which keeps the in-request 201 + document) makes `POST /upload` and `POST /upload/complete` run their existing validation and then enqueue the bytes in `rag_ingest_jobs` (migration 015, `INGEST_JOBS_POSTGRES_TABLE`, row-level security like `rag_documents`, counted in `checks.rowLevelSecurity`) and answer 202 `{jobId, docId, fileName, status}`; `GET /ingest-jobs/:jobId` reports the job to its own access scope only (another tenant gets 404) and adds `document` once it succeeded. The store (`server/rag/ingest-job-store.js`) is PostgreSQL when a database is configured, otherwise in-process memory with the same contract; the worker (`server/rag/ingest-worker.js`) claims as the owner with one `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` update (tenant-fair: fewest running jobs first), ingests under `runWithDatabaseTenant(job scope)` through the same `ragService.ingestDocument`, and fences every later write (renew/succeed/fail/release) on `job_id + claimed_by + attempt_count`. Keep that fence, keep worker ids unique, and keep the database (not the registry map) as the judge of whether an earlier attempt already committed the document. Retryable errors (408/409/425/429, 5xx, network) requeue with backoff no shorter than Retry-After; other 4xx fail at once only at parse and chunk, while at embed and index (a provider or configuration 4xx) they spend the stage's retries and then dead-letter, and an error's own `retryable` flag always wins (`isRetryableIngestError(error, stage)` in `rag/ingest-worker.js`); public job errors expose only the ingest path's own 413/415/422 messages, never a model backend's (`isUpstreamError`). Per-tenant caps on pending jobs and bytes return 429; finished rows and stale `ingest-tmp/` files are pruned by the worker. `server.js` starts a worker only in async mode (`resolveApiIngestWorkerPlan` forces the in-process worker when the queue is in memory or the vector store is `local`), and `server/ingest-worker.mjs` (`npm run worker:ingest`) runs only the loop, requires PostgreSQL and a shared vector store. Tests: `test/ingest-jobs.test.mjs`, `test/ingest-jobs-routes.test.mjs`, and `test/ingest-jobs-postgres.integration.test.mjs` (part of `test:pgvector`).
- Cross-instance document visibility depends on the registry backend, not the ingest mode. When `isDocumentRegistryShared()` (`server/rag/doc-registry.js`) is true, `loadDocumentsIngestedElsewhere` reads a docId the map misses and `refreshDocumentsIngestedElsewhere` re-lists the requesting tenant on `GET /documents`, in sync and async mode alike. It is true for a store whose `backend` is `postgres`, and for the default store when a database URL is set; a ragService may answer through its own `isDocumentRegistryShared`. The file-backed and in-memory registries skip both. A refresh keeps entries this process is writing (`trackDocumentWrite`, used by ingest, delete and clear). Idle ingest worker loops sleep `RAG_INGEST_WORKER_POLL_MS` (default 1000) and are woken through `store.subscribeToEnqueues`. In-process enqueues and releases call subscribers directly. The PostgreSQL enqueue and release statements `pg_notify` the `<jobs table>_enqueued` channel, which one dedicated owner LISTEN session per store receives (`server/rag/postgres-notification-listener.js`: outside the pool, background connect, exponential-backoff reconnect, closed by `worker.stop()`). A wake-up is only a hint, so keep the poll.
- Tenant statement round trips: A tenant-scoped `queryPostgres` sends the tenant settings and the statement as one extended-protocol pipeline: Parse/Bind/Execute for each, then a single Sync, so the whole thing is one round trip. The implicit transaction ends at the Sync, so the settings stay transaction-local, a failure rolls back, and the connection goes back to the pool as the owner. Under a tenant, `withPostgresTransaction` sends BEGIN plus the settings as one round trip, then the callback's statements, then COMMIT. The role and ids are always bind parameters, never SQL text, so do not build them into SQL with escapeLiteral. If a tenant statement leaves a transaction block open (for example a statement text of BEGIN), the call fails with `ARCHIVE_RAG_TENANT_PIPELINE_STATUS` and the connection is destroyed instead of pooled. A connection whose ROLLBACK failed is also destroyed. Tenant statements always use the extended protocol, so one call cannot carry several semicolon-separated statements. The pipeline relies on pg's Submittable interface (`PipelinedQuery` in `rag/postgres.js`). When pg is upgraded, run `test/postgres-tenant-pipeline.test.mjs` (a fake server that pins the exact wire messages) and the RLS integration suite. The native pg bindings fall back to the four-round-trip path. Like `pool.query` on the owner path, a tenant statement commits at the Sync: a client-side error (a `query_timeout`, a disconnect after the Sync) leaves the outcome unknown rather than rolled back, so a write that must not commit after a client error belongs in `withPostgresTransaction`.
- Read replicas (`server/rag/postgres-replicas.js`, `POSTGRES_READ_REPLICA_URLS`, unset by default; unset changes nothing, including the pinned statement counts).
  - **What may go to a replica:** only `queryPostgres(text, values, { readOnly })` with `readOnly` = `true` or `{ guard }`, under an enforced database tenant, outside `withPostgresTransaction` (`canRouteReadToReplica()`). The replica gets one pipelined round trip: tenant settings, the guard (as the tenant), an optional prelude, the statement, one Sync, so row-level security there is the primary's replayed policy. The only callers that mark reads are the pgvector dense search (plain and iterative; the iterative `set_config` is the statement's prelude, on the replica as on the primary) and the sparse search (BM25 / pruning function, tenant `ts_rank_cd` function, plain statement) in `vector-store-pgvector.js`, and only when no caller client is passed and every searched docId is in this process's registry map.
  - **What must stay on the primary:** writes, migrations, LISTEN, advisory locks, every owner/system statement (so a request without an access scope, or any request under `POSTGRES_ROW_LEVEL_SECURITY=off`, gets no offload, and health does not say so), the index-version pointer snapshot, registry loads and refreshes, `GET /documents`, `GET /documents/:id/file`, and the run and task read APIs. Those reads are the read-your-writes anchors or have none; do not mark them `readOnly`.
  - **Consistency:** `buildDocumentFreshnessGuard` makes the replica prove, in the search's own round trip, that it holds each searched document at least at the `content_version` the registry read from the primary and the index pointer at least at the generation of the snapshot the search's version came from (taken before any await; another snapshot keeps the search on the primary). A failing guard raises SQLSTATE 22P02 with `archive_rag_replica_behind`, the primary answers, and the read counts as `version_behind`. The anchors: `buildChatResponse` refreshes the tenant's registry rows from the primary before the run (when that refresh fails, `refreshDocumentsIngestedElsewhere` returns false and the whole run goes through `runWithPrimaryReads`, counted as the bypass `registry_unverified`, because a registry that missed a replace or delete would let a lagging replica pass the guard), and the retrieval tier's `maskInvisibleDocIds` re-reads every requested docId from the primary when routing is on. This relies on pgvector writes, replaces and deletes committing the document row with its chunks.
  - **Lag guard:** a monitor samples the primary's `pg_current_wal_flush_lsn()` every `POSTGRES_READ_REPLICA_LAG_POLL_MS` and each replica's `pg_last_wal_replay_lsn()`; lag is the age of the newest primary sample the replica has replayed past (unknown means skipped; an LSN that moves back restarts sampling). `pg_last_xact_replay_timestamp()` is not used. Replicas over `POSTGRES_READ_REPLICA_MAX_LAG_MS` (default 2000), unreachable, not in recovery, with an open circuit, or with `POSTGRES_READ_REPLICA_POOL_MAX` reads in flight are skipped. Choice is least in flight, ties rotate; each replica has its own circuit (`_CIRCUIT_FAILURE_THRESHOLD` 3, `_CIRCUIT_COOLDOWN_MS` 5000; only unavailable errors count).
  - **Fallback:** every replica error falls back to the primary and is counted (`version_behind`, `lag_exceeded`, `lag_unknown`, `circuit_open`, `replica_down`, `replica_saturated`, `replica_unavailable`, `recovery_conflict`, `replica_error`); a replica never fails a request. An invalid `POSTGRES_READ_REPLICA_URLS` (including one that names no URL, such as `,`) or an invalid integer setting makes every `readOnly` statement throw and `checks.readReplicas` report `error`: fail closed, never a silent fallback.
  - **Observability:** `getReplicaRoutingSnapshot()` (reads, fallbacks, bypasses, per-replica state, lag, circuit; endpoints are host:port, never a URL) feeds `metrics-postgres.js`. `checks.readReplicas` appears only when the variable is set and the process hosts the retrieval tier; it is `ok` or `warning`, never `error` for lag or a down replica, so `/ready` stays 200.
  - **Limits:** replica statements have only a connect timeout (`POSTGRES_READ_REPLICA_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS`), not the request deadline; BM25 on a replica uses that replica's scope statistics, which may predate other writes of the same tenant within the lag bound; `vector:reindex --apply` rewrites chunks without bumping `content_version`, so a lagging replica can miss re-embedded rows until it catches up; a failed registry refresh at the start of `/chat` is only logged, so the guard can be stale for that request; nothing checks that a replica follows this primary (`system_identifier`); the lag bound assumes `synchronous_commit` is on.
  - **Tests:** `postgres-replica` (config, lag, classification, snapshot, health), `postgres-replica-routing` (routing and fallback over `postgres-replica-fake-server.mjs`, a helper), `postgres-replica-vector-search` (guard contents, snapshot races, the retrieval-tier re-read), `postgres-replica-load-bench` (report helpers), and `postgres-replica.integration` (real streaming replica with paused replay: upload, replace and delete on one instance, `/chat` on two; skipped without `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and `PGVECTOR_TEST_REPLICA_URL`). It covers two monolith instances only; the split topology under paused replay is not integration-tested.
- Agent run writes on the /chat path do not read the run back. `createWithEvent` and every CAS in `server/rag/postgres-agent-run-store.js` return the committed row together with its complete event list (a `run_events` CTE: the stored events UNION ALL the `recorded_event` rows, aggregated into parallel arrays so node-pg parses them exactly like `getEvents()` rows). `update`/`updateWithEvent` take the caller's `baseRun` at `expectedRevision` instead of re-reading. The service carries the latest snapshot through one invocation with a run cursor (`createAgentRunCursor` in `server/rag/agent-runs.js`, created in `runAgentRagInScope` and in graph resume, and passed to `createAgentRunStepLifecycle`, `completeRun`, `failRun`, `updateRun` and the graph checkpoint load/save). The cursor is an optimistic CAS base, not a cache: a mutation decided on it commits only if the revision CAS succeeds. A conflict, a no-op or a rejected decision is decided again from a fresh read with the method's normal retry budget. A decision that reads events (a standalone step next to a graph checkpoint) sets `requiresFreshRun`. Never share a cursor across requests, never answer a read API from it, and never use it for a mutation whose decision depends on events without `requiresFreshRun`. `test/agent-run-chat-statement-count.test.mjs` pins one /chat at 1 insert, 3 CAS, 2 event appends and 0 run or event reads: update it only together with a pg_stat_statements measurement. Its guarded custom-Skill cases (on LangGraph, the outer plan around them included since phase 7, held to the native scheduler's counts in its golden) count the in-memory store's calls and the test's own PostgreSQL stand-in (which models the plain `UPDATE` as returning the row alone); they are regression pins of that stand-in, and the header comment names the real pg_stat_statements measurement they were checked against. Each CAS write takes `SELECT ... FOR NO KEY UPDATE` on the run row in the same pipelined round trip first, so the returned event list includes every concurrent append that committed before it.
  - **Public event ids:** `rag_agent_run_events.event_id` (and the task events' id) is one BIGSERIAL every tenant shares, so the stores' `eventId` would let a tenant count other tenants' events from the gaps. Every run projection the service hands out (`stripInternalRunFields` in `agent-runs.js`: getRun, listRuns, the write results and the cursor's peek, so GET /agent-runs, the run actions and recovery) rewrites an all-digit id to `${runId}:${n}`, its rank by id within the run (the in-memory store's form; other ids are kept). The rank is stable because every event insert takes the run row's lock first and draws its id under it. The PostgreSQL store lists a run's events by that id (commit order), not by `created_at` (the writing transaction's start, taken before the lock, so two racing appends could list against their numbers), and a projection whose every id is a store id lists them in rank order too. The stores keep the BIGSERIAL: it orders the event list and pages the tenant export, whose event records name `${runId}:${n}` (`${taskId}:${n}` for task events; `createExportEventOrdinalMapper` in `rag/tenant-records.js`) instead of the raw id, and is what the store-level goldens pin; no code reads `eventId` for ordering, dedupe or "created after" checks (those use list position and type), and the frontend never reads it. Task event ids are never read back by any API.
- Index versions (`VECTOR_STORE_PROVIDER=pgvector` only; local and Qdrant keep one in-place index that `vector:reindex` rewrites).
  - Migration 016 adds three tables:
    - `rag_index_versions`: one row per version, with status building/ready/active/retired/failed, `chunk_table`, `sparse_rank_function`, the embedding space (`embedding_space_source`, model, identity, prefixes, dimensions), `index_params`, row counts, the build lease and `dual_write_until`.
    - `rag_index_versions_pointer`: the single active pointer.
    - `rag_index_versions_build_progress`: owner-only; no tenant grant.
  - The existing chunk table is registered in place as version 1. It follows the configuration until another version is activated, and that activation pins it.
  - Where the code lives:
    - `server/rag/vector-store-pgvector-versions.js`: registry, spaces and pointer.
    - `server/rag/vector-store-pgvector-version-lifecycle.js`: build, gate, activate, rollback and retire.
    - `server/rag/vector-store-pgvector-runtime.js`: the shared runtime seam, including `configurePgvectorRuntime`.
    - `server/vector-index.mjs`: the CLI (`npm run vector:index -- status|build|resume|validate|activate|rollback|retire`).
    - `GET /admin/index-versions` in `routes/documents.js`: read-only, behind `admin.status.read`.
  - Every pgvector ingest, delete and clear transaction must call `beginVectorIndexWrite` (vector-store.js) first and use the client it returns. That call takes a shared transaction-scoped advisory lock before any documents-row lock, then reads the write targets in a separate statement (READ COMMITTED snapshot).
  - Registration, the pointer switch and retire lock the pointer row, then the version rows, then take the advisory lock exclusively. Do not reorder these locks and do not merge the lock with the target read.
  - Version tables are rendered by `renderIndexVersionChunkTableDdl` from the 012/013/014 templates (own `vector(N)`, HNSW/GIN, `tenant_isolation`, tenant GRANT, `<table>_sparse_rank`). Never create them by hand.
  - A builder's document transaction (`writeVersionDocument`) takes the document row `FOR SHARE` first (every other writer locks the document row before any chunk row), writes the chunks, then renews the lease (`renewLease`: an UPDATE of the version row `WHERE builder_id = ... AND status = 'building'`, the fence) right before its progress row, so a lost lease rolls the document back and the version row is held only from there to COMMIT. Retire first stops an abortable build in a statement of its own (`stop_build`: status `failed`) before it locks the version row, so no builder waits on a row retire holds. Progress rows commit with the chunks, which is what makes `resume` exact.
  - Searches read the pointer through a TTL cache (`RAG_INDEX_VERSION_POINTER_TTL_MS`). That is safe only because a replaced version keeps receiving writes for `RAG_INDEX_VERSION_DUAL_WRITE_GRACE_MS`, which is never below twice the TTL. Keep that invariant, and never drop a table a fresh pointer can name. Migration 019's `pointer_ttl_ms` on the pointer row bounds the TTL across processes: an instance caches for the smaller of its own TTL and that value (`getInstancePointerTtlMs`), and the lifecycle (grace floor, retire window) assumes the larger (`getLifecyclePointerTtlMs`).
  - A version pinned to another model embeds queries and dual writes in its own space: `embedQuery` and `embedTexts` in `openai.js` accept `{ embeddingSpace }`.
  - `vector:reindex` rewrites the active version in place, one document per transaction (`writeReindexedDocument` in `vector-reindex.mjs`: the index write lock, then the document row FOR UPDATE), and skips a document whose content version or hash changed, or that was deleted, since its chunks were prepared.
  - `createIndexVersion` embeds a probe text in the new space first (`probeEmbeddingSpace`), so a model that cannot answer at the declared width registers nothing. An upload whose embedding fails in a space no search reads fences the versions in that space (`fenceFailedWriteSpace` in `vector-store-pgvector.js`: marked failed) and goes on; a failure in the active version's space fails the upload.
  - The activation gate also fails when any chunk's `metadata.documentVersion` differs from the registry's `content_version` (`staleDocuments`: a replacement that never reached the candidate). `--active-unreadable` (`allowUnreadableActive`) skips the comparisons with an active version whose table is gone and judges the candidate on its own checks.
  - Retire is two steps: one DDL-free transaction under the exclusive lifecycle lock marks the version `retired` (refused for the active version and within two pointer TTLs of its deactivation), then `dropRetiredIndexVersionStorage` drops its table and functions (version 1's table is truncated) in short tries under `lock_timeout` (`RAG_INDEX_VERSION_RETIRE_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` 200, `RAG_INDEX_VERSION_RETIRE_DROP_ATTEMPTS` 25, `RAG_INDEX_VERSION_RETIRE_RETRY_DELAY_MS` 400, jittered), so a waiting DROP never holds readers of the documents table for long. A result with `dropPending: true` is retired already; running retire again finishes the drop.
  - Unreadable sources: a document whose stored PDF cannot be decrypted or read as a file (any TENANT_* coded error: a key destroyed by a standalone `tenant:keys destroy`, a changed byte, bytes that only look like an envelope), or a stored Word, PowerPoint or Excel file the document loader refuses (any DOCUMENT_* code: an `RAG_OFFICE_*` or `RAG_XLSX_MAX_CELLS` limit lowered since its upload), is recorded as `failed` with an `unreadable_source <CODE>:` progress error (build summary `unreadable`) and the build goes on. It keeps its chunks in the active version and gets none in the new one, so the gate's chunk-count comparison holds it back and names it (`unreadable`, `unreadableDocuments`); `allowUnreadableSources` (`--allow-unreadable-sources` on validate/activate/rollback) passes over exactly those documents, which are then not searchable in the new version. Never a default: a builder that cannot see the keystore or the master key reports every encrypted document the same way. `vector:reindex --from documents` skips such a document (`prepareDocumentForReindex`), keeps its chunks and goes on.
  - Tests:
    - Unit suites use `test/pgvector-version-fake-database.mjs`.
    - `test/vector-store-pgvector-versions.integration.test.mjs` provisions its own database (the pointer is global state), spawns the CLI and a crashing builder as child processes with a temp cwd, and is skipped without `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL`.
- Staged async ingestion (`RAG_INGEST_MODE=async`; `rag/ingest-pipeline.js`, `rag/ingest-worker.js`, `rag/ingest-job-store.js`, migrations 017/018).
  - **Stages.** A job runs parse → chunk → embed → index. Each stage's output (`pages`, `chunks`, float32 `embeddings`, and the PDF as `document_file` once parse succeeded) is stored in `rag_ingest_jobs_outputs` (tenant grant and `tenant_isolation` policy, bounded by `RAG_INGEST_STAGE_OUTPUT_MAX_BYTES`) before the next stage starts, so a retry resumes at the failed stage.
  - **Retries and fencing.** Each stage has its own budget and backoff (`RAG_INGEST_<STAGE>_MAX_ATTEMPTS`, `_RETRY_BASE_MS`, `_RETRY_MAX_MS`). Writes stay fenced on job_id + claimed_by + attempt_count, and `stage_attempts` counts the current stage. `advanceStage` is idempotent under that fence: a retry of a call whose COMMIT went through but whose answer was lost finds the job at the next stage with its output stored and reports success.
  - **Dead letter.** A stage out of attempts moves the job to `dead_letter` with the stage and a message-free reason, keeping its bytes and outputs. Clients see it as `status: "failed"` with `deadLetter`. Operators requeue it at that stage with `npm run ingest:jobs -- dead-letter list|requeue` (owner-scoped or `--all`) or the tenant-scoped `GET /admin/ingest-jobs/dead-letter` / `POST /admin/ingest-jobs/:jobId/requeue`. `checks.ingestJobs` in health counts them.
  - **Index stage.** It commits through `commitDocument` in `rag/index.js`, the same path as a synchronous upload, and records the job's success inside the same write transaction (`store.completeInTransaction`). Do not add a separate success write after it.
  - **Embed stage.** It goes through the per-process cross-document batcher in `rag/ingest-embedding-batcher.js` (`RAG_INGEST_EMBED_BATCH_MAX_ITEMS/_MAX_TOKENS/_LINGER_MS`, at most `RAG_LLM_MAX_CONCURRENCY` in flight, through the model-call guard). A batch-wide failure (`isBatchWideEmbeddingError`: only `CIRCUIT_OPEN` and 429, refusals made before the inputs were looked at) fails every job in the batch. Any other failure, an unavailable 5xx included, re-sends each job's texts separately, so a bad input fails only its own job.
  - **When a job is staged.** Only when `ragService.ingestDocument` carries `STAGED_INGEST` (`rag/ingest-stages.js`), which the real one does. A stub's ingest runs as one step and receives `contentSha256`/`deduplicate`/`replace`/`requestedAt`.
  - **Dedup.** Documents record `content_sha256`, `content_version` and `content_updated_at`. `RAG_INGEST_DEDUP` (default on) resolves an upload of bytes the same owner and workspace already stored to that document: sync 200 with `duplicate: true`, async 202 with a job already succeeded and resolved. `npm run archive:ingest` applies the same rule and prints the existing document with `"duplicate": true` (since 2026-11-17; it used to store every file again). It never crosses tenants. The authoritative check runs under a tenant+hash advisory lock taken after the index write lock and before any documents-row lock. Rows stored before migration 018 need `npm run ingest:jobs -- backfill-hashes`.
  - **Replace.** `PUT /documents/:docId` (same validation and scope as uploads; sync 200, async 202 `kind: "replace"`) locks the row FOR UPDATE, keeps docId, owner and uploadedAt, bumps `version`, and swaps the chunks of every write-target index version in one transaction. It is atomic on pgvector only. A replacement whose request is older than the current content is `superseded`. Hybrid search keeps, per document, only the results of the newest content version either route returned (`keepNewestDocumentVersion` in `vector-store.js`), so a replacement committing between the dense and sparse statements never fuses old and new text.
  - **Index builder.** It compares `uploaded_at` and `content_version`, so keep both in its change check.
  - **Tests.** `test/ingest-pipeline.test.mjs`, `test/ingest-pipeline-routes.test.mjs`, `test/ingest-jobs-cli.test.mjs` and `test/ingest-pipeline-postgres.integration.test.mjs`. The integration suite needs `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and is reported as skipped without it.
- The heterogeneous v3 DAG (its `guarded` rollout, admission rules, approval inside the graph, planner and trajectory cases) was removed in ADR 0001 phase 10; see "v3 unified graph removed" above for what is left and `docs/unified-agent-dag-migration.md` for the record. Two things it did have no equivalent on the outer execution plan and would have to be built there if wanted: a Skill that runs only after the document answer passes its evidence check and receives that verified answer, and an approval whose task text comes from the verified answer.
- Query embedding follows the active index version: `embedQueryCached` (`rag/embedding-cache.js`) embeds each query once, in the space `peekServingQueryEmbeddingSpace` / `resolveServingQueryEmbeddingSpace` (`rag/vector-store-pgvector-versions.js`) names (the configured space for local/Qdrant or without a database; otherwise the active pgvector version's, read through the pointer TTL cache the search reads next). One LRU keyed by space and text (`RAG_EMBEDDING_CACHE_MAX`, `RAG_EMBEDDING_CACHE_TTL_MS`, `RAG_EMBEDDING_CACHE_ENABLED`) serves every space, with in-flight dedupe (the shared in-flight call runs detached from every caller's request: no request deadline or signal and no caller's run meter, but under the starting caller's database tenant so the model gateway's quota and usage ledger still count it; flights are shared only within one tenant (the flight key carries the tenant pair or the non-tenant context, the result cache does not), so one tenant's quota refusal never reaches another; each caller waits under its own signal and rejects with its own `RequestCancelledError`; the call is aborted only when its last caller left and is charged once, to the earliest caller still waiting; never run a deduplicated model call inside one caller's context), and every returned vector is tagged with its space (`getQueryVectorEmbeddingSpace`). `searchPgvectorDocuments` uses a vector whose tag matches the active version as is. An untagged vector counts as the configured space's. Only a mismatch (the pointer moved, or a caller's own configured-model vector) re-embeds the text in the active space through the same cache (`embedQueryInSpace`). Do not add back a per-query embedding in the configured space or a second query cache. `buildRetrievalInputs` (`document-rag-execution.js`) starts the planned retrieval queries' embeddings next to the question's (`prefetchQueryEmbeddings`) when retrieval runs in process, the semantic cache is off and the embedding cache is on, so the per-query calls that follow join those flights or hit the cache. On the success path that adds no model call. When the question's own embedding fails, the sub-query embeddings already started still finish (cached and charged to that request), and with `RAG_EMBEDDING_CACHE_MAX` smaller than a request's planned queries a prefetched vector can be evicted before retrieval reads it and is embedded again. `configuration_differs_from_active` stays a warning (never a health error) and carries `settings` from `describeConfigurationChangeFor`: only the variables that differ, including prefix overrides.
- Cross-tenant cache timing. A cache hit that skips a model call or a database read is measurably faster, so a cache shared across tenants and keyed by tenant-chosen input tells a tenant what other tenants recently asked. `RAG_EMBEDDING_CACHE_SCOPE=tenant` (default `shared`, unknown refuses start and fails the embedding) adds the flight's tenant partition (`describeFlightTenant`: the tenant pair or the non-tenant context) to the result-cache key; under `shared` the key is unchanged, and flights, space tags and `describeEmbeddingCache` behave the same under both. `TENANT_REGISTRY_CACHE_SCOPE=tenant` does the same for the registry's workspace lookups (per requesting user). Sweep of the other caches (keep a new one in one of these classes):
  - **Tenant-partitioned already:** the semantic answer cache (`toTenantKey` in `rag/semantic-cache.js`, bypassed without an access scope), claim-judge verdicts in process and Redis (`resolveVerdictPartition` in `self-check/claim-judge.js`), cross-encoder rerank scores (the key starts with the bound tenant or the non-tenant context, `rag/reranker.js`), registry membership lookups (`memberCacheKey`), the document registry's refresh queues (per tenant key), ingest content locks and dedup (owner + workspace + hash), pgvector BM25 statistics (per scope, migration 030), in-flight embedding flights.
  - **Harmless:** the document registry map (a probe needs another tenant's random-UUID docId and learns only that it exists), the per-call, per-`/chat` and per-streamed-attempt claim-check memo (`evidence-context.js`), the query adapter's WeakMaps (keyed by the cached vector object, so they follow the embedding cache's scope), the pgvector pointer snapshot and schema verifications, compiled LangGraph plans (keyed by plan shape), planner models, model and gateway client instances, keyrings, the token replay cache (random ids), RBAC/OIDC/replica config caches, warning-dedupe sets, and the per-tenant counters of limits, quotas, usage and upload sessions. The model-call guard and ingest embedding batcher share capacity across tenants (availability, never content or cached answers).
  - **Out of scope here:** local and Qdrant BM25 statistics span the collection; both stores are documented single-tenant (`single_tenant_vector_store`). There is no query-rewrite, decomposition, prompt or answer-draft cache.
- Index version builds run `RAG_INDEX_VERSION_BUILD_CONCURRENCY` (default 4, CLI `--concurrency` for build and resume) documents at once through `runBuildDocuments` in `rag/vector-store-pgvector-version-lifecycle.js`. They embed through the ingest batcher (`getDefaultEmbeddingBatcher`), so `RAG_LLM_MAX_CONCURRENCY` and slot-first merging apply. Each document is still one transaction in the order above (document row FOR SHARE, chunk rows, the lease renewal on the version row, the progress row), so chunk writes of different documents never queue on the version row; only a progress row written alone (skipped, failed) renews the lease first. Keep that order.
  - After the first error no new document is taken, and the lease is released or the version failed only after the documents in flight have settled.
  - Pages finish (counters, logger, `afterBatch`) in listing order. A test that asserts per-page progress passes `concurrency: 1`.
  - The versions integration crash test accepts any committed subset of the documents in flight; resume must finish exactly the rest.
- The index-switch load scenario takes `--switch-build-concurrency`. With the query cache on, it warms every instance's cache under the new version (phase "new version, cache warming") before measuring "new version active".
- The pgvector sparse route has two scorings. Set them with `RAG_SPARSE_SCORING=ts_rank_cd|bm25`; the default is `bm25`, and an unknown value throws.
  - `ts_rank_cd` is PostgreSQL's cover-density rank. Never call it BM25. Its runtime `sparseBackend` is `postgres_fts_ts_rank_cd`.
  - `bm25` is Okapi BM25 (`rag/vector-store-pgvector-sparse.js`, migrations 029/030). Its `sparseBackend` is `postgres_bm25`.
  - `bm25` uses Lucene's IDF and `RAG_BM25_K1` / `RAG_BM25_B`, the same as the local store's BM25 in `rag/sparse-store.js`. That local store is the reference: over the same chunks and statistics the scores match within 1e-9.
- Migration 030 installs `<chunks>_install_bm25(table, regconfig)`. It runs for the migration-012 table and for every live version table, and `createIndexVersion` calls it for each new version. It creates, per version table:
  - `sparse_length`: a BEFORE row trigger fills it with the tsvector position count. Rows written before 029 keep NULL and are counted at query time. The migration does not backfill them with an UPDATE, because every updated row is indexed again, HNSW included; `npm run vector:sparse-length` fills them in short batches (run it off-peak, like a reindex).
  - `<t>_sparse_scope_log` and `<t>_sparse_term_log` (append-only deltas, no key) and `<t>_sparse_scopes` and `<t>_sparse_terms` (the folded totals): statistics keyed by (owner_user_id, workspace_id), with the `tenant_isolation` policy and no tenant grant (only the owner-run triggers, fold and search function touch them); `checks.rowLevelSecurity` requires the policy on the four tables of every live version.
  - statement-level INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE triggers with transition tables. They keep the statistics exact for every writer, cascades and bulk loads included.
  - `<t>_sparse_fold()` and `<t>_sparse_search(...)`, both SECURITY DEFINER.
- Retire drops these objects after the table (`renderPgvectorBm25DropDdl`).
- Statistics writes: the triggers only append the statement's deltas to the two log tables, so writers never wait on each other for the statistics, then call `<t>_sparse_fold()`, which moves committed log rows into the totals under `pg_try_advisory_xact_lock` (the statement that gets the lock folds and keeps it to commit; every other one skips without waiting). Only the fold writes the totals. A search reads the totals plus the unfolded log under one snapshot. Do not put a shared per-scope counter row back into the triggers: it serialized every writer of a scope, and `test/vector-store-pgvector-bm25.integration.test.mjs` pins that a writer never waits on another open writer of its scope.
- The search function:
  - takes the scopes of the searched documents from the documents table, so one tenant's statistics never reach another tenant;
  - under the tenant role, filters the doc ids with the policy predicate itself, so a direct call with another tenant's ids returns nothing;
  - has one statement for every caller.
- Common-term pruning (`RAG_SPARSE_PRUNE_DF_FRACTION`, default 0.1, either scoring):
  - candidates come through GIN from terms whose document frequency is at most that share of the scope's chunks, and every term then scores them;
  - it runs exhaustively when no query term is common or when the documents hold at most `PGVECTOR_SPARSE_PRUNE_MIN_CHUNKS` (1000) chunks; when the rare terms bring fewer than K candidates, the rest come from the common terms (`pruned_filled`); a query with only common terms is `common_bounded`; each common-term pass scores at most `RAG_SPARSE_COMMON_TERM_CAP` (default 2000) candidates. Each row names its path (`sparseCandidates`).
- `npm run eval:sparse-scoring` measures this route. `eval:qasper-retrieval` measures the local store instead. `bench:pgvector-scale -- --sparse-doc-sets ... --prune-df-fractions ...` measures latency (the report: `--sizes 100k --latest-name latest-pgvector-scale-sparse`; `--sparse-length null` measures rows without `sparse_length`).
- BM25 is the default by the user's decision, not because it measured better:
  - on QASPER dev, hybrid evidence recall minus ts_rank_cd was -0.0012 [-0.0122, +0.0097], not significant either way and short of the pre-declared -0.01 non-inferiority bound, so do not describe BM25 as the better scoring;
  - pruning does cut latency on the synthetic 100k-chunk, 2,000-document benchmark (RLS on, whole table, `evaluation/results/latest-pgvector-scale-sparse.md`): the 11 of 100 mixed queries holding a common term went from p50/p95 135/180 to 39/49 ms under ts_rank_cd and 281/389 to 40/51 ms under BM25, and the whole mixed set's ts_rank_cd p95 from 135 to 73 ms;
  - a query of common terms only is cut in physical order, not by relevance: for one common word the pruned top-10 shares only 0.32 (ts_rank_cd) / 0.29 (BM25) recall@K with the exhaustive one, and QASPER has only 3 (train) + 19 (dev) such questions, so its effect on evidence recall is unmeasured.
- Keep the triggers on whichever scoring is selected, so switching needs no rebuild. They add about 6% per ingested document.
- Semantic answer cache (`server/rag/semantic-cache.js`, `RAG_SEMANTIC_CACHE=on`, default `off`; config block in `rag/config.js`).
  - Seam: a per-process LRU (`RAG_SEMANTIC_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES` 500, `RAG_SEMANTIC_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` 32 MiB of stored response JSON, an answer larger than that is not stored, `RAG_SEMANTIC_CACHE_TTL_MS` 1 h) at the answer seam `executeDocumentRag`. The lookup runs after routing and the query embedding and before retrieval, so it adds no model call. A hit returns the stored document RAG response and skips retrieval and the answer model.
  - Key: the tenant (`accessScope` userId/workspaceId; a caller that passes none bypasses the cache); the exact docId set with each document's content version; the active index version (pgvector version id and pointer generation) plus the query embedding space; the answer prompt fingerprints; the chat and fallback models; route mode and requirement count; the agent retrieval-plan shape with the resolved question removed (follow-up plans are never cached; the cross-language rewrites are not part of the plan); the preference block; and a fingerprint of the `RAG_*`, `OPENAI_*`, `VECTOR_STORE_*`, `QDRANT_*`, `EMBEDDING_*`, `PDF_*` and `DOCLING_*` configuration (`CONFIG_ENV_PREFIXES` in `semantic-cache.js`; secrets and the semantic/embedding cache, observability, `RAG_LLM_*`, `RAG_INGEST_*`, data-directory and shared-state settings excluded; a secret is a name with a whole `KEY(S)` / `TOKEN` / `SECRET(S)` / `PASSWORD(S)` / `CREDENTIAL(S)` part, since a substring match had also left out `RAG_ANSWER_MAX_TOKENS` and `RAG_KEYWORD_WEIGHT` until 2026-11-10). The lookup compares the model's own query vector, so the key takes the query adapter from the configured file as currently loaded (`getQueryAdapterReportFingerprint` in `query-adapter.js`, reloaded on a path, size or mtime change; `unavailable:<code>` for a file that cannot be used): a file retrained under the same path moves lookups to a new key and purges the entries built under the old one (a replacement that keeps both size and mtime, such as `cp -p` or `rsync -a`, is not noticed until restart; the trainer's atomic write always gets a new mtime). An unchanged file that fails to parse is remembered by path, size and mtime and not parsed again.
  - Hit condition: within a key, a hit needs cosine ≥ `RAG_SEMANTIC_CACHE_THRESHOLD` (0.97) AND `compareCacheQuestions` in `semantic-cache-guard.js` (`semantic-cache-guard/v2`): a near-exact match of the normalized question plus a short allowlist (case, spacing, punctuation, contractions, articles and a few filler words, a plural "-s", modals within one class, a few listed synonyms, digits vs number words, "per day"/"daily"); negators and their positions, antonyms, numbers, comparators, dates and time units, entities, modal classes and every other word must match in order, and a question with any non-Latin letter must match exactly apart from case, spacing and punctuation. Do not go back to dropping word classes: v1 dropped modals, prepositions, pronouns and negator positions and let contrasts such as a moved "not" or a role swap through. When a session rewrite made the retrieval question differ from what the user typed, the raw question must pass the guard too, because the answer prompt answers it.
  - Do not weaken the guard to raise the hit rate. nomic-embed-text scores negations, role swaps and one-word swaps in long questions at 0.98–0.997, above some real paraphrases (0.94–0.999).
  - Storage and invalidation: abstentions are never stored. `rag/index.js` calls `invalidateSemanticCacheDocuments` on commit, delete and clear, and a registry read that finds a document deleted or replaced by another instance drops its entries (`onDocumentStoreChange` in `doc-registry.js`). A lookup that sees a new document version, index version or query-adapter fingerprint purges entries built on the old one, and an answer whose document, index version or adapter changed mid-flight is not stored; neither is one whose retrieval reports another adapter (`routes.dense.queryAdapter`) than the key names (`query_adapter_changed`), which covers a retrieval tier that loads another file.
  - Markers: hits are marked in the RAG trace (`semanticCache`), in the OTel event `rag.semantic_cache.hit` (numbers only), and in the RAG response as `semanticCache` on hits only, whose `retrieval` block then says `servedFromCache: true` with every route `executed: false`, so a report cannot count a cached answer as a fresh retrieval. `/chat` `agentObservability` and MCP `formatAskResult` do not carry the marker yet.
  - PostgreSQL: there is deliberately no PostgreSQL copy (it would be a tenant-text table needing RLS, retention and delete-on-delete); keep it in process unless that is built.
  - Evaluation: `npm run eval:semantic-cache` uses `evaluation/semantic-cache-cases.js`, a real model and the synthetic-corpus-5docs documents. It writes ignored `evaluation/results/latest-semantic-cache.*`, sweeps threshold × guard mode offline, and exits 1 on a live contrast hit, a held-out false hit, or a lookup-path verdict that disagrees with the offline one. Keep quality gates and `verify:quality` evidence runs with the cache off unless measuring the cache: reports record `evidence.semanticCache`, and `quality:current` and `release:gate` fail a report run with the cache on (`semantic_cache_enabled`, `eval-evidence-validation.js`).
- Query-side embedding adapter (`server/rag/query-adapter.js`, `RAG_EMBEDDING_QUERY_ADAPTER=<file>`, default off): a d x d matrix W trained by `evaluation/train-query-adapter.py` maps query vectors only, so document vectors are never reindexed; `embedQuery` returns the model's own vector and the retrieval seam (`adaptQueryVectorForSearch`, called from `rag/vector-store.js`) adapts a copy for one search. The file names its embedding space (model, both task prefixes, width). It applies only to the single-document QA route's own retrieval (`single_document_qa`, exactly one docId, no agent retrieval plan) on the hybrid route with RRF fusion, to a vector the embedding model produced in the adapter's space, on a store that can rank by W q while scoring with the model's cosine (local, pgvector); everything else searches unadapted, and an in-scope search that cannot be adapted (another route, fusion or store, a stand-in vector, a space mismatch, a malformed or unreadable file) logs a one-time warning per reason. `checks.queryAdapter` is an error (`QUERY_ADAPTER_INVALID`) for an unreadable or invalid file and a warning for the rest. A query embedded for a pinned index version in another space is left unadapted. The query-embedding cache (`embedding-cache.js`) holds the model's own vectors, so the fingerprint is not part of its key; the fingerprint is stamped as `provenance.queryAdapter` on dense-ranked results and as `routes.dense.queryAdapter` on the retrieval summary. Nothing is added when the adapter is off, so reports and baselines keep their shape. A configured stand-in embedding provider is never adapted unless it declares `allowQueryAdapter: true`. `eval:qasper-retrieval`, `eval:qasper-answers` and `eval:abstention-gate` record the fingerprint in `config.queryAdapter`, and `eval:qasper-retrieval --compare` refuses a report with another one. The trained file defaults to `evaluation/generated/query-adapter/`, which `.dockerignore` keeps out of the image, so a deployment mounts it. Reproduce with `npm run adapter:data` (QASPER train with the app chunker and task prefixes; resumable), `npm run adapter:train` (InfoNCE with same-paper hard negatives and in-batch negatives, lambda*||W-I||^2, grid plus early stopping on 20% held-out train papers, CPU-deterministic), and `npm run eval:query-adapter` (QASPER dev, the hybrid single-document QA route, identity vs adapter, paired CIs; the run fails if dense provenance does not prove which arm ran or a dense `vectorScore` differs between the arms). Measured with nomic-embed-text: hybrid evidence-among-candidates rose from 0.692 to 0.738 (+0.046, CI [0.024, 0.069]), but answer F1 on 200 QASPER dev questions with qwen2.5:7b was flat (0.2576 -> 0.2562, not significant), so it is an optional retrieval setting, not a recommended one; keep it off by default. It moves absolute cosines (held-out within-paper mean 0.59 -> 0.14), so `vectorScore`, and every admission floor keyed on it, stays the model's own cosine; never let an adapted cosine reach an absolute bar. The dense-only route (`combined` scoring takes max(cosine, coverage, weighted)) lost top-3 (-0.032, CI excludes 0) and is never adapted; comparison and multi-document retrieval are not adapted either (unmeasured). Do not describe it as helping dense-only or comparison, and retrain it for any other embedding model or corpus.
- `VECTOR_STORE_PROVIDER=pgvector` is the default: chunks and vectors live in PostgreSQL (`server/rag/vector-store-pgvector.js`, migration `server/db/migrations/012_create_rag_document_chunks.sql`, local instance via `docker-compose.yml`). `local` and `qdrant` remain explicit opt-ins and are single-tenant stores (they scope searches only by the caller's docIds, with no row-level security; Qdrant's BM25 statistics span the whole collection; after the 2026-11-17 tokenizer change a collection holding text outside ASCII and U+4E00-U+9FFF serves misaligned sparse scores until the next upload or delete rewrites its sparse vectors, and pgvector rows written before it keep the old search text and BM25 statistics for such text until `npm run vector:reindex -- --from documents --apply`): `checks.vectorStore` warns (`single_tenant_vector_store`, never an error) when either runs with `API_AUTH_TOKENS`, JWT or OIDC, and Qdrant returns `[]` for an empty or missing docIds list instead of searching the collection. Any other value fails closed at health, ingest and search -- never add a silent fallback. Document RAG runs the dense and sparse routes independently by default (`RAG_HYBRID_ENABLED=true`, `RAG_HYBRID_FUSION=rrf`); rerank stays after fusion, and the sparse route on pgvector is Okapi BM25 over PostgreSQL FTS by default (`RAG_SPARSE_SCORING=bm25`, set by the user after QASPER showed no significant difference; `ts_rank_cd` remains available and must not be described as BM25). Archives written before migration 029 need `npm run vector:sparse-length` to backfill chunk lengths, or BM25 on those rows is 3-4x slower (see `docs/retrieval-tuning.md`). The pgvector ingest path registers the document row and writes its chunks in one transaction (`rag/index.js`); keep it that way. `npm run vector:reindex` (dry run by default, `--apply` to write) backfills pgvector from a local index, Qdrant, or the registry's PDF bytes.
- Retrieval results carry `provenance` (route, raw rank/score, fusion, retrieval queries) and `/chat` responses carry an additive `retrieval` block (provider, whether each route executed, candidate counts). Synthetic evaluation reports record it as `summary.retrieval` and per-case `retrieval`; `quality:current` and the robust gate fail when it disagrees with the manifest or when a route did not actually run. Keep `summary.config` unchanged by these fields: the pinned deterministic baseline's regression profile key is built from `config`.
- Suites that exercise the local JSON index (`test/rag.test.mjs`, `test/app.test.mjs`) pin `VECTOR_STORE_PROVIDER=local` themselves. The real-database suite is `test/vector-store-pgvector.integration.test.mjs`; it runs only when `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set (CI provides a `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` service) and is reported as skipped otherwise, never as passed.
- Startup health checks report OpenAI, auth, vector store, PostgreSQL document/session stores, and long memory. `STARTUP_HEALTH_STRICT=false` allows the server to start while reporting health errors.
- For RAG debugging, `RAG_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED=true` writes JSONL traces under `server/data/rag-observability/` (or to `RAG_OBSERVABILITY_EVENTS_PATH` when set); set `RAG_OBSERVABILITY_INCLUDE_CONTEXT=true` only for local debugging when full chunk text is acceptable.
- Service split (`ARCHIVE_RAG_ROLE`, opt-in). One image and one entry point run as `all` (default: today's monolith), `api`, `agent`, `retrieval`, or `model-gateway`, so retrieval and the model gateway scale on their own (api and agent run one each). The default must stay one process with unchanged behavior: with no role and no service URL, `validateServiceTopology` finds nothing and says nothing, and the monolith health report keeps its 16 checks in the same order (`checks.readReplicas` is added only when `POSTGRES_READ_REPLICA_URLS` is set, `checks.postgresTls` only with `POSTGRES_SSL_ENABLED` on or an invalid `POSTGRES_SSL_MODE`).
  - **Roles:**
    - `api` (`createApp()` in `app.js`): the public edge. It keeps auth, rate limits, CORS, the static frontend, uploads, documents, ingest jobs (and the in-process ingest worker), artifacts, memory, quality, admin status/audit. Every route in `AGENT_EDGE_ROUTES` (`rag/agent-service/edge-router.js`) and `POST /admin/actions/:action` is validated at the edge with the monolith's zod schemas and 2 MB limit, then forwarded to `AGENT_SERVICE_URL`. Role `all` with `AGENT_SERVICE_URL` set behaves the same.
    - `agent` (`createAgentApp()` in `rag/agent-service/app.js`): the same chat and tasks routers over the same `createAppServices`, behind `requireServiceIdentity({ audience: "agent", issuers: AGENT_SERVICE_CALLER_ROLES })` (the policy's `all`, `api`) → `express.json({ verify: verifyServiceRequestBody })` → `handleServiceRequestBodyError` → `bindDatabaseTenant` → `bindServiceTraceContext` → `bindServiceCallDeadline`. It owns `runAgentRag`, tasks, startup recovery and `recoverRunnableTasks`. A forwarded admin action runs only when the signed `adminPermission` claim equals that action's permission (the edge authorized and audited it).
    - `retrieval` (`createRetrievalApp()` in `rag/retrieval-service/app.js`): query embedding, dense + sparse, fusion, rerank, query adapter. The role refuses to start without PostgreSQL, under the standalone profile, or with `VECTOR_STORE_PROVIDER=local` (it would search a stale per-process copy).
    - `model-gateway` (`createModelGatewayApp()` in `rag/model-gateway/app.js`).
    - Split roles start through `startServiceRole` in `rag/agent-service/role-server.js`: topology errors refuse the start, SIGTERM/SIGINT drain within `SERVICE_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_MS`, then `app.locals.stop`, finalizers (including the metrics listener), and `resetPostgresPool`. Roles `all`, `api` and `agent` serve `GET /livez` (`routes/system.js`); the retrieval and model-gateway apps do not, so the image `HEALTHCHECK` probes their `/health` (status code only; the report needs a probe identity, see "Probes and tenant-data caching"). Every role serves `/metrics` on its own port when `METRICS_ENABLED=true` (see "Prometheus metrics").
  - **Topology** (`rag/service-topology.js`): `AGENT_SERVICE_URL`, `RETRIEVAL_SERVICE_URL` and `MODEL_GATEWAY_URL` list replicas. A URL moves that work to the tier in every role except the tier's own (a tier never calls itself), so one environment can be handed to every role. The getters throw on a bad value; never add a silent fallback to in-process work. `hostsServiceTier("agent")` decides whether a process runs agent recovery and background agent work. Role `agent` without `RETRIEVAL_SERVICE_URL` retrieves in process (a warning, not an error). Supported scope: pgvector, one api and one agent; retrieval, the model gateway and inference backends may have replicas. `AGENT_SERVICE_URL` with more than one agent only warns (audit #16: each agent's startup recovery treats every running run as interrupted, another agent's live ones included; agent replicas need run ownership, leases and heartbeats first), and the api keeps per-process state (the saved arXiv suggestions a direct arXiv import is checked against, upload claims judged by PID, per-IP limits). The arXiv selection token is not signed: a confirmation must present the token the server stored with the offer, the recommendation task's internal payload on a task confirmation (which the agent tier runs, reading the shared task store) and the saved suggestion on a direct import (`rag/arxiv-selection-token.js`); any other well-formed token (an offer a newer search or an import replaced, a snapshot lost with its process, an altered one) is 409 "no longer current; search again". An import's papers left become a new offer built from the confirmed token (`updateAfterImport` in `rag/arxiv-recommendation-snapshots.js`), stored on the task, so the task ends the same on either tier; an import the agent ran does not update the api's saved suggestion, which still lists the imported papers (a direct import from it skips them as already indexed). Until 2026-11-20 it was an HMAC with a per-process key, so a suggestion searched on the api failed its confirmation on the agent.
  - **Internal identity** (`rag/service-identity.js`, `rag/service-identity-keys.js`): a short-lived token in `x-archive-service-token`, audience = target tier, issuer = the caller's role (`getServiceIssuer`; a role-`all` process may sign as `ingest-worker` with `INTERNAL_SERVICE_ISSUER=ingest-worker`). `INTERNAL_SERVICE_AUTH` picks the scheme; any other value refuses the start and every sign/verify:
    - `hmac` (default): HS256 with the shared `INTERNAL_SERVICE_KEYS`, first key signs, every key verifies. Rotate in three rolling restarts: append the new key (verify only), move it to the front (it signs), then remove the old one; prepending it in the first round makes restarted tiers sign with a key the others do not know yet. Every tier holds every key, so under `hmac` the issuer is self-asserted.
    - `ed25519`: EdDSA with this process's own `INTERNAL_SERVICE_SIGNING_KEY` (PKCS8 PEM or base64 DER; kid `INTERNAL_SERVICE_SIGNING_KEY_ID`, default a fingerprint of the public key), verified with `INTERNAL_SERVICE_TRUSTED_KEYS` (`issuer:kid:base64spki,...`). A public key speaks only for the issuer it is registered under (`SERVICE_TOKEN_KEY_ISSUER` otherwise), so a compromised tier can sign only as itself. HS256 tokens are refused. The model gateway calls nobody and needs no signing key.
    - `mixed`: signs EdDSA and accepts both, for the rolling move. Order (header of `service-identity-keys.js`): every tier to `mixed`, callees first (model-gateway, retrieval, agent, api), then every tier to `ed25519` in any order, then drop `INTERNAL_SERVICE_KEYS`. Rotate an Ed25519 key in three rolls: append its new public entry everywhere, switch that tier's signing key, remove the old entry after the token lifetime.
    - The algorithm is pinned per key (no HS256/EdDSA confusion); signatures must be canonical 64-byte base64url; `timingSafeEqual`, expiry and clock-skew checks stay.
    - **Call policy:** `SERVICE_CALL_POLICY` in `service-identity.js` is the one table, enforced at the receiver in every mode (also under `hmac`): agent ← `all`, `api`; retrieval ← `agent`, `all` (probe also `api`); model-gateway ← `agent`, `all`, `api`, `ingest-worker`, `retrieval`. `requireServiceIdentity`'s `issuers` can only narrow it, and an unknown audience throws.
    - **Request binding:** the service client signs a fresh token per attempt with `htm` (method), `htu` (SHA-256 of path and query; the query may hold a question) and `bdh` (SHA-256 of a non-empty body). The receiver checks method and target in `requireServiceIdentity` and the body in `express.json({ verify: verifyServiceRequestBody })` + `handleServiceRequestBodyError`; a token with `bdh` is accepted only with `application/json`. Required for EdDSA tokens, checked when present on HS256 ones (`INTERNAL_SERVICE_REQUEST_BINDING=required|optional` overrides). A new internal receiver must mount the verify hook, or only method and target are bound. A proxy that rewrites the internal target or body breaks calls in every mode.
    - **Replay cache** (`rag/service-token-replay.js`): each token id is accepted once until it expires. On by default under `ed25519` and `mixed`, off under `hmac` (`INTERNAL_SERVICE_REPLAY_CACHE`).
