Per process and bounded (`INTERNAL_SERVICE_REPLAY_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES`, default 100000; past it the oldest entries are evicted, which reopens their window); with `RAG_SHARED_STATE=redis` shared across replicas through `SET NX PX`, falling back to the local verdict on a Redis error. Under `hmac` without it, tokens remain bearer tokens for their TTL.
    - **Validation:** `validateServiceTopology` refuses a process that calls a tier without a signing key, a called tier with no trusted key for any caller the policy admits, a signing key the trusted list binds to another issuer or another public key, `mixed` without `INTERNAL_SERVICE_KEYS` on a called tier, an HMAC and an Ed25519 key sharing a kid, and an unknown value of `INTERNAL_SERVICE_REQUEST_BINDING` or `INTERNAL_SERVICE_REPLAY_CACHE` (`listServiceIdentitySettingErrors`; a warning in the monolith). It checks only that at least one legal caller is trusted, not every one. `ingest-worker.mjs` and `chat-mcp.js` do not run it, so their identity errors appear on the first gateway call.
    - **Keys tool:** `node server/service-keys.mjs generate <issuer> [--kid id]`, `compose` (the `export` lines for `compose.services.yml`) and `public <issuer>` (reads `INTERNAL_SERVICE_SIGNING_KEY` from the environment) print to stdout only and never write a file. `describeServiceTopology().internalIdentity` adds mode, issuer and trusted `{issuer, keyId}` under `ed25519`/`mixed`; under `hmac` its shape is unchanged.
    - The tenant comes only from the token's scope, which the edge fills from `requireApiAuth`. `requireServiceIdentity` sets `req.accessScope` in the `requireApiAuth` shape and deletes the token header. A body never names a tenant.
    - The public edge mounts `stripInternalServiceHeadersMiddleware` first, so a public client can never present an `x-archive-service-*` header.
    - Claims carry identifiers only. None of this is mTLS: internal ports must stay off the public network in every mode.
  - **Service client** (`rag/service-client.js`, `getServiceClient(tier)`):
    - It picks the replica with the fewest requests in flight; ties rotate. A connection failure or 502/503/504 marks a replica unhealthy for `INTERNAL_SERVICE_UNHEALTHY_COOLDOWN_MS`.
    - Each replica is tried at most once per call. A connect-phase failure fails over for any method; anything after bytes were sent fails over only when the call is idempotent. Asking is POST only (GET `/chat` was removed 2026-11-17); a GET route that ran the agent would have to be marked non-idempotent in `AGENT_EDGE_ROUTES`, or a failover would run a second agent run.
    - `timeoutMs` bounds the whole call and travels as `x-archive-service-deadline-ms`; `stream()` bounds only the wait for headers unless `deadlineMs` is given. Inside a request with a bound deadline (`rag/request-deadline.js`) remote retrieval and model-gateway calls spend `min(own timeout, remaining)`, send the shrunken header and carry the request signal. One `x-archive-service-request-id` is shared by every attempt. `traceparent` is injected and receivers mount `bindServiceTraceContext`.
    - Failures are `ServiceUnavailableError` (503) or `ServiceTimeoutError` (504) with stable codes; messages never carry a URL, token or body.
  - **Edge to agent:** answers pass through with a header allowlist. `/chat/stream` is relayed at SSE event boundaries; a client that leaves aborts the upstream request, and an upstream break ends the client stream with `error` (`SERVICE_STREAM_INTERRUPTED` or `SERVICE_TIMEOUT`) and `done`. An unreachable agent tier answers 503/504 with `Retry-After: 5` while documents and uploads keep working; an agent tier that refuses the edge's token is 502 `SERVICE_IDENTITY_REJECTED`. The agent tier's own 503/504 passes through with its body and `Retry-After` restored from `retryAfterSeconds`. `AGENT_SERVICE_TIMEOUT_MS` bounds one forwarded answer and one whole stream, and it is also the agent run's deadline: the agent tier mounts `bindServiceCallDeadline` app-wide, so its outbound calls are capped by it and the agent routes cancel the run (`deadline_exceeded`) when it passes. This also caps forwarded admin actions, step retries and recovery actions, which used to keep running after the edge gave up.
  - **Model gateway:**
    - What moved there: the `openai.js` retry, backoff, Retry-After, timeout, empty-completion retry and registry failover, and `model-call-guard.js`. It runs that same code; it does not reimplement it.
    - What it adds: upstream replica pools (`MODEL_GATEWAY_{CHAT,EMBEDDING,RERANK}_UPSTREAMS`, `upstream-pool.js`) with one guard key `${url}|${model}` per replica, so `RAG_LLM_MAX_CONCURRENCY` and the breaker apply per replica; per-workspace quotas (`quotas.js`, fixed windows, off by default, shared through Redis Lua scripts under `RAG_SHARED_STATE=redis` and per gateway process otherwise; system calls are never limited); a per-tenant usage ledger behind `GET /usage`.
    - The tenant comes from the token. Upstream error text is never forwarded; errors are a status and a stable code.
    - Callers: with `MODEL_GATEWAY_URL` set, `completeText`, `completeTextWithMetadata`, `embedTexts`, `embedQuery` and the cross-encoder call go to the gateway (`rag/model-gateway/client.js`), unless a custom provider is configured or the call already runs inside a gateway request. Identity is the active database tenant, or the system identity without one. The caller keeps no retry, failover or guard of its own; only a request no gateway replica received (and, for embeddings and rerank, one lost mid-request) moves to another gateway replica.
    - Task prefixes, the pinned embedding space and the query adapter stay on the caller; the gateway embeds text as given with the caller's explicit model, in batches of 512. Chat routing uses the gateway's own model registry (the body's `model` is ignored), so model settings must match on both sides.
    - Errors seen by callers: 503 `MODEL_GATEWAY_UNAVAILABLE`, 504 `MODEL_GATEWAY_TIMEOUT`, 502 `MODEL_GATEWAY_PROTOCOL_ERROR`. Rerank keeps its own `RAG_CROSS_ENCODER_TIMEOUT_MS` budget and still degrades through `rerankResultsOrKeepOrder`. A gateway-side retry of a stream sends an `attempt` chunk, so `answer_draft_reset` still fires.
    - Metering: the gateway's events (`model_gateway_metered`, one per attempted model, with the tenant) are authoritative. The caller writes one `model_gateway_mirror` event per call with the gateway's metered usage, so run ceilings and `agentObservability` keep working. Totals over a deployment must skip mirrors; `observability-report.js` does (`isModelGatewayMirrorEvent`), so keep that filter when adding another report over LLMOps events.
  - **Retrieval tier:**
    - The seam is `retrieveGlobalContextForQueries`, `retrievePerDocumentContextForQueries` and `searchGlobalContext` in `document-rag-execution.js`; each dispatches to its `*InProcess` function or to `rag/retrieval-service/remote-retrieval.js`. Every chunk read on the document RAG path (QA, comparison, gap-plan searches, `retrieveQaCandidates`) goes through it. A new retrieval must too.
    - In remote mode the calling process never reads a chunk table and never embeds a retrieval query; the only exception is the semantic cache lookup when `RAG_SEMANTIC_CACHE=on` (whose key also reads the `RAG_EMBEDDING_QUERY_ADAPTER` file in the calling process, so mount the same file there). Requests carry query texts, docIds, topK and the query-adapter scope, never vectors, so the retrieval tier embeds in the space its active index serves.
    - Parity bar: remote results are `deepStrictEqual` to in-process ones. The wire codec (`wire.js`) keeps undefined properties, NaN, ±Infinity, -0, Maps and `$` keys; do not replace it with plain JSON.
    - Tenant: the token scope becomes the database tenant (row-level security), and `maskInvisibleDocIds` replaces every docId the registry does not show that scope with a sentinel, so another tenant's document answers like a missing one on every store. Empty `docIds` is a 400, because Qdrant reads `[]` as no filter. Data endpoints refuse system tokens and accept issuers `all` and `agent` only.
    - Bounds: `RETRIEVAL_SERVICE_MAX_*` (400 with a stable code), a 4 MB body (413). A dependency outage is 424 with `dependencyStatus`, so callers do not fail over on an outage every replica shares; the client restores the dependency's 502/503/504 and maps the tier's 401/403/404 to 500. Each answer carries `usage`, which the client charges to the active agent run.
    - Deadline: the tier refuses a request that arrives after its deadline and answers 504 when it runs out. The retrieval tier runs each request inside `runWithServiceCallDeadline` and the agent tier mounts `bindServiceCallDeadline` (`call-deadline.js`, after `requireServiceIdentity`): outbound calls spend at most what is left of the inbound deadline, and in-flight model calls are aborted when it passes; database statements are not cancelled. A caller that disconnects early does not stop the retrieval tier's work before its own deadline.
  - **Never in role `api`:** `runAgentRag`, agent startup recovery, `recoverRunnableTasks`, task execution, admin actions (they are forwarded). Each role reports only its tiers' health checks (`HEALTH_CHECKS_BY_TIER` in `health.js`); split processes add `checks.serviceTopology`, and the edge adds `checks.agentService`, which is a warning and never an error, so `/ready` stays 200 while the agent tier is down. It also resolves each agent URL's host (`countAgentHosts`: at most 250 ms, since `/ready` waits on it, and one lookup per name at a time, since getaddrinfo keeps a libuv thread until it ends): one name with several addresses of one family, as with Compose `--scale agent=2`, is the warning `several_agents_behind_one_name` (one IPv4 plus one IPv6 address is one host; a load balancer in front of several agents is not seen, and one that itself resolves to several addresses warns even with one agent behind it).
  - **Invariants:**
    - A route added to `routes/chat.js` or `routes/tasks.js` must be added to `AGENT_EDGE_ROUTES`, with `idempotent: false` if it is a GET that writes; a test fails otherwise.
    - A new model call goes through `openai.js` or `reranker.js`, so gateway mode covers it. `chat-mcp.js` requires a local `OPENAI_API_KEY` only when the model gateway is off.
    - Nothing that crosses a tier (error bodies, log lines, span attributes, token claims) carries a question, prompt, model output, document text or secret.
    - Every split role shares one PostgreSQL for documents, runs, tasks, audit and ingest jobs; memory-backed stores do not work across tiers, and the topology check does not detect that. The vector store is pgvector: `VECTOR_STORE_PROVIDER=qdrant` only warns in `validateServiceTopology` (roles api, agent and retrieval, and all with an agent or retrieval URL), because Qdrant's sparse vocabulary is built per process and refreshed only by that process's own writes, so a tier that searches scores with numbering another tier's ingest replaced.
    - A new internal receiver mounts `requireServiceIdentity` with an audience from `SERVICE_CALL_POLICY` and `express.json({ verify: verifyServiceRequestBody })` + `handleServiceRequestBodyError`. A new agent route that runs a run goes through `runAgentRequestWithCancellation`, and new long work checks `throwIfRequestCancelled` at a boundary, never in the middle of an effect.
  - **Tests:** `service-topology`, `service-identity`, `service-client`, `service-roles` (edge and agent tier), `service-roles-server` (start-up, shutdown, health per role), `service-roles-tracing` (one trace across the edge hop), `model-gateway`, `model-gateway-units`, `retrieval-service` (parity, masking, bounds, failover, deadline, usage), `retrieval-service-process` (role retrieval with the real app), `retrieval-service-remote-only`, `retrieval-service-tracing`, all under `server/test/*.test.mjs`. `retrieval-service-pgvector.integration.test.mjs` needs `PGVECTOR_TEST_DATABASE_URL` and is reported as skipped without it. Identity: `service-identity-ed25519` (keys, modes, algorithm pinning, issuer binding), `service-identity-binding` (htm/htu/bdh, content type, replay cache; its real-Redis case needs `REDIS_TEST_URL` and is skipped without it), `service-identity-processes` (cross-process gateway hop), `service-identity-split` (api → agent → retrieval → gateway as four processes under `ed25519` with binding required and the replay cache on; retrieval uses the local store there, not PostgreSQL), `service-identity-tool` (`service-keys.mjs`). The older `service-roles`, `service-roles-server`, `retrieval-service` and `model-gateway` suites hand-sign unbound tokens and assume `hmac`; they do not pass under `INTERNAL_SERVICE_AUTH=ed25519`.
- Deadlines, cancellation and dependency outages (`server/rag/request-deadline.js`, `server/rag/dependency-outage.js`; monolith and split alike).
  - **Where the deadline comes from:** `/chat`, `/chat/stream` and `POST /agent-runs/:runId/actions/:action` run under `runAgentRequestWithCancellation`. The deadline is the earlier of the inbound service deadline (`x-archive-service-deadline-ms`, which the edge sets from `AGENT_SERVICE_TIMEOUT_MS`) and arrival + `AGENT_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (default `0` = none; very large values are timed in steps, not cut at the 2^31 ms `setTimeout` limit). `AGENT_CANCEL_ON_DISCONNECT=on` (default off) also cancels when the client disconnects, including before the route bound the request. With neither, the monolith binds nothing and behaves as before; a split agent tier always has the edge's deadline.
  - **What is cancelled:** outbound calls in a bound request spend `min(own timeout, remaining)` and carry the request signal (remote retrieval, model-gateway client post and stream, direct `openai.js` chat/embeddings with abortable backoff and no retry after an abort, `reranker.js`, `chat-mcp.js` web search; a custom provider's `completeText` gets `options.signal`, its `embedTexts`/`embedQuery` do not). The run stops at safe points only: each scheduled execution-plan stage (a node of the outer plan graph; a built-in stage whose Skill was not selected is pruned and has none, the next node's or the drain covers it), `startStep`/`startGraphStep`, each graph node launch, `executeAgentSkill` entry (which rethrows the cancellation instead of returning a failed Skill result), and before `finalizeAgentRun`. A Capability with `approvalPolicy.writesWorkspace === true` runs outside the request store once started: it completes, its step completes, and the run stops at the next safe point; the effect is kept and not replayed by the cancellation (a step retry of the completed `capability_call` step can run it again). Database statements never read the signal. Scheduled tasks run under `runOutsideRequestDeadline`, so they never inherit a request's deadline or signal. An agent task binds its own request cancellation per iteration (`createAgentTaskIterationRunner`, `rag/agent-tasks.js`), fired by the task's cancel or a lost claim: the run ends canceled (`AGENT_CLIENT_CANCELLED`) at its next safe point, the iteration rethrows the task's own abort reason (so a lost claim is still fenced, not failed), and approved deliverables stop before the next one (the one being written finishes).
  - **Terminal states:** through the existing revision-CAS `failRun`: deadline → `failed` with `run.error.code` `AGENT_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`, `reason: deadline_exceeded`, `retryable: true`; client gone → `canceled` (`run_canceled` event with `{error, reason, status}`), `AGENT_CLIENT_CANCELLED`, `retryable: false`; outage → `failed` with `retryable: true`, `dependency` and `causeCode`. Once a `capability_call` step has completed, `failRun` records `retryable: false` instead and the answer drops `Retry-After` and says `retryable: false` (agent-runs.js `hasCommittedCapabilityWrite`): a retry would repeat the write. Other errors keep `{message, name}`, the message through `serializeError` (`lib/error-text.js`; fallback `Agent run failed.`); the deadline, client and outage messages are `expose: true`, so they stay. Startup recovery never lists failed or canceled runs.
  - **Answers:** deadline → 504 `{code: AGENT_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED, reason, retryable: true, agentRunId?}` without Retry-After; client gone → 499. A dependency outage (codes and statuses only: `SERVICE_UNREACHABLE|UNAVAILABLE|TIMEOUT`, `MODEL_GATEWAY_*`, `MODEL_UPSTREAM_*`, `CIRCUIT_OPEN`, the retrieval tier's restored 502/503/504, a direct model 5xx/408 or network error, PostgreSQL 08xxx/57P01-03/53300 or a lost connection; never a 4xx, 429 or protocol error) → 503 `AGENT_DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` (504 `AGENT_DEPENDENCY_TIMEOUT`) with `Retry-After` (the cause's, else 5) and `{code, error (generic), dependency, causeCode?, retryAfterSeconds, retryable: true, agentRunId?}`. SSE ends with `error` (`{...same body, status}`) and `done`, no `result`; a completed run keeps `result` identical to `/chat`. Helpers: `describeAgentRouteError` / `sendAgentRouteError` in `routes/chat.js`.
  - **Behaviour that changed by default:** a failed primary `document_rag` step, a `custom_skills` stage whose every result failed on an outage, a research brief whose every question failed on an outage, or an outage thrown anywhere in the run or in `buildChatResponse` now answers 503/504 and fails the run. It used to fall through to the Web stage and answer 200 with a Web-approval clarification. Ordinary evidence gaps keep their 200 clarification; non-outage errors keep `{ error: message }` and their status. Background tasks with an outage now fail instead of pausing for Web approval. `service-split.e2e` asserts the new 503 for a stopped gateway or retrieval tier.
  - **Circuit breaker:** a model call its own caller cancelled (a `RequestCancelledError`, directly or as a cause) is neither a failure nor a success for `model-call-guard.js`; a cancelled half-open probe gives its turn back (`breaker.abandon()`). Counting deadline aborts as failures let five short deadlines open the circuit for every tenant of the process (measured: 226 of 234 runs answered 503 instead of 504).
  - **Limits:** a writing Capability that crosses the deadline completes and the run then fails as not retryable (above). The retrieval tier does not stop when its caller disconnects. A direct-model connection failure is labelled `dependency: "dependency"`, not `model`. An agent replica answering an outage 503 is still marked unhealthy at the edge for the cooldown.
  - **Tests:** `request-deadline` (store, budgets, timers, disconnect before binding), `agent-cancellation` (safe points, terminal states, the Capability registry's rule that a writing Capability runs outside the request's deadline and signal, outage answers), `request-deadline-split` (real edge and agent apps over HTTP with stand-in retrieval and gateway), all `server/test/*.test.mjs`. Split-mode disconnect cancellation and a real retrieval tier aborting at its deadline are not tested end to end.
